# Changelog

## 0.6.5.2 [Unreleased]

- **Documentation (28 October 2026):** Aligned the roadmap with merged features and pending implementations, corrected obsolete historical links, and preserved existing bugfix registration.
- **Terrain (merged #73):** Seed-compatible landscape revision 3 curves river water footprints, adds riparian moisture and wet banks, and replaces grid-like tree scatter with biome-weighted groves and varied tree silhouettes. Existing saved maps retain their original generation recipes.
- **Workforce CI (27 October 2026):** Preserve three staff at Quarry, Barracks and Prison under automatic rebalancing; guard staffing and stone production recover without general job shuffling.

- **Workforce stability (27 October 2026):** Construction borrows at most one worker per empty site, after idle and existing crews. Job rebalance opens empty posts only; staffing never shuffles workers to top up partially staffed posts.
- **Performance (27 October 2026):** Reused construction-crew and building indexes across staffing passes, removed redundant prisoner scans, and bucketed Virtual Player surplus workers once.

- **CI:** Bound pull-request colony calibration to short and medium scenarios; full-year runs now stop at the first exhausted-food checkpoint instead of continuing after failure.

- **Changed:** Captive and surrender choices now affect reputation and election standing according to resident traits and wartime context, without fixed moral rewards or penalties. Prisoner and settlement-fate controls show neutral options without outcome warnings.

- **Documentation (27 October 2026):** Reconciled the v0.6.5.2 roadmap and architecture notes with merged PRs, separating accepted changes from broader release-hardening work.

- **Changed:** Migrated building-action imports to their owning modules and removed obsolete building-action and renderer-loader TypeScript facades; added a guarded migration script.

- **Fixed:** Removed redundant audio, calendar, combat and food-ration aliases; corrected module owner matching and decision-write scopes.
- **Changed:** Map presets now keep recognizable coastlines, islands, highlands, Nordic fjords and a central oasis; founding clearings preserve neighboring forests. Generation revisions preserve old compact saves.
- **Fixed:** Production buildings retain the biome bonus from their original site after construction removes the underlying forest.
- **Changed:** Terrain now uses a shared biome-edge rule for gameplay tiles and scenery, places vegetation by habitat, and renders connected river masks without duplicate water strokes.
- **Changed:** Ground rendering adds cached horizon shading for stronger 2.5D relief and paints uniquely owned decor after the terrain chunks to avoid seam overlaps.
- **Fixed:** Removed redundant renderer aliases and unused world-generation forwarding exports while preserving compatibility for existing entity creation scripts.
- **Fixed:** Roads snapped to building entrances now align the whole straight drag to the safe road row, including drags starting at the entrance.

- **Fixed (#62, pending CI verification):** Food-producer planning counts all settlers including children; the Virtual Player prioritizes eligible staffed Farm upgrades under measured deficits, protects after-cost reserves, and avoids auto-taming when food flow or valley ecology is unhealthy.
- **Fixed (#62, pending 360-day verification):** Workforce assignment repairs a worker's job assignment when the workplace roster already contains them but the pointer was unset. Added targeted regression tests and seven-day food and verified farm staffing diagnostics.


- **Fixed (#62):** Berry foraging, fed-pet bonuses and grief comfort now change authoritative stamina instead of transient human energy; berry eligibility uses nutrition.
- **Fixed (#62):** Restoring human form after a Moon Howler night rebases the needs clock. Time spent transformed no longer counts as missed human meals, rest or stamina.

- **Fixed (#62):** The Virtual Player now attempts real workforce staffing of completed idle food producers before proposing additional Farms in a measured food shortage. It refuses to multiply unstaffed Farms when nobody can transfer; targeted regressions cover the action and refusal.

- **Fixed (#62):** Preserved elected-leader office during surviving Moon Howler priest rites; added equivalent meal accounting for focused and background settlers, canonical rolling food-flow diagnostics, and a full-year gate that rejects any intermediate producer-colony food-exhaustion checkpoint.

- **Changed (#62):** Auto-play uses three to seven consecutive days of recorded food supply and demand to decide farm expansion. Persistent deficits pause optional recruitment; sparse or stale data is not treated as evidence.

- **Changed (#62):** Removed redundant physiology wrappers and meal aliases, registered sole function owners, and documented the human-needs pipeline and acceptance gates.

- **Fixed:** Physiological stamina now retains leisure, sick-day, graduation, birth recovery, hunting and disaster effects instead of losing legacy energy writes on the next human tick; hospital medicine spends through the one food ledger.

- **Fixed:** Releasing or escaping prisoners who also guard their Prison retains the staff roster and job link. Long-run colony tests now share the live Virtual Player action path so health checks use sustained food production.

- **Changed:** Overnight workers schedule daytime sleep after their shift; saved recovery intent lasts until genuine residential sleep restores rest. Status reads no longer mutate break state.

- **Changed:** Off-duty settlers now take actual stamina recovery breaks, retain break intent across saves and resume activity after recovery.

- **Changed:** Small building settings copy only the changed building; optimistic assignments reuse their isolated display world. Worker ticks compare saved fingerprints and omit unchanged histories, and save export avoids an extra world copy.
- **Added:** Rollback snapshot timing in the performance profiler, preserving the full recovery contract.
- **Fixed:** Looping construction previews now account for earlier placed footprints and real material costs; roads snapped to building entrances no longer overlap foundations.

- **Changed:** Consolidated strip rotation helpers and documented construction, navigation, and function ownership without introducing another implementation path.

- **Fixed:** Repeated wall-to-gate construction previews no longer alter lifetime resource-gathering statistics before placement.

- **Fixed:** Virtual player postpones road construction during food shortages and budgets multi-leg road projects across the full stroke instead of reusing the starting funds for each leg.

- **Added:** Completed wall gates can be opened or closed from their building panel; the saved state controls wall collision and invalidates route grids. Legacy gates remain open.

- **Fixed:** Construction previews now show affordable pieces, net material costs, current stores and shortages. Blocked wall recoveries cannot teleport through obstacles.

- **Fixed:** Commute eligibility no longer grants a whole-route speed bonus from a short road crossing; road drags can snap to the front approach of suitable buildings.

- **Fixed:** Minor pointer drift no longer creates perpendicular road, wall or gate segments or extra construction costs; genuine freehand bends remain connected.

- **Fixed:** Walkers consider faster road routes on short trips and for savings below five percent; bounded searches and route caching remain in use.

- **Fixed:** The annual integration check counts only newly added chronicle entries instead of rescanning the entire history on every tick; simulation coverage and timeout remain unchanged.

- **Fixed:** Save inspection and simulation tooling compile again after restoring a missing building type import and removing an unused assertion helper.

- **Fixed:** Junctions use actual infrastructure footprints, preserving legacy positions and excluding disconnected nearby or distant strips.

- **Fixed:** Roads accept passable rocky ground while rejecting water and reserved obstacles. Only bridges open blocked terrain; navigation refreshes when infrastructure dimensions or road or bridge type changes. Sprite provenance is registered.

- **Changed:** Roads and walls can now be painted freehand with a single drag across multiple bends; a continuous preview tracks the stroke, and the release sends one bounded placement command.

- **Fixed:** CC0 pavement aligns across joints using a stable texture phase and scale. Masonry repeats upright along either wall axis, gates stay upright, and merged demolition uses the authoritative batch cleanup owner.

- **Planned:** Added the independent nutrition, rest and stamina overhaul with measured food-economy, save and simulation acceptance gates; linked the existing roads, walls and navigation work to its tracking issue.

- **Fixed:** Failed worker ticks now roll back nested human and Moon-form needs exactly. Foreign captives retain their legacy exhaustion death while player settlers require prolonged starvation; attendance reasons use nutrition instead of combat energy.

- **Fixed:** Worker render sidecars and reconstructed human views retain detached nutrition, rest and stamina values instead of silently omitting new physiology from worker-facing inspectors.

- **Tested:** Deterministic short- and medium-term physiological budgets cover regular and night shifts, idle food demand, delayed starvation and snapshot equivalence. Full-colony balance remains a separate merge gate.

- **Changed:** Work output and workshop estimates apply a bounded physiological impairment alongside roster fatigue without stacking two full fatigue penalties. Attendance hours remain authoritative.

- **Changed:** Recovery sleep can be pursued at home off-shift when critically sleep-deprived; a bounded persisted sleep plan prevents tick-to-tick priority jitter. Prisoners can rest at night in their actual cell.

- **Changed:** Settlers advance physiology once per tick before camera throttling, eat against nutrition thresholds with ledger-backed food debit, and face delayed starvation rather than zero-energy death.

- **Changed:** Player-human nutrition, rest and stamina now persist separately, including Moon-form recovery; the settler inspector displays all three (#61).

- **Fixed:** Raid surrender decisions and final announcements retain midpoint casualties after population cleanup and reload. Recovery retains cumulative repair and food goals and its full seven-day protection window.
- **Fixed:** Captive deaths close custody history without creating village graves; confirmed executions retain their distinct outcome.
- **Changed:** Mass raid destruction reconciles housing, staffing and storage once per batch with bounded effects. Warfare panels cache troop, captive and repair lookups between world updates.

- **Tested:** Added a browser check for cancelling and confirming settlement razing, then verifying permanent removal and cleared decisions in the exported save.

- **Fixed:** Raid reports retain a fallen starting leader after population cleanup, and removing a prison resolves enemy captives as living escapees instead of leaving inaccessible custody.

- **Changed:** Incoming defenses and outgoing battles advance hourly over six game hours; saved milestones prevent repeated casualties, and truce or razing cleanup releases deployed soldiers.
- **Added:** Defeated villages enter repair and food recovery. Explicit razing intent, failed last resistance and devastated shelter can end a settlement; final reports remain exportable and cannot resume simulation.
- **Added:** Real adult enemy fighters can be held in existing prison cells, released, voluntarily recruited or executed after confirmation. Abstract world-map populations never become fabricated captives.
- **Fixed:** Warfare outcomes, graves, custody, diplomatic memory and leadership awards survive saves and worker rollback; repeated trivial victories no longer duplicate election merit or mercy rewards.

- **Fixed:** World-map settlements now appear from day 10 at 30-day intervals, up to six friends and rivals combined. No rival buildings or settlers enter the village map; existing saved settlements and diplomacy are preserved.

- **Fixed:** Indoor workers can leave for secret meetings without being pinned at work; the browser smoke now requires an observed living patient inside a completed hospital.

- **Fixed:** Settlers disappear while inside completed workplaces, homes, schools, hospitals, prisons and indoor venues, including selected occupants and hotel guests; travel, construction and outdoor work remain visible.

- **Fixed:** Long road routes preserve corners while coalescing straight waypoints; fast commuters clamp their steps before applying the road multiplier to prevent waypoint oscillation.

- **Changed:** Road runs and intersections now use Drummyfish's CC0 cobblestone tile; walls and gates use matching original top-down PNG art, vendored locally for offline rendering with attribution and fallback assets.

- **Fixed:** Empty road and demolition previews no longer crash render frames; rejected road detours reuse negative cache results. Snapshot build-grid validation now shares perpendicular strip crossing rules with authoritative placement.

- **Fixed:** Manor entitlement includes the leader, partner and dependent minors; unhoused adult descendants remain independent across housing reconciliation, worker transport and saved games.
- **Changed:** Divorce custody now selects one parent consistently for shared minor children, and relocating ex-partners prefer the farthest suitable home while preserving existing remarriage restrictions.

- **Changed:** Roads use time-aware routes and on-tile speed bonuses; unreachable journeys stop at barriers. L-shaped drags deduplicate sections and join perpendicular junctions, with aligned wall-to-gate replacement, wall art, and Alt-drag batch demolition.

- **Fixed:** Oversized blended households split by each minor's actual custodian, and bounded movement pathfinding stays directed even when a distant road discounts commute costs.
- **Refactored:** Rival diplomacy and strategic settlement handling now use dedicated owners; the obsolete frontier autospawn is removed.

- **Changed:** Random world events and the frontier milestone no longer create rival settlements on the player's map; existing off-map rival diplomacy remains unchanged.

- **Fixed:** The targeted 90-day housing gate uses the game's residence selectors and distinguishes independent homeless adults from custodians of dependent minors, while checking entire households and capacity.

- **Fixed:** Imprisoned blacksmiths keep reserved jobs but no longer contribute to operational production bonuses; regression coverage added.

- **Fixed:** The 90-day colony gate now checks every home's occupancy against its bed limit and forbids homelessness while beds remain free, rather than requiring zero homelessness after population outgrows fixed starter housing.

- **Fixed:** Release of a surviving parent restores temporary child custody even when the other biological parent died during imprisonment.

- **Changed:** Replaced the duplicate 100-day endurance simulation with an independent restocked scenario (seed 67890); the producers comparison retains seed 12345.

- **Fixed:** Adoption now reports success only after household relocation succeeds; failed placements leave custody and surnames unchanged.

- **Fixed:** Adoption capacity now counts jailed residents' reserved beds without making them eligible to move; regression coverage prevents overcrowding.

- **Fixed:** Manual staffing now counts imprisoned workers' reserved posts, preventing overstaffing after release; added a regression test.

- **Docs:** Housing architecture now diagrams move validation, refusal and rollback and provides eight worked relocation examples.

- **Changed:** PR40 housing endurance comparison now runs 100 days instead of 600 to reduce CI duration; the baseline and optimized scenarios retain the same seed and checks.

- **Changed:** Leader's House has 20 fixed beds (was 12), no upgrades, and one linked annex unlocked above 20 occupants (40 beds combined). The leader stays in the original; an orphan annex cannot replace it.

- **Changed:** Load, immigration and player residence actions now supply the mandatory leader-house phase to the shared housing ledger, while immediate leadership events still reconcile through the same housing move rules.

- **Fixed:** Capacity rebalancing counts imprisoned settlers' reserved beds before selecting movable resident groups, preventing overcrowded houses from retaining too many free residents.

- **Added:** Regression coverage for the manor annex and orphan linkage, prisoner-bed rebalance, and fixed 20-slot upgrade refusal; the housing architecture document now describes these policies.

- **Fixed:** A new leader's household takes the manor as one unit, so the outgoing household leaves together and a minor child is never left behind while their parents are moved out.

- **Fixed:** A child whose parents are both serving a sentence is now placed with a guardian instead of being left unplaced, and custody returns to the parent once the sentence ends.

- **Fixed:** The simulation check no longer reports a jailed settler's reserved job post as a missing worker while the workplace roster omits them.

- **Fixed:** A jail sentence no longer costs a member of the leader's household their manor bed: the house they are away from is theirs until release, exactly as for any other home.

- **Fixed:** An adult child no longer trades the family home for a house whose beds a jailed settler still reserves, so the custody move cannot fill a home past the beds a prison sentence already holds.

- **Fixed:** Relieving an overcrowded home no longer rehouses a jailed settler, so a prisoner keeps the bed reserved for their return while the family they share the home with carries the move.

- **Fixed:** The Leader's House again seats the leader's own dependent children when they share a house with a grown child's family, while that grown child's partner and children keep their own home.


- **Changed:** The Leader's House hold now runs as the pipeline's first phase: household move-ins and manor evictions pass the same whole-household check and ledger as every other housing decision, while the manor retains its mandatory household assignment regardless of its fixed capacity.

- **Added:** A housing architecture note explains the single ordered decision pipeline: which phase owns which question, how a run's ledger protects a household a mandatory phase settled, and how to add a rule without a second scheduler.

- **Changed:** Housing decisions now run as one ordered process — repairs, guardianship, seating and capacity before the optional adult-child and commute moves — so a household a mandatory step settled is no longer moved again by a later pass, and the whole household moves or none of it does.

- **Changed:** Every household relocation now passes one whole-household check before anyone moves: overcrowding rehousing, sibling guardianship and couple formation no longer move residents one at a time, so a refused destination leaves the family where it was.

- **Fixed:** An overcrowded home only rehouses a settler into a home the household can actually occupy, and unhouses the surplus instead of staying over its beds when the only free homes are a manor or unfinished site.

- **Fixed:** A jailed settler leaves the construction crew they were on, so a building site no longer counts a prisoner as a builder, while their home and job post stay reserved.

- **Fixed:** A jailed settler is no longer moved to another workplace or credited with worked hours and output while serving their sentence, so a reserved post stays theirs and pays nothing.

- **Fixed:** A married adult child of the leader who already holds a home keeps their partner and dependent children with them, instead of the manor taking the children from their parents.

- **Changed:** A prison sentence holds the settler's home and job: they stay off shift while jailed and keep their post, so the colony no longer refills it, and their bed stays reserved.

- **Fixed:** A household is no longer moved to a nearer home when the household total improves but one member's own daily trip lengthens, and a colony makes at most eight moves a day.

- **Fixed:** A save carrying an identity at the very top of the range is refused, and an accepted save always floors its identity counters to values the next save still accepts, so one spawn can no longer make a colony unsavable.

- **Fixed:** A settler born on January 1 keeps that birthday when a save is opened, instead of having the date rebuilt from a whole-year age because the day and month are both zero.

- **Fixed:** The Leader's House keeps its fixed beds in a save written while it still carried an upgrade level, rather than reporting two extra beds for every stored level.

- **Fixed:** The auto-play ladder staffs an empty Quarry by moving a hand from an over-crewed producer instead of only naming the shortage, so a colony short of stone recovers on its own.

- **Fixed:** Housing capacity now counts every resident, including settlers the pass may not move, and the Leader's House protects its household only while a manor stands, so a house can no longer be filled past its beds.

- **Changed:** A couple no longer shares a house with another, unrelated couple: with no home of its own free it moves in with its own family, and single settlers keep to singles' houses.

- **Fixed:** Two settlers who pair up are no longer left without a bed when no home has room for both of them, and a child with no bed left stays with the parent who still has one.

- **Fixed:** Housing changes now use one residence writer, keeping assignment counts, household traits and resident rosters consistent across marriage, divorce, births, deaths, demolition, leadership and Moon Howler transitions.

- **Fixed:** Growing families first reclaim space from unrelated residents, then seek a larger home together. During shortages children stay with their guardian, a family warning remains visible, and the household reunites when space opens.

- **Fixed:** Orphans consider grandparents on both sides, including grandfathers, and siblings receive a shared guardian and home whenever possible. Historical family records retain custody links after parents leave the living population.

- **Fixed:** The Leader's House admits the leader's married children and their spouses, but an adult child settled in a home of their own is no longer moved out of it into the manor. Grandchild eligibility and succession protection are unchanged.

- **Fixed:** An orphan's guardian is never taken from the Leader's House, so placing a child can no longer move the leader's family out of the manor.

- **Changed:** Saves missing identity counters are explicitly refused because retired identities cannot safely be inferred from surviving records. Intact saves retain their counters and existing identities.

- **Changed:** Daily commute relocation skips work without vacant homes and indexes candidate homes, destinations and roads. Benchmarked work-and-school outcomes remain identical while large-colony sweep time falls substantially.

- **Fixed:** Opening a save keeps each settler's own birth date instead of restamping it from a whole-year age, so a citizen's birthday no longer moves every time the colony is loaded.

- **Fixed:** A search for the cheapest walk now scales its estimate by the cheaper road step, so a paved detour that is longer on the map but quicker to walk is priced as the better route instead of the plainer ground line.

- **Fixed:** A settler who graduates releases the school seat they held as a pupil, so neither the school roster nor a housing move treats an adult as a daily walker to their old school.

- **Fixed:** A household no longer trades its home for one that shortens the parents' commute while leaving a child more than two hours from school; a move now checks every pupil's school reach first.

- **Changed:** A household's trip to work and school is now measured along the route settlers really walk — around rivers, mountains and detours — instead of the straight line through them; each route is priced once and a daily sweep stops searching once its budget is spent.

- **Fixed:** The auto-play status line names an unmanned producer before any other shortage, so an empty Quarry reads as a building waiting for a hand instead of its neighbour's low shelf.

- **Added:** The daily council lists every village building in one table with its building type number and its unique building ID, sorted by type and number, so two buildings of the same kind are told apart at a glance.

- **Changed:** A school day is now earned in the classroom: a pupil credits school days only while a teacher stands at the school, and each teacher seats ten pupils, so a fully staffed school holds twenty.

- **Fixed:** The Leader's House can no longer be upgraded: its beds stay at 12, as the original design has it, and the building inspector no longer offers the button. A level an older save already carries still counts.

- **Added:** A household may trade its home for a vacant one that meaningfully shortens the family's commute and school trip, checked once a day with a margin that stops near-equal homes swapping them. The Leader's House stays exempt.

- **Fixed:** Settlers no longer walk on open water at the spawn: the founding camp is placed on buildable ground instead of the map centre, a camp clearing no longer fills sea with walkable land, and the opening view starts at the camp.

- **Fixed:** A colony loaded from a save with no identity counters no longer re-issues the number of a settler the family tree still remembers, or of a camp structure a rival settlement still names. An id so high that no next number exists is refused rather than loaded.

- **Fixed:** The identity numbers a save may carry now leave two numbers of headroom below the ceiling, so the allocator a saved id floors to can always hand out one more usable number; an allocator saved that high is refused.

- **Added:** The building inspector now labels every structure with its building type number and its unique building ID, so two buildings of the same kind are told apart.

- **Changed:** Workplace assignments use workerBuildingId throughout simulation, UI, diagnostics and worker transfers. Older saves translate the previous field once on load, including saved Moon Howler forms, without changing building IDs.

- **Fixed:** Legacy rival camp buildings left over from an older save — a house, farm or well that no rival settlement still listed — are removed when that save loads, and the daily rival pulse now retires any of them it finds. They no longer linger on the map as a camp you cannot interact with.

- **Fixed:** A cursed Moon Howler in the leader's household no longer unseats the family from the Leader's House every full moon; the nightly relocation and move-in chronicle pair is gone.

- **Added:** Base44 sandbox development environment — a Linux compose setup that serves the game's development server from the mounted clone on port 3000, with a lockfile-synced dependency install and a service healthcheck.

- **Fixed:** Raid victory decisions now reach the simulation worker, restoring mercy, tribute and raze choices after a defeated settlement surrenders.
- **Fixed:** Residents reserve a free hotel bed before walking there. Reservations count toward capacity, survive saving, and release when the stay ends, preventing overbooking by residents still en route.

- **Added:** A short runbook beside the repository facts now describes the sandbox preview: one Vite dev service runs from the mounted clone on port 3000, so edits stay live without rebuilding an image.

- **Changed:** The strategic map is drawn as an antique survey sheet — parchment, engraved relief, compass rose and scale rule — and the raid, war and recall controls now sit once, in the rival roster beneath it.
- **Fixed:** Auto-play no longer repeatedly issues the persistent Auto-fill command for an empty producer; normal workforce assignment retains ownership.

- **Fixed:** Short dialogue bubbles remain readable for at least four simulation ticks after the new clock pacing.

- **Changed:** Game hours now contain 12 simulation ticks, settlers walk faster, and commute reach uses building centres. Existing 72-tick-day saves retain their calendar dates on load.

- **Changed:**  Immigrant children arrive with 20 credited school days per completed school-age year; 
- **Changed:** Tier 3 graduates gain +20 to skills andeligible for grandmaster (80+) apprenticeships training place opens.
= **Changed** Schools hold 20 pupils.
- **Fixed:** Education now awards all three tiers at 120, 135 and 150 credited school days; Tier 1 was previously unreachable.
- **Fixed:** School runs 09:00–15:00 on weekdays, with a three-hour classroom attendance threshold independent of work shifts. Enrollment stays with each child and transfers to a substantially 
- **Fixed**  closer school on a daily review; school commutes start at the real home standing point. Changed the count days needed for graduation
- **Fixed:** Children leave for weekday school up to two hours before class, based on distance from home. Pupils walk into the classroom at commute pace; travel overrides home and leisure routines, and attendance counts only during class.
- **Fixed:** Removed an unused staffing import left behind by the virtual-player auto-fill repair, restoring compilation.
- **Fixed:** Rival founding and growth no longer create enemy houses, farms or resident settlers inside the village. Legacy camp objects retire on the next rival update; strategic population, diplomacy and raids remain. Village camp markers and hit tests now cover visitors only.
- **Fixed:** Camp structures left on the map by older saves are swept on the next rival update, even when no rival record still lists them; no enemy building stands in the village and whoever lived or worked there is released.
- **Added:** Full-year diagnostics report the first invariant failure, watched settlers' moves, confirmed deaths and unconfirmed removals, actual versus assigned residents, and recent combat events. Checkpoint details print before assertions; debug output can be disabled.
- **Fixed:** Company-following movement regression clones one isolated terrain-free world across all eight comparisons, avoiding repeated world generation and load-sensitive timeouts without changing its assertions.
- **Fixed:** Ground Reinforcement catalogue artwork and its asset registration now match the shipped filename casing, restoring the preview on case-sensitive hosts.
- **Fixed:** Regression fixtures now create adults with consistent calendar ages, provide decoration-free terrain for pathfinding, and assert that a wronged parent keeps the family home with their child after a caught-affair divorce.
- **Fixed:** camera zoom-out now reaches the overview and LOD presets (minimum 0.2); rabbits and deer replenish in bounded 16-animal batches instead of large single-tick bursts; a reverting sitting Moon Howler leader regains their Leader House entitlement immediately even at generic capacity.
- **Fixed:** Departing migration deer clear hunter targets and active tick indexes. Moon Howler curses exclude all foreign factions, including trade caravans. Priests return to the church's southern entrance.
- **Fixed:** Ground chunks refresh after terrain edits. Construction cells inspect every subcell; starting clearings rebake terrain grids, and untouched boulder collisions survive rebakes.
- **Fixed:** continuous terrain relief no longer jumps at 50% elevation; river incision depth scales with channel width rather than saturating, and river-distance classification samples the dedicated water-resolution lattice.
- **Fixed:** wildlife viewport culling respects foot-anchored sprite bounds; build-zone blocker and snap markers follow 2.5D terrain rise; spousal connection markers remain visible while married couples are expecting.
- **Improved:** Name-width caching avoids redundant zoom keys. Highlighted visitor and rival camps pulse every frame while static markers remain cached.
- **Fixed:** step validation and straight-line obstruction now seed elevation from the origin tile, preventing first-boundary cliff traversal; A* waypoint smoothing checks diagonal side cells so wall corners cannot be cut. Added regression tests for steep first steps and corner obstructions.
- **Fixed:** Strip-built wall corners retain all four orientations. Demolition refreshes storage and housing capacities, and construction effects use the building centre.
- **Fixed:** manually unassigning a workplace employee switches the workplace to manual staffing, preventing instant auto-refill. Bulk Fill Workers now emits one consolidated notification and floating label rather than a notification for each worker.
- **Fixed:** newborns now receive their actual colony-day birth date at creation and are immediately registered in their residence's occupants list; rival houses no longer inflate player open beds, villas appear in housing counts, and trade caravan humans count as transient visitors.
- **Fixed:** affair encounters pass the entity index when recording secret tryst locations; caught-cheating divorces prioritize the wronged spouse for ordinary housing while preserving a sitting leader's manor; home standing slots use the canonical building-centre coordinates.
- **Improved:** entity-type cache now stores rebuilt buckets on a miss, the schooling maturation helper is exported for integration, and regression tests cover building geometry and transient populations.
- **Fixed:** Corrected a case-sensitive entity-factory import for Linux builds. School routing and attendance use building-centre coordinates; added geometry regression tests.
- **Fixed:** Corrected housing constants and adult thresholds. Married adult grandchildren leave the manor; orphan custody searches include living parents excluded from housing, and cursed settlers retain their beds.
- **Fixed:** Entity-layer camera anchoring preserves the full cache margin in every pan direction, avoiding unnecessary rebakes on small movements.
- **Fixed:** adopted adult children qualify for move-out when living with an adoptive parent; separated parents can no longer claim the same child in competing housing units; married adult leader descendants leave the manor household rather than repeatedly moving between homes.
- **Visuals:** Ground Reinforcement uses existing soil artwork in the Build catalogue and persists terrain overrides without placing a blocking building.
- **Changed:** shallow water is now walkable for settlers and visitors; deep water and rivers remain impassable. Existing wall gates retain their normal passable behavior.
- **Added:** Ground Reinforcement under Build → Resources. Spend 30 stone and 8 gold to prepare a 30 × 30 pixel patch of mountain, snow, shallow water or riverbank terrain. Changes persist and refresh terrain grids; active rivers and deep water remain protected.
- **Changed:** outgoing raid marches take at least two in-game days to reach their destination, with visible strategic-map army progress. Food provisions now scale with deployed soldiers and both legs of the journey.
- **Improved:** outgoing battles provide staged, text-only commentary based on simulation events. Named soldier deaths are recorded during combat using the existing death and Graveyard system, retained in final battle reports, and guarded against duplicate resolution.
- **Balanced:** outgoing raid casualties now account for enemy strength and population. Overwhelming superiority can result in no village casualties instead of imposing deaths based on army size alone.
- **Improved:** razing a surrendered rival affects nearby settlements' persistent diplomacy and response readiness; faraway rivals do not automatically react. Confirmed decisions apply only once.
- **Added:** regression coverage for terrain reinforcement, raid marching provisions, live commentary, proportional casualties and razing reactions.

- **Changed:** launching an outgoing raid mobilizes every available adult Soldier into a persistent roster of soldier IDs, with a saved deployment ID that excludes them from home militia strength and barracks defense; deployed soldiers suspend normal work and are shown as away with the war-band.
- **Added:** persistent outgoing-raid marching, engagement and returning phases. Battle or tribute decisions only unlock after the simulated march reaches the target. The original soldier roster, not newly assigned soldiers, supplies combat experience, fatalities and survivors.
- **Added:** Recall Army reverses a deployed force from its current travel position. Survivors return over the remaining distance and cannot defend home until arrival; provisions are not refunded.
- **Fixed:** launching a counter-raid no longer prematurely cancels an enemy incoming attack; the danger remains until the battle outcome is resolved. Active peace treaties prevent new war declarations.
- **Improved:** clickable rival markers, current troop counts and army recall controls on the strategic world map. Returning markers use the saved recall position rather than jumping to the rival camp.
- **Added:** targeted regression tests for whole-army mobilization, premature-engagement blocking, recall transit, battle arrival, returning forces and peace-treaty restrictions. Full build and gameplay verification remain pending.

- **Added:** a strategic Valley World Map inside Frontier showing the player village and rival settlements at their actual world coordinates, with a legend, relationship status, population, and clickable settlement focus. The map remains available before rivals appear.
- **Added:** persistent Declare War actions on the strategic map, using the existing hostility command and reputation penalty. War status is stored on the rival settlement; repeated declarations are blocked and Mercy ends the war with the existing 30-day truce.
- **Added:** Strategic map markers show outgoing war-bands and incoming raiders advancing along routes, labelled by side and raid progress. These are travel indicators, not simulated tactical combat.
- **Added:** strategic-map Raid actions showing current march provisions and respecting existing armament, population, truce and pending-raid eligibility rules. Regression test covers persistent declaration and peace resolution.

- **Warfare roadmap, partial implementation:** The latest 40 resolved raids retain unique battle reports with sides, outcomes, named casualties, other enemy losses, damaged buildings, supplies lost, commander, and merit. Reports survive reloads.
- **Warfare UI:** Frontier shows five recent battle reports with distinctly labelled village and enemy casualties, supplies lost, structural damage, and recovery guidance. Reports persist through worker updates and saves.
- **Leadership warfare merit:** each recorded battle adjusts only its original leader's capped election merit. Serious successful defenses award up to +60, costly defenses +20, and offensive victories +15 to +30 based on risk. Major defeats can reduce merit; trivial attacks give no victory bonus.
- **Rival diplomacy:** only other settlements within 750 map pixels of an explicit mercy or raze decision update their relationship by one step; distant rivals remain unchanged. Pending-victory consumption prevents reopening the choice to duplicate these effects.
- **Regression coverage:** tests for one-time combat reports, battle merit, and nearby settlements reacting to mercy or razing.
- **Not yet implemented from the full warfare roadmap:** Live phased battles and casualties, prisoners, breaches, devastation, razing, settlement outcomes, refugee handling, and complete end-to-end verification remain release blockers.

- **Refined:** Elections nominate twelve highest-merit eligible villagers, including an eligible incumbent. The top three by support become finalists. Adults cast seeded, energy-dependent ballots instead of mandatory votes.
- **Refined:** promise selection compares proportional gaps to its targets rather than mixing raw food, construction and reputation units; public-health and trust pledges can now compete meaningfully with food and defense. Completed promise windows no longer reappear through legacy flags.
- **Added:** regression coverage for election-specific ballot identity, stability of recorded ballots after candidate skills change, and independently judged promises for two elections in the same year.

- **Changed (P0):** Elections now span 24 game hours: eight gathering, ten discussion, six tension. Each has a persistent ID and seeded vote tally. If a finalist is disqualified, another recorded finalist replaces them. Results survive reloads.
- **Changed (P0):** Campaign promises retain their original leader and 180-day deadline across later elections. Fulfilled pledges award +12 merit each; broken ones cost 16, with total bonus capped from -60 to +60. Legacy promises retain fallback evaluation.
- **Changed (P1):** accumulated professional trade skills now contribute a maximum of 60 election merit instead of 100; age experience is worth 0.5 merit per year after adulthood, up to 50 points. Battle achievements, family prestige and promise fulfillment remain independent scores.
- **Changed (P1):** Voter support now accounts for shared jobs, food security, and defensive achievements alongside friendship, feuds, and seeded candidate variation. Candidate attributes are indexed once per election for performance.
- **Added (P1):** the Village Leadership window shows recorded candidate ballot totals, their vote shares, voter turnout and a graphical results breakdown for the active election and latest completed election.
- **Added (P2):** campaign promises can also demand stronger public reputation and a completed Hospital, alongside food, fortifications and forge work. Promise success or failure now leaves a persistent positive or negative mark on the individual leader's election record.
- **Added:** election regression checks for the rebalanced merit skill cap and saved promise merit influence.

- **Changed:** working at the Town Hall no longer grants 60 election merit simply for holding an assignment; existing profession experience remains part of the normal skill score.
- **Changed:** only living, non-imprisoned adult settlers currently assigned the Soldier job can be mustered for militia strength, defenses, barricades, outgoing raids, combat animations, experience and raid casualties. Ordinary civilian villagers are excluded from the fighting pool.
- **Added:** Successful defenders earn +15 permanent leadership merit and successful outgoing raiders +25. The combined bonus is capped at 100 per settler and saved. Stalemates, defeats, and partial raids earn none.
- **Added:** Established surnames contribute up to +25 election merit based on living relatives and generations. Single-member surnames receive no bonus. The dynasty summary is calculated once per candidate-ranking pass.
- **Added:** election and militia regression tests for Town Hall neutrality, soldier-only combat, earned battle merit, and family-name prestige.

- **Added:** About 1% of newborns gain a strong starting professional skill (40–75), including roughly 0.1% with exceptional talent (80–95). Rolls are seeded and saved; schooling, aging, and existing settlers remain unchanged.
- **Added:** regression coverage for natural-talent frequency, single-trade awards and skill bounds.

- **Changed:** childhood age now follows calendar days only; school attendance and nurturing villagers no longer accelerate physical aging, preventing abrupt age changes when staffing or school attendance changes.
- **Changed:** the Village → Schooling entry shows enrolled children versus school-age children without places, and the Schooling window distinguishes classroom attendance during school hours from enrollment and children too young for school.

- **Fixed:** Leader's House residents are recorded when leadership becomes vacant and retain their beds through the succession period, including housing sweeps and saving and loading. The chosen successor takes over the manor only after the election, at which point the prior household is relocated.
- **Fixed:** hotel lodging notices and Chronicle entries report occupied beds across all completed player hotels instead of one hotel's eight-bed capacity. Occupancy announcements no longer disappear when no visitors are waiting to check in.
- **Fixed:** the citizen inspector no longer infers hunting or work from proximity to a building. Juveniles show no work shift or military gear; the simulation clears stale juvenile hunting targets and excludes children from hunting-post movement.
- **Added:** regression cases for child activity near hunting posts, citywide hotel reporting with no waiting visitors, and Leader's House retention through succession and elected handover.

- **Fixed:** the colony-wide Auto-fill command switches every player workplace from Manual to Auto-fill before staffing, remains available from Manual workplaces, and reports when no additional idle adults were assigned.

- **Fixed:** generic family convergence leaves manor residents to the leader-house owner; leader move-ins immediately clear old residence occupant lists, preventing duplicate households after reload.

- **Fixed:** a leader household already living in the manor is protected before ordinary family-capacity checks, preventing repeated moves into normal housing and back, including a manor with identifier zero.

- **Fixed:** unconfirmed commands survive simulation-worker restart and replay against the last confirmed state; obsolete command chains cannot submit duplicate actions after recovery.

- **Fixed:** queued commands stop new tick submissions so the worker queue can drain; worker recovery holds simulation ticks and defers commands instead of running expensive recovery work on the UI thread.

- **Fixed:** colony-wide staffing, demolition, construction and other expensive commands wait for the worker result instead of replaying the same simulation on the UI thread; bounded single-worker and venue-hour previews remain immediate.

- **Changed:** A* searches reuse generation-stamped buffers; a bounded LRU retains routes for large commuter populations and retries failed routes after an origin or destination change.
- **Changed:** automatic staffing reuses assignment and transfer-eligibility counts; commute eligibility skips road scans when walking-distance bounds already decide the result.
- **Added:** opt-in CPU stage reports include rolling p50, p95 and p99 timings and lifetime counts above the one-second tick budget; population benchmarks support repeatable seeds and save and reload checks.
- **Changed:** developer profiler and simulation diagnostics panels load conditionally in development and are excluded from production bundles.


## 0.6.5.1[19 october 2026]

- **Fixed:** synchronized the asset register with the shipped Village Square artwork (`square.jpg`) and updated the Square's sprite path; Graveyard and Villa are also marked as used by the building renderer.
- **Added:** automated Villa housing regression tests covering Better Housing unlocks, build-catalog registration, 14/16/18 resident capacities, the level-3 upgrade ceiling, and unchanged House/Mansion capacity rules.
- **Added:** Villa residence using `/sprites/villa.png` with shelter for 14 villagers; listed under Housing and included in residential occupancy and population housing capacity. Building a Villa requires the new **Better Housing** research in the Architecture tree (after Fine Construction), including on older saves. Villa upgrades follow the standard housing rules: level 1 shelters 14 people, level 2 shelters 16 and level 3 shelters 18.
- **Added:** a unique Village Square using `/sprites/square.jpg`, serving as the gathering place for festivals and elections, with the previous gathering locations retained when no completed square exists.
- **Changed:** the three leading election candidates stand at the center of the ceremony, election phases last longer (24 hours gathering, 20 hours discussion, 8 hours final tension), and each phase is recorded in the Chronicle.
- **Added:** buildable Graveyard using `/sprites/graveyard.png`, one Gravekeeper job per graveyard, and up to 50 memorial records per graveyard; names of villagers appear in green and enemy casualties in red. Adult relatives can occasionally visit recorded family graves outside working hours.
- **Added:** a single persistent **Weather visuals** switch under Graphics, controlling all weather overlays, particles, fog, clouds, storms and seasonal particles without changing simulated weather.
- **Added:** a paced full-screen text chronicle for outgoing raid battles, presenting successive phases and recorded battle outcomes and named casualties.
- **Changed:** victorious outgoing raids cause permanent rival population losses, with overwhelmingly successful attacks able to eliminate rival settlements and clear associated events and structures. Rival deaths are recorded individually where characters exist, alongside counts for off-map casualties.
- **Changed:** raid outcomes report spoils, own and enemy casualties, village victory announcements or mourning, and leadership rewards in the game Chronicle.
- **Fixed:** the simulation worker is only enabled in supported runtimes, so main-thread fallback and diagnostics work without a browser Worker.
- **Fixed:** prison guard patrols retain the assigned crew and do not leave their posts for social walks while commuting to a shift.
- **Fixed:** the Leader's House can be upgraded, with each level increasing the number of available beds.
- **Fixed:** valley ecology transitions continue to enter the Chronicle when warnings are muted, without bypassing the cooldown for visible alerts.
- **Changed:** continuous integration now typechecks and runs the full unit test suite, in addition to source checks and the production build.
- **Fixed:** raid chronicles distinguish threats, marches, combat outcomes, casualties and cancellations; raid totals and village traits no longer count the same raid multiple times.
- **Changed:** combat CSV exports include raid stage and kind, and the combat panel labels each stage.
- **Fixed:** removed the Gazette hook dependency warning while keeping live Chronicle updates visible; newspaper summaries are only calculated when the Gazette is open.
- **Fixed:** restored the CPU profiler integration removed by a later update, repairing the build and restoring start, stop, live samples and the final worker report.
- **Fixed:** the CPU profiler docks above the map's lower-right controls instead of covering the build menu; expanding its report no longer moves the toggle.
- **Fixed:** prison watch assignments keep the same weekly crew, including guards from overstaffed saves. Guards commute on weekends and off-screen; hourly post checks run after movement so arrivals count.
- **Fixed: pregnancies conceived through youth love keep their origin through breakups, birthdays and Moon Howler transformations; babies take the mother's surname without a bastard label. Existing custody and housing rules remain unchanged.**
- **Added:** an opt-in in-game CPU profiler records call counts and CPU timing for simulation and rendering; export its report as JSON.
- **Fixed:** Stop now waits for the worker's final CPU sample before Copy, and the profiler control no longer covers the mini-map.
- **Added: a read-only weekly village newspaper in the Log window, with real stories, social news and weekly event counts from the existing Chronicle.**
- **Fixed: when the Chronicle reaches its retention limit, the oldest Gazette week is marked as potentially incomplete and its counts are shown as minimums.**
- **Added: a saved toggle to hide ordinary pop-up notifications without disabling the simulation, Chronicle or decision cards.**
- **Changed: human updates reuse workplace groups, social venue pools and entity lookups per tick, avoiding repeated scans and temporary coworker arrays.**
- **Changed: each election gets a seeded swing of up to ±20% in candidates' merit support, so a close race can go either way while a large lead still matters.**
- **Changed: one capped trade now adds 50 merit, total skill merit caps at 100, and incumbent record penalties are softened and bounded.**
- **Changed: children decide once per in-game hour whether to play with a nearby child, then keep steering toward that playmate between checks.**
- **Changed: settlers walk commutes without teleporting or distance-based speed spikes; urgent walks use a fixed speed cap, and stuck settlers recover onto nearby walkable ground.**
- **Changed: the valley's ecology ladder is live again** — Stable, Strained, Damaged and Collapse now follow the deer, the wolves, the meadows and the town's footprint, thinning hunts and farms and costing reputation, with every warning arriving before its effect.
- **Fixed: the Wildlife Preserve can be built again** — it was left out of the build menu's categories, so its ecosystem bonus and the Keeper of the Wild chapter were unreachable in normal play.
- **Fixed: the shortcut sheet and the Guide now name every window the way the header does** — the doors read People and More instead of Village and Menu, and both say that V and O open the same People window.
- **Changed: wolf fight-backs at the Hunting Spot now bite in one hunt in ten rather than one in three** — and a hunter's own trade skill cuts the fatal band from 5% to 1% at mastery.
- **Fixed: the Wildkin is now the apex creature it was meant to be** — it ate grass and its bites were billed against the pasture, so the top of the ladder read as a deer; it now eats meat, is no longer a grazer, and its birth, first sighting and death are chronicled with the place each happened.
- **Fixed: every trade now advances when its workers turn up, not when the store accepts the goods** — a full store paid the crew no skill at all, so only farming produced masters while smiths, miners and teachers stayed at their entry skill; each staffed post now pays a shift of skill a day.
- **Changed: election merit weighs a whole career, not only trade skill** — experience, Town Hall service, friendships, titles and the incumbent's record all contribute to the result.
- **Fixed: a build spree no longer floods the chronicle** — a day that finishes several buildings of one kind now writes one line with a count ("Wall completed ×30") instead of one line each.
- **Fixed: a festival name that already ended in "Festival" said it twice** — the chronicle read "Harvest Festival festival began in the village"; both festival writers now name the festival once.
- **Fixed: the shift-fatigue line dropped a word** — "Short shifts spared 1 settler that output cost" now reads "spared 1 settler from that output cost".
- **Fixed: the worked-hours figure in the shift-fatigue line reads as the average it is** — "each worked 22.7h on average", not a shift length.
- **Fixed: childhood now ends at the adult floor** — a settler became an adult and took a job at 12, so children filled the postings while adult settlers sat idle and their children could not be apprenticed.
- **Fixed: a settler widowed mid-pregnancy never married again** — conception marks both spouses as expecting and only the birth clears the father, so a wife's death stranded her husband in a status the courtship rule refuses; existing saves repair on the next tick.
- **Fixed: a saved valley now regenerates as the valley it was saved from** — a compact save records the terrain noise seed it was generated with, and an older save with none falls back to the pre-change seed when its own completed fishing docks show the current one reclaimed their water.
- **Fixed: two lifetime records that never recorded anything** — a colony's gathered resources and its most productive year stayed at zero for a whole game; gathering is now counted where storage accepts it, the best year is settled at each year boundary, and both show under Lifetime Records.
- **Fixed: an on-duty prison guard no longer abandons his post to visit his wife** — a free-time walk to a distant spouse overwrote the work commute in the same tick, so the rota was full, nobody stood on the post, and every such hour rolled a prisoner escape.
- **Fixed: animals and settlers no longer flick back to where they stood a moment ago** — the render snapshot and the simulation were both writing entity positions, and the snapshot is a past tick, so it dragged every mover back one buffer behind itself.
- **Fixed: the drawn position no longer goes stale while a command is in flight** — the render cache was keyed on the tick alone, and a command's refresh arrives at an unchanged tick, so it served the previous frame's entities.
- **Fixed: a settler-only pregnancy counter no longer rides on every blade of grass and tree** — it was initialised on every entity, so each save carried a number only a mother can use; it now appears only once a settler is expecting, which shrinks every save.
- **Changed: grass and trees no longer carry a combat roll seed** — only the species that take part in a predator or prey roll are seeded, which trims a number a blade or a trunk can never use and shrinks every save.
- **Changed: grass and trees no longer carry an animation frame or a settler generation** — only species whose animation clock runs, and only settlers in a family tree, read them, so both numbers leave every blade and trunk and the save shrinks.
- **Fixed: a reloaded colony no longer re-adds an animation frame and a generation to every blade of grass and tree** — the load path defaulted both on every element, so one reload put them back and the next save paid for them again.
- **Fixed: a long pause no longer reads as a stalled simulation worker** — the silence clock kept ageing while the game was paused, so the first tick after a pause condemned a healthy worker and the game fell back to main-thread ticks.
- **Fixed: settlers no longer walk over water or through walls** — the grid that blocks them was read only on long walks, so a short leg, or one with no way around, crossed the obstacle. Every step now asks it and slides along what it refuses.
- **Fixed: a bridge is now a crossing** — a completed bridge was marked as walkable but left the water under its deck blocked, so the router still refused it and the settler waded the river beside it.
- **Fixed: walkers no longer cut a corner diagonally** — the route advanced to the next waypoint from half a tile away, which cut one-tile gaps and left the walker pressed against the bank.
- **Added: the save inspector audits the ground the colony stands on** — a sent-in save now names anyone standing in water, inside a wall or on reserved decor, and can run the colony forward to show whether anyone walks onto it.
- **Fixed: a festival no longer pays innkeepers for the whole day** — the all-day tavern opening was credited as work, so every innkeeper worked a 24-hour day and sat at maximum fatigue, which cost the tavern a third of its output.
- **Fixed: loading a save no longer blacks out the game** — the toast list was dropped on load while the shell still expected one, so the first render after any load threw.
- **Fixed: the opening cinematic and the valley choice behind it are back on every load** — the cinematic was always skippable in place, so suppressing it on a return visit only removed the map screen.
- **Fixed: a tamed animal that returns to the wild when its owner dies now leaves a Chronicle entry, so the change no longer happens silently.**
- **Changed: the mountain ground fill is now the re-exported PNG** — the bake and its boot preload name the file the art actually ships as, so the rock texture loads again.
- **Fixed: removed the 30-second auto-save — a manual save now always exports a JSON file, and the toggle now means save-on-exit.**
- **Changed: the stale one-off scripts under the archive folder are gone** — nothing referenced them and their imports pointed at modules that no longer exist, so the folder and the two exclusion clauses it needed were removed.
- **Changed: auto-assign is now the default for every workplace** — a building staffs itself automatically unless the player sets it to manual, and the manual toggle still keeps a hand-picked crew out of the auto pass.
- **Changed: toasts are no longer written into a save** — a notification's life is measured on the real-world clock, so a restored one arrived already expired; the save keeps the dismissal ledger but not the toasts.
- **Fixed: a speech bubble's tail now reaches the speaker** — it was stopped three pixels short of its target even with nothing in its way, so it pointed at the head without touching it.
- **Added: the hotel and hospital inspectors name everyone staying there** — the hotel lists visitor guests and settler lodgers with whether they are asleep, and the hospital lists each patient in the ward with the reason they need care.
- **Changed: a hospital now takes five doctors instead of two** — the ward's crew cap rises so a full ward round and doctor-on-duty care can both be staffed at once.
- **Fixed: the player's inspector no longer shows the dev-only simulation diagnostics, and stale copy names the current windows** — the drawer is gated on the debug flag, and the guide and tutorial no longer point at the retired tab rail.
- **Fixed: a visitor walking to a hotel no longer re-plans the whole route every step** — the route cache key held the visitor's moving position, so it missed every tick, re-ran the pathfinder and churned the shared cache; it is keyed on the visitor now.
- **Fixed: an unaffordable building tile no longer accepts a click** — the catalogue re-derived the cost rule locally and used it only for dimming, so an unpayable building could be selected and then refused at the map; it now reads the cost owner and names what is short.
- **Fixed: the long-run gate's checkpoint verdict no longer counts a rival's buildings as the colony's own** — a checkpoint read healthy because a neighbour camp had raised buildings, while the end-of-run verdict correctly saw the player had built nothing.
- **Fixed: a settler cursed into Moon Howler form mid-romance no longer comes back to a one-sided sweetheart link** — the pair is held still while one is transformed, so the bond stays mutual.
- **Fixed: the village head's next-vote year no longer prints a fraction** — the office badge read "next vote Y2.2472222222222222" where the election date is a fractional year; it now shows the whole year.
- **Made a weather turn leave a record** — the weather changed silently, so the Chronicle never showed when conditions shifted; each change now logs a line.
- **Fixed: the lifetime "Largest population" record no longer under-reports the peak** — it sampled only the year-end headcount, so a colony that peaked mid-year read a lower figure; it now tracks the peak every tick.
- **Made settlers follow a cached route to its end instead of re-planning every few steps** — a long blocked walk re-ran the pathfinder every 32 px of progress although the route had not changed; it now re-plans only when the walker leaves the route.
- **Made the pathfinder drop one stale route instead of clearing the whole cache** — past 200 settlers pathing at once, the cache emptied and everyone re-planned from scratch; it now evicts only the oldest.
- **Fixed: the fatigue line no longer reports an average of worked hours as a shift** — a fractional day like 22.3h read as an impossible shift length; it now says the settlers worked that many hours on average.
- **Added a lint and typecheck pass for the harness scripts** — the simulation probes and drivers sat outside every check, so their unused code and type errors went unseen; they are now reported by their own config.
- **Fixed the city benchmark so each tier holds its own population** — the per-tier target landed in the maintainer's minimum-headcount slot and was ignored, so the larger tiers ran down instead of staying steady.
- **Made auto-play fortify only a neighbour it has already paid** — the bot sends the food gift first and only raises a watchtower, barracks or wall when a hostile rival took that gift and stayed hostile.
- **Stopped auto-play raising a Bridge** — bridges are left to the player to place, so the bot no longer proposes one.
- **Spread the colony's residence assignment across the day** — the day boundary rehoused every settler at once and could freeze the game for a minute; each pulse now sweeps a quarter of the colony instead of the whole at once, so no single tick carries the day's housing work.
- **Refused a corrupt election-buildup year at the save boundary** — the year the buildup notice was raised was the last leadership value accepted without a rule, so a save carrying an unusable one loaded and then misread the notice; it is refused by name now, like its siblings.
- **Made a raid grow with the colony it is aimed at** — the war-band and the loot it is after now scale with the settlers you can muster, so a large village faces a fight worth answering instead of a walkover.
- **Opened the frontier in time to matter** — a neighbour camp now appears once your colony is real instead of waiting years on a rare event, so raids, the raid banner and the raid action can exist in a normal run.
- **Made rival camps roll for a raid every day** — the roll only ran on the camp's own action day, once every two to three months, so raiding could go a whole game without happening; the camp's own cooldown and odds now set the pace.
- **Made the long-run gate judge the colony, not only its invariants** — a settlement that raises nothing while its beds are full and settlers stand outside them now fails the run as stalled, and a stalled default scenario is reported rather than the rule softened to pass it.
- **Made auto-play say what it is waiting for instead of calling an idle hour healthy** — the status line now names the larder, the beds, the study or the price behind the wait, in the game's own wording, and says the colony looks healthy only when nothing is short.
- **Stopped auto-play ignoring a producer nobody works, and let it build ahead of a growing village** — a quarry with no settler on it made no stone while the bot treated stone as handled; it now mans it or adds another, and housing, food, wood, stone and iron keep pace with the population.
- **Bounded that extra spending so it cannot starve the colony** — building ahead now leaves the price of a repair and two days of food in store, while a real shortage still spends the last of them.
- **Let the colony reach its storage buildings** — a silo waited for a full gold store although it holds food, and the barn, silo and wood storehouse sat behind a food reserve a full shelf never needed.
- **Smoothed movement and fixed hover tinting** — settlers, animals and particles blend between ticks instead of jumping once a second (a teleport is drawn straight), building hover tints update every frame and while paused, and all motion runs on one clock.
- **Made the ground re-bake at the zoom you are looking at** — a chunk now re-bakes in place when the zoom crosses a step, at most one chunk per frame, so a zoomed-out overview resolves at half resolution instead of sharpening every chunk at full detail; the enlarged maps stay smooth.
- **Let settlers sleep at a hotel when home is too far** — a settler whose walk home would outlast the hours before their sleep hour now lodges at the nearest completed hotel instead of crossing the map; the eight beds are shared with paying guests and the cap is never exceeded.
- **Raised a hotel's sleeping capacity from four to eight** — the staffed hotel's beds are one pool, shared by paying visitor guests and by settlers lodging overnight.
- **Kept the night prison guard on his post, and let settlers work through a festival** — a pre-dawn trip home teleported the rostered night guard off his post, so those hours counted unguarded; and a festival suspended everyone's work, where it now gathers only settlers who are off shift.
- **Made a feud's end announced** — a feud could be announced as brewing and then fade with no word, because the start line and the end line used different bars; both use the same one now, and a feud ended by a death is left to the death line.
- **Made a reload continue the same colony** — a save taken while the simulation ran in its worker recorded the main thread's random streams instead of the colony's, so a reload did not carry on the same deterministic sequence; the snapshot now travels with the saved world.
- **Made rivers reach the sea on the big maps** — a river could let out into a lake whose own channel climbs back over land, so the channel ended in a basin; an outlet now has to drain to a below-sea border cell, which holds at every map size and land type.
- **Enlarged the three map sizes to the renderer's full spec** — Medium 2560×1920, Large 4096×3072, Huge 6144×4608; settlers walk twice as fast so a commute still takes the hours it did, the smaller-map assumptions were re-derived, and a big-map colony saves to a file instead of the in-game slot.
- **Made a building's hover highlight appear at once, and while paused** — the highlight left the entity layer's cache key when that layer was memoised, so the ring only redrew on the next tick; it is painted per frame now, beside the placement ghost.
- **Put the home and workplace rings on the building they mark** — both were built from the sprite's base anchor as if it were a centre, so a settler's house ring sat half a footprint above the house.
- **Gave a refused raid the reason the rules already return** — marching on a rival re-implemented six eligibility gates and returned silently, so a blocked raid button did nothing and explained nothing; it now shows the same reason the eligibility owner gives.
- **Let a vacant leader's house pass to the next household** — the manor stayed with the departed head's family until a new head was elected, and a save preserved the squat; a house is vacated whenever no living settler holds the office.
- **Made the voting-day gossip reachable** — it was gated to a tick remainder the ceremony can never land on, so the four "they are about to announce" lines could never fire; the roll now happens when the phase turns to tension.
- **Let a research gift that fills the bar finish the project** — a visitor's gift clamped progress at the cap instead of completing, so the unlock arrived only on the next systems pulse; both gift paths now share the owner's completion rule.
- **Made the default test gate check types, lint and import cycles** — the default run ran neither a typecheck nor the cycle scan, which is how a committed type error and a runtime import cycle went unnoticed; it now runs both, and the long gate runs the year-long integration test.
- **Stopped the dashboard and the combat log freezing at the values they opened with** — both memoised a projection on the world object, which the simulation mutates in place, so neither could see a change while the simulation ran on the main thread.
- **Made the Guide and the shortcut sheet name the same six header doors** — each listed its own set, so the in-game help sent a stuck player to a door the header does not have; both now render one door list.
- **Kept the map-setup gallery from clipping its own cards** — the second row of "Choose your land" was cut by the footer and pushed the size and difficulty choices off-screen at 900×600; the gallery scrolls inside a bounded height now, with the cue in its heading.
- **Stopped a School and a Town Hall claiming their workers are assigned automatically** — the panel restated the manual-staffing rule by hand and omitted both, so a player waited for a teacher who could never arrive; it now reads the workforce owner's own verdict.
- **Stopped a prison counting its prisoners as guards against the guard cap** — the workers line measured everyone in the building against the post count, so a fully guarded prison with cells to spare said "No idle settlers"; both numbers now come from the staffing owner.
- **Stopped a malformed save's lists breaking the next day's food ledger** — the food history and the two dismissal lists had no rule at the load boundary, so a string in one of them was accepted and then threw inside a tick; each is now refused by name.
- **Made the placement ghost and the placed building stand on the same ground** — the ghost sampled the bottom centre of a footprint while a placed building sampled a corner half a footprint away, so a building jumped vertically on a slope.
- **Stopped the work-hours panel and the taming list freezing at the values they opened with** — a projection memoised on the world object cannot see a tick when the simulation runs on the main thread; both now derive every render.
- **Made the venue editor quote the rule the tavern actually opens by** — the festival note was its own third copy of the override and could promise behaviour the simulation no longer had; it now renders the schedule owner's answer.
- **Stopped panning the map throwing away what you had selected** — the click that follows a drag arrives after the drag ends, so every pan cleared the selection and collapsed the inspector; a drag is now left to the camera.
- **Made the work-hours editor and the family tree hold the keyboard** — the map's hotkeys stayed live behind those two windows and Escape acted on the game instead of closing them.
- **Showed a catalogue tile with no sprite art as the building's own emoji** — a decor type with no image asked the server for the page itself.
- **Repointed a settler-trait note at the modules that read it** — the comment named a simulation file that was split into domain modules long ago.
- **Stopped a house holding more settlers than it has beds** — a marriage that could not seat the whole family, a divorce and a birth each wrote a residence without asking for a free bed, so a six-bed house could hold ten; every such move now takes the residence owner's room rule.
- **Made the long-run gate check housing at every checkpoint** — an over-capacity settlement was only tested at year end, so a failure named the house but not the day it first appeared.
- **Stopped a home with room for one bed being asked to take two movers** — the least-crowded rule discounted a mover who already lived there twice, so a settler marrying someone already housed walked the couple into a full house.
- **Gave the Prison four guard posts beside four cells** — the owner's cap: three nine-hour shifts still need three guards for round-the-clock cover, and the fourth takes rest days in turn.
- **Stopped the prison's own guards being jailed for scandal** — a caught affair jailed both offenders with no role check, so the sentence dissolved the very post the prison needs, and an unguarded prison leaks its prisoners.
- **Checked every lifetime statistic a save carries** — only two of the fourteen fields were backfilled, so a damaged payload could leave the colony's counters undefined.
- **Made the Big News cards survive a save** — the cards were dropped while their dismissed-id list was kept, so a loaded colony's news panel came back empty.
- **Made a loaded colony's id bookkeeping consistent** — duplicate chronicle ids were kept beside live ones, and the allocator and entity-index repairs ran after the passes that resolve entities by id.
- **Stopped a load writing the player's auto-save preference** — a save's own setting is now adopted by the loaded colony only, never by the browser default the next settlement starts from.
- **Said which field a bad save file failed on** — the load-from-file path reported only "cannot be restored", and valid JSON that is not a save was blamed on a syntax error instead.
- **Made the save-slot check read the slot instead of parsing the save** — a returning player paid a full synchronous parse before the first frame.
- **Vetted the weather on load** — an unknown weather or a non-numeric timer loaded into a valley whose weather could never change again.
- **Removed the dead founding-card state** — a deleted title card left a state slot and an unreachable branch; the live chapter card now has the name it actually renders.
- **Showed the biodiversity index against its own ceiling** — a bare number is now a score with an evenness bar a player can read.
- **Kept the diagnostics drawer when nothing is selected** — clearing a selection silently removed the panel the player had deliberately opened.
- **Put the build hotkey on the collapsed build rail** — the only hint was a hover tooltip, which a touch player never sees.
- **Let the Guide open the shortcut sheet** — the sheet it pointed at could not be reached from the Guide, which has no hotkey of its own.
- **Listed every taming candidate instead of four** — the picker silently truncated its list, and now says how many are available and why a button is dead.
- **Showed a settler's name instead of their internal id** — the citizen panel labelled people with the save's allocator id.
- **Reached the chronicle and the combat log pre-filtered from a concern** — the dashboard's concerns now open the log on their own filter.
- **Deleted the retired sidebar's styles** — the tab rail is gone, and its CSS was still shipped.
- **Stopped the parked ecology ladder rewriting persisted state every day** — the disabled valley stage is now idempotent and normalises a legacy save once.
- **Removed a third Big News id allocator** — a private counter produced ids that the rollback contract and the dismissed-id list could not see.
- **Counted the colony where the daily tail uses it** — the tail reasoned about the population the day started with.
- **Rolled one prison escape chance per unguarded hour** — the daily pass stopped after the first escape, so a prison left open all day freed at most one prisoner.
- **Read the ecosystem score through its owner** — the village-health line read the raw field, so a legacy save scored an undefined ecosystem.
- **Made one despawn rule for finished caravans and departing visitors** — the carrier splice was the last manual removal beside the tick's own membership rebuild.
- **Repointed the architecture notes at the files that exist** — they still named a simulation file that was split into domain modules.
- **Stopped rebuilding per-frame work the frame did not change** — the ground-chunk plan, the overlay vignette's gradient and the entity-layer cache key are reused until an input actually moves.
- **Kept the blacksmith scan out of camera-only frames** — a panning camera re-scanned every building for a colony-wide fact only a tick can change.
- **Gave a building without a sprite its own footprint pad** — an unloaded sprite drew one white box instead of the building's own category colour.
- **Dropped four sprite paths that pointed at files which never existed** — those decor types draw procedurally, so the catalogue advertised images that could only 404.
- **Added a gender glyph to settler name plates** — gender was carried by label colour alone.
- **Honoured the system's reduced-motion setting on the canvas** — pulses, sway, shimmer, sparkle and screen shake now hold still, while movement and floating text keep their timing.
