- **Loaded exactly the fill sprites the ground stamps** — the preload list named files nothing read and missed the ones the bake draws.
- **Made the volume preset reach the fallback music player, and bounded the master bus** — soft, normal and loud did nothing when the HTML audio fallback was playing, and the loud preset could clip.
- **Refused a save whose container arrays cannot be read** — a malformed list among the world's arrays reached the interface and showed the player raw engine text instead of naming the field that was wrong.
- **Bounded the clock fields a loaded save may claim** — a negative, fractional or astronomically large tick was accepted, and the header, seasons and daily gate all derive a date from it, so a crafted file could freeze the calendar or name a year before the settlement.
- **Made a malformed venue window load as its documented default** — the tavern and hotel schedules were copied from the save unchecked, so unusable hours silently became the default and the bad value was written back by the next save.
- **Stopped offering a settler a post they could not walk to** — assignment took the nearest idle settler whatever the distance, so a family housed across the valley could be handed a school they could never reach; a post over two hours' walk from home is now refused, and a road on the route helps.
- **Made the commute estimate agree with the walk it describes** — the lead-schedule estimate omitted the travel multiplier the work commute actually uses, so every reported commute read 3.5× longer than the one walked, and settlers set off for work hours earlier than they needed to.
- **Counted a settler's shift even when the camera was elsewhere** — the off-screen throttle skipped the attendance counter on 23 of every 24 ticks, so a workplace's output depended on where the player was looking; attendance is now recorded for the shift, not for the frame.
- **Moved a docked caravan's wait onto the clock it is measured in** — the countdown ran inside the same per-settler throttle, so a caravan the player watched reached its partner up to 24 times sooner than one they did not.
- **Stopped the yearly death tally counting departures as deaths** — finished caravan carriers and departing visitor groups are despawned by marking them not alive, so every completed trade run added a phantom settler death to the year review.
- **Let a disaster end when hostile weather is switched off** — the feature's early return sat above the loop that ages disasters, so a save carrying one kept it forever and permanently penalised the village's election score.
- **Made the grid markers and zone hints ride the terrain relief** — they were drawn at the flat-map height while the buildings that land on them were lifted, so the two disagreed by up to a relief cap on a slope.
- **Kept every critical alert on the strip and marked it without colour alone** — a flat two-chip limit hid the third of three simultaneous criticals behind "+N more".
- **Gave blocked trade and raid buttons a visible reason** — the refusal lived only in a hover tooltip, which is unreachable on touch and on a disabled, unfocusable button.
- **Showed the village why a workplace cannot be staffed** — switching a building to manual with no idle settler printed a bare "No idle settlers"; it now lists the settlers the staffing owner refuses and its own wording for each.
- **Made the day rollover's entity rebuild linear** — the end-of-tick merge tested every new spawn against the whole living list, so the tick with the most arrivals did the most work; one membership set replaces the scan, keeping the same survivors in the same order.
- **Stopped a loaded valley growing its forest back over the houses** — a compact save regenerates the ground from its seed, and the record of terrain the player cleared was never written, so every building's footprint returned to forest on load; the sparse overrides now round-trip.
- **Cleared the relationships a loaded colony cannot resolve** — a spouse or courtship id naming nobody left a settler stuck as married to nobody, who could then never widow and was shut out of courtship permanently; a load now sweeps those ids, reusing the same owner the death path uses.
- **Stopped a save choosing its own map size and calendar** — an untrusted tile dimension pair could ask for a 4000x3000 valley, and a forged ticks-per-day rescaled the whole timeline to the year 66666; both are now bounded to what this build actually writes.
- **Made a refused load leave the running colony alone** — the restore replaced the RNG seed part-way through, so a save that was then refused kept the failed load's randomness for the rest of the session; the session's seed and auto-save setting are now put back.
- **Stopped a corrupted save poisoning every later spawn** — a payload missing an id allocator gave every new settler and building an id of `NaN`, and a rewound one collided with live ids; each is now raised past the highest id the world holds.
- **Refused save files with duplicate, fractional or negative ids** — two entities sharing an id made one of them vanish from every id lookup, unable to be selected or targeted, and the next save wrote the collision back.
- **Stopped a corrupted festival lasting forever** — the daily tick ends a festival when its remaining days reach zero, so a stored non-number made that test permanently false and the 1.5× festival boost never ended.
- **Told the truth when a save reaches a file but not the browser slot** — a full storage quota was swallowed, so the game reported success and then offered Load against a slot it had never written, which could restore an older colony instead.
- **Made a wolf able to kill** — a fight-back could only ever cost the hunter energy, so a long colony's chronicle held thousands of bites and no deaths; one roll now chooses between killed, mauled and an ordinary bite at the owner's odds.
- **Stopped divorced couples remarrying the next day** — nothing recorded that two settlers had already been married, so a pair who still shared a house courted and remarried immediately after every divorce; a marriage now keeps them apart for a year.
- **Stopped selling upgrades that do nothing** — the Church, School, Tavern, Prison, Barracks, Hospital and Town Hall all offered an upgrade because they are staffed, but nothing reads their level; the rule now lists the types whose level really changes output or beds.
- **Let the affair system actually run** — its daily check compared a tick of the day against an hour, so it could only match the small hours when the settler was asleep at home and its own rules refused it; most married settlers never reached it at all.
- **Counted a Town Hall official as working** — officials served their shift while the chronicle called every official day a rest day.
- **Skipped the opening cinematic on a browser that has seen it** — it is a 23.6-second reveal that gated every load, so a returning player waited that long to reach Load; it plays once and stays reachable from the menu.
- **Made resuming a pause only a resume** — it also auto-staffed every idle settler, so pressing Space undid a deliberate choice to leave a building unstaffed.
- **Made settlers clickable** — the hit box was measured in world units and shrank as the player zoomed in to pick someone out of a crowd; it now has a floor measured on screen.
- **Fixed the mini-map's viewport box** — it was sized from the world instead of the canvas, so it was the wrong size at every zoom and ignored a window resize.
- **Stopped a notification self-destructing when you act on it** — clicking the card both jumped to the subject and dismissed it; the card now jumps and its own × dismisses.
- **Gave the arrow keys one owner** — they panned the camera and stepped the map cursor at the same time, which made the keyboard route onto the map unusable.
- **Taught auto-play to finish the village and raise its buildings** — the bot now builds the well, tavern, workshop, school, church, storage, mill, hotel and taming post once the settlement needs one, and upgrades completed buildings from surplus, only after food, housing and defence.
- **Cut the day-rollover freeze from 58 s to 18 s, and it is still going** — residency assignment asked whether a house held a couple, a minor or only singles by re-scanning the whole colony per question, per house, per family; one walk now answers all three.
- **Gave prison guards days off and split the jail's posts from its cells** — the Prison seats five guards beside four cells, three on watch and two resting each day, a resting guard no longer walks to a post he is not rostered for, and prisoners are no longer listed as the jail's workers.
- **Made the high-speed report admit when it cannot see** — it averaged the simulation's clamped frame time, so a three-second frame printed as 100 ms; it now reports the raw frame maximum, ticks in flight, a pending command, banked lag, worker silence and page visibility.
- **Fixed the population benchmark measuring one colony at every tier** — the per-tick maintainer re-pinned the fixture to 300 settlers, so all six tiers ran the same population; each tier is now that fixture at its own size.
- **Stopped a silent worker command freezing the village** — a command whose reply never arrived held the tick pipeline shut, and with no tick left in flight the stall watchdog could not fire, so the world stopped advancing while the map kept drawing at full rate. A command now has a deadline.
- **Stopped a save reading the sim worker's host after a fault replaced it** — the export checked the host once, then awaited; a stall landing in that gap left the next line reading nothing, which threw while saving. The host is captured and re-checked by identity, as the load path already did.
- **Added Deno alongside npm** — the Deno 2.9 runtime is installed beside the existing setup, with `deno:build` and `deno:test` entries that drive the same single gate runner, so npm keeps the lockfile and nothing is replaced.
- **Made the Deno build produce a real desktop window** — it pointed at a browser-only React entry and died on a CSS import; it now packages the built game into a self-contained window that opens like the Tauri shell, with the unused dependency payload left out of the binary.
- **Stopped one unappliable worker message from wedging the sim** — a tick or command result that arrived with no world attached was dropped without clearing its in-flight flag, so the game fell back to main-thread ticks and every save timed out waiting for the worker to settle.
- **Told the player how a prison sentence ends** — an escape and a release both only reached the map and the generic Events bucket of the chronicle; both now raise a notification and file under the prison filter, beside the jailing that started them.
- **Counted any guard on the post, not only the one the roster named** — a fully-staffed prison still freed prisoners, because an hour with other guards standing there read as unguarded and carried an escape roll.
- **Gave the prison four prisoner beds beside its three guard shifts** — its occupancy was the guard posts plus one cell, so a fourth guard left the jail nowhere to hold anyone, and two places counted the cells differently.
- **Stopped a leaderless village repeating the postponement every day** — a vacancy that found nobody eligible retried, and logged, once a day; it now re-arms on the same delay the term election uses.
- **Let a founding-year election's promises count** — year 0 is a real election year, but promises recorded in it read as "none recorded", so they were never shown and never judged.
- **Drew the family tree instead of listing names** — the window drew one decorative stem per generation, so no name was joined to any other; each parent now branches to their own children, and a settler's card opens the tree from one icon.
- **Called a parent's spouse a step-parent** — every married-in relative was named an in-law, which is why a toddler with a remarried father appeared to have two mothers.
- **Deleted bullshit about buildingid names that is not correct**, residencyOccupancy.hasWorkAssignment` = `homeBuildingId != null`) is bullshit and made up in relationship file. removed the comments.
- **Named the jobless state honestly and gave the teardown one owner** — the shared job label called an unemployed settler a "Settler" as if it were a trade; it now reads Unemployed, and one helper clears the workplace pointer, job and occupation together.
- **Fixed the daily friendship pass starving large groups and bonding the jobless** — a group larger than the pair budget handed every bond to the same members, and the unemployed counted as sharing a trade; the window rotates daily and only a real job groups.
- **Made settlers actually walk the roads you build** — the route finder was blind to the road network, so a paved path only helped if someone already stood on it; a road tile now costs less to walk, so routes follow the network instead of cutting across open ground.
- **Fixed rison escapes reading the roster instead of the post** — a guard asleep at home still covered his shift, so a full crew never leaked; every clock hour nobody stands on the post is now one escape chance, and a guard works his own shift's hours.
- **The Prison window shows the roster** — who holds the night, morning and afternoon watch this week, who is on post right now, and how many shifts are unstaffed.
- **The Prison runs on shifts** — three nine-hour watches (night, morning, afternoon and evening) with a one-hour handover cover the day, the crew rotates weekly on its own, and a prisoner can only slip out in an hour no watch covers.
- **The Prison now seats the guard crew its own shifts require** — coverage divides the day into three 8-hour shifts, but the building allowed only two workers, so outside hours were certain and every prisoner escaped; it holds four, and the prisoner cap follows the crew.
- **Recorded the owners the call graph showed missing** — the terrain subsystem, the Hunting Spot's pursuit and kill, the window shells and three single-purpose leaves had no ownership row, so 110 functions had no owner; the graph now resolves all 2767.
- **Fixed children holding adult jobs, and school with it** — the juvenile flag is set at birth and only cleared by graduation, so a child with a wrong flag stayed a grown-up: posted, armed, and refused as a pupil. Loading a save now returns them to childhood.
- **Stopped lending the energy glyph to fatigue** — the panel gave fatigue as a percentage where higher meant a worse penalty, beside a settler's energy percentage where lower means trouble, so it read as energy left; it now names the fatigue band and gives the penalty as a multiplier.
- **The chronicle can now show its whole history on request** — the list drew the newest 500 rows with no way past them, which read as a 500-entry log; one button now loads the rest, and the rows are memoised so a full list stays cheap while the world ticks.
- **A scandal now says so when it goes unpunished** — a caught affair with no guard on duty at the prison was refused in silence, so the whole jailing chain looked broken; it now writes one line explaining why, the first time it happens.
- **A jailing now raises a notice that waits for you** — an imprisonment was announced only as one of the four transient toasts, so a rare jailing could pass unseen; it now posts a card like a raid or a festival does.
- **Archived the retired probes and the broken cycle tools out of the scripts folder** — they are parked with a note saying why, so the next reader extends the harness instead of writing another one-off probe.
- **Announced the election revelry, which ran silent** — the party boosts production, courtship and immigration but only wrote a Chronicle line, so its effects arrived with no word about them; it now raises a notice like the ceremony start does.
- **Fixed the chronicle export dropping its blob before the browser read it** — the download revoked its object URL in the same task as the click, so a large log could vanish with no error and no file; the revoke now waits.
- **Fixed a downloaded save's name disagreeing with the header clock** — a save taken in the colony's second year was named for the first, because the name printed the stored year while every other surface prints the year the player reads.
- **Fixed the family tree so it is about the person you opened it on** — the walk crossed every marriage into the whole colony and stopped at 200 names in visit order, so a settler's own child could go missing while a distant in-law stayed. Two generations either side now.
- **Kept 6 000 chronicle entries instead of 2 000** — a mature village filled the old window with ~200 days of history, so a first-year leader death had already rolled out of the log before anyone could look for it.
- **Capped close friendships at three per settler** — feuds always had a ceiling and friendships never did, so one measured settler ended up close to 54 people and the village read as "everyone became friends"; bonds an old save already holds are left untouched.
- **Removed the empty right-hand sidebar** — the column under the inspector held one div and a comment and no controls at all, so it took width from the map for nothing; the inspector now has that height.
- **Gave the village overview a keyboard route** — it had exactly one door, an unlabelled header icon with no fallback, so `C` toggles it beside `O` for the citizen overview.
- **Disabled the village portrait panel, which duplicated itself** — its window printed its own title twice, reprinted trait blurbs another window already shows with scores, and closed with commentary about the game's win conditions rather than the settlement.
- **Put the family tree behind a small tree icon and widened its window** — the full-width row made the right column tall, so the label and the relative summary moved into the tooltip, and the window opens 44rem wide instead of the chat-sized 26rem.
- **Fixed hot reload being disabled for the citizen card** — it exported a helper beside its components, which React Fast Refresh refuses, so every edit to it forced a full page reload.
- **Added lineage grouping to the family owner** — settlers now group by who descends from whom through the parent links, so two unrelated settlers who share a surname no longer read as one family, and a line stays rooted in a founder who has died.
- **Gave feuds a real source** — two settlers whose traits clash and who share a home, workplace or job can now drift into one, as can a wrong: a famine foot-bite, losing an election, or a rumour that reaches a cheated spouse. A 500-settler colony settles near a dozen live feuds.
- **Fixed the year the player reads, so the colony's first year is Year 1 and not Year 0** — every notice, log line, header and panel now reads the year through one formatter that adds one; the stored year, its comparisons and the save format are untouched.
- **Fixed the family tree so it draws the whole family from any member** — one row per generation with parents, grandparents, siblings, spouses and descendants; the clicked settler is highlighted, the same family appears whoever was clicked, and cycles and remarriages no longer cut it short.
- **Restored tabs in the work & venue hours window** — where three editors were stacked and scrolled, there is now an index you switch between, and every editor stays mounted so changing tab cannot discard a half-set hour window.
- **Put the child marker of the settler table back on the chip row** — a juvenile name no longer carries a bare emoji; a labelled child chip with a tooltip sits beside the no job and no home chips.
- **Added one test entry point with args, replacing seventeen npm scripts** — the gates are now one runner with a step menu, and the old names stay as aliases. Green: 245 files, 1495 tests, types and lint.
- **Fixed the lint gate, which named 70 files it never read** — the linter honours the ignore file, so listing the tooling directory linted nothing for a month; reading it surfaced 134 pre-existing findings, mostly missing node types.
- **Put the test suite, the tooling tree and 59 bug reports under version control for the first time** — 316 files that existed only on this machine, taking the tracked count from 733 to 1112.
- **Added a clean command that empties the scratch directory wholesale** — 391 MB of scratch, 44 MB of build output and 162 MB of leaked browser profiles, with no keep-list, because an exception list turns scratch into storage.
- **Fixed the front page, which pointed at three files that do not exist** — and it described a tool removed months earlier. The developer commands left it; that page is for players.
- **Added a docs guard to the check step** — a link in a live document must resolve, and the newest changelog section stays short and names no files.
- **Added a ratchet so newly added code comments cannot carry history** — no dates, audit IDs or file references. The 173 existing audit citations stay untouched, so it never fires on old code.
- **Changed the colony's social news to one line a year** — routine friendship wrote a line per bond, which made 60 % of a real 2 000-event chronicle friendship noise and buried the leadership election inside it.
- **Restored the building panel's inline sections** — its four subjects are visible again instead of an index you had to click into. The header icons are unchanged.
- **Fixed stale election cadence wording** — two places still claimed a decade for a two-year term, including the villager line that said "Decennial year!".

- **Fixed (developer workflow): the dev server no longer dies on a locked scratch file, and an HMR parse error is what "fast-forward stopped working" actually was** — two findings from one `vite` crash log. **(1) The crash.** The server had been exiting (`code 1`) six times in a session, each time leaving a page that could not reload: chokidar threw `EBUSY: resource busy or locked, watch 'C:\Wilderfolk\tmp\godot-ai-inspect\v4payload\addons\godot_ai\LICENSE'` and the error took the whole process down. `server.watch.ignored` now skips `tmp/**` alongside `src-tauri/target/**` — scratch is never imported by the app, so it has no business in the reload graph, and a locked file there can no longer kill the server. The **same fault had a second source** that killed it again straight after: the agent harness writes root files (that run: `command.md`) by atomically replacing them through a staging directory at the project root (`.command.md.<pid>.<guid>.tmpdir/command.md.tmp`), and vite watched that locked temp file and died — so editing the harness's own instructions took the dev server down. `**/*.tmpdir/**` and `**/*.tmp` are ignored too: nothing inside them is ever imported by the app, they are mid-write staging. **(2) The "broken" speed control.** The same log shows `[PARSE_ERROR] Identifier 'venueGatherPosition' has already been declared` in `humanLeisureBehavior.ts` at the moment a duplicate import existed during this session's editing — a failed transform breaks the module graph under HMR, so the page keeps its UI while the simulation stops advancing, which is exactly "the time fast forward doesnt work no anymre". It was fixed within the minute and the owner confirmed it cleared (*"nvm its solved"*); the measurement taken meanwhile is worth recording because it rules the speed path itself out: in a fresh build the **10× button crossed a colony day in under 4 s** and the **`5` hotkey advanced 01:00 → 16:00 in 4 s**, i.e. about a day every 5 s from both the pointer and the keyboard — better than the 9–12 s/day baseline the handover recorded for 10×. `tsc -p tsconfig.node.json` exit 0 after the watcher change.

- **Fixed: the idle crowd — settlers go home, to a venue, or somewhere pretty instead of piling into the map centre** — reported from play as *"they again in a circle"*, and identified only because the owner ruled out the two obvious suspects themselves (*"for what there is no election"*, *"i tgink there was no festival"*, *"i got 5 taverns"*). **Three separate rules were stacking settlers on one coordinate, and none of them needed an event.** (1) **The map centre.** Two idle rules — the partner-seeking drift and the idle-leisure wander in `humanTick` — sent every unoccupied settler to `width * 0.5, height * 0.5` inside a 35-slot lattice of offsets, so a village with nothing on piled its whole idle population into one chatting blob in the middle of the map. That is the clump: no election, no festival, five taverns and still one crowd. `idleDestination` (in the leisure owner) now gives each settler a real destination, chosen deterministically per person per day (`personDayRoll`, no RNG): **home** ("spending at home"), the **nearest venue** — tavern, market, town hall or church — or **somewhere pretty** off the beauty grid, which is the owner's own list: *"they should goto a tree or to the tavern etc or spending at home various options"*. (2) **The venue point.** The leisure gathering pointed every visitor at a single coordinate — `b.x + b.width / 2, b.y + b.height * 0.92`, the corner form applied to a value that already is the footprint centre — and each stopped within 20 px of it. `venueGatherPosition` now gives each settler a slot on three arcs in front of the building, cleared of the wall at every angle, and the arrival test measures the point the settler actually walks to, so the check and the walk cannot disagree (the failure mode the handover recorded for the home/work pair). (3) **The festival.** Its crowd used `((id % 7) - 3) * 11` × `((Math.floor(id / 7) % 5) - 2) * 9` — **35 repeating slots** around the hall or the performers' camp — so a village stacked tens deep on each one; `crowdGatherPosition` spreads twelve staggered rings of sixteen places instead. **And the ceremony, which the radius cap had made worse:** capping the ring radius without capping attendance meant a thousand settlers shared sixty slots and stood in a *tighter* disc than the wide rings it replaced — attendance is now `CEREMONY_ATTENDEE_LIMIT` (two rings, 24 settlers, the people the vote is about) and everyone else has a day's work to do. **Measured, not asserted:** 200 settlers now occupy ≥150 distinct places in a festival crowd and 40 settlers ≥20 places at a venue, both deterministic. **Also fixed while measuring:** the venue lookup I first wrote scanned every building for every idle settler on every tick — a hundred thousand comparisons per tick in a thousand-settler village on a loop already fighting for 10× — so the village's venue list is gathered once per tick beside the other per-tick lists. **Reported and cleared in the same breath:** *"the time fast forward doesnt work no anymre"* — measured working from both doors in a fresh build (the 10× button crossed a colony day in under 4 s and the `5` hotkey advanced 01:00 → 16:00 in 4 s), and the owner confirmed it resolved; the tick-cost-at-scale measurement I had started for it was stopped and deleted at their word. **Verified:** `humanMovement.test.ts` 5 tests (2 new — venue dispersion and the festival crowd), `tsc` exit 0 on both projects, oxlint 0 warnings / 0 errors on every touched file, full gate **245 files / 1495 passed / 2 skipped / 0 failed**.

- **Changed: the Hunting Spot's hunter walks to the animal, and only takes it at arm's length — half of this is verified working, the other half is not yet** — from the owner's report *"why is it so difficult that hunter goes to the prey"*, the ruling on reach, *"120 px is way to far, its 1800's they dont have guns"*, the reminder *"dont forget animals fight back"*, and finally *"let the animals just dont run anyway"*. **What was there:** the assigned hunter stood at the spot's work point and the shot resolved only if an animal happened to wander inside **120 px** (~10 m) of it — a turret, not a hunter. The only settlers who walked to prey were *job hunters* free-roaming, and only when below 85 % energy, which is why a staffed spot looked inert. **What changed, in the owner's order:** (1) **a move-to-prey order** — `huntingSpotChaseTarget` (new owner module `src/game/huntingSpot.ts`) is applied in `humanTick`'s *work-commute* branch, so the assigned hunter walks at the animal and holds beside it. That branch, not the shift branch above it, is the one a spot's worker actually runs in: measured, a hunter stood frozen at its post (`v=(0,0)`) all day while its quarry walked from 193 px to 209 px. (2) **Kill reach is bodies touching** — `huntingKillReach` = hunter radius + prey radius (≈21 px against a deer, ≈17 px against a rabbit), the same expression `humanHuntingBehavior` uses and the same rule the wolf mauls on. The 120 px constant is deleted, and with it the note that predicted this change: *"the honest fix is the chase"*. (3) **The fight-back is unchanged and now lands on the hunter** — same 35 % wolf roll, same 85 % accuracy roll beside it; only the victim moved from the Hunting Spot's health (an animal the settler was never near, hit from up to 320 px) to the settler standing next to it, since a settler's health *is* its energy. (4) **The hunted animal no longer flees the settler hunting it** — `isWildlifePredator` counts every adult settler, so a grazer inside its `fleeRange` bolts at `speed × 1.5`: a deer at **4.5 px/tick** against a working hunter's ~3.6, which is why a hunter could never close. The exception uses the index that already exists for exactly this pairing (`ctx.huntTargetByPreyId`, prey id → the hunters after it), so wild predators and every *other* settler still spook it. (5) Supporting: a spot **commits** to one animal (`huntingSpotTargetId`) instead of re-picking the nearest each tick (measured: the pick cycled #357 → #431 → #452 in one day and the hunter closed on none), a commitment survives past the eyes' 350 px, the hunter's pursuit is paced (`HUNTING_PURSUIT_SPEED_MULT = 1.4`) and aims where the animal is going (`huntingPursuitPoint`), and a contact is recorded (`huntingSpotInReachTick`) because the production pass samples a single instant. **Verified:** the hunter leaves the post and closes — 0 px → **306 px** from the post, closest approach **65 px → 10 px** once the hunted animal stops fleeing — and the full gate is green (`245 files / 1493 passed / 2 skipped / 0 failed`), with three fixtures updated to stand the deer beside the hunter because a contact reach is the point of the change (`huntingSpot.cleanup`, `foodLedger.acceptedCatch`, `low-9-simdata`). **NOT verified, and stated plainly: a catch still does not land in the measurement** — 162 of 162 spot production passes over twelve colony days decided "too far to shoot", every one of them in the small hours with the hunter back at its post, and the contact record did not change that count. So the *walking* half of this works and the *taking* half does not yet. The reason is now clear and is a design fork, not a bug hunt: a **job hunter's free-roam path already walks to prey and kills at contact distance** (`humanHuntingBehavior`, gated on energy below 85 %), so the spot's separate per-pass sampler duplicates a hunt that already exists — the two should be one, with the spot's work *being* the hunt. That unification is the next step and is the owner's call.

- **Investigated and deliberately not "fixed": a worker slot can overlap a building footprint, and that is not a reachability defect** — the per-worker row introduced above is wider than a small building (eight workers on a 40 px farm span 112 px), so in a dense layout **17 of 33 slots** landed inside *some* building's footprint (measured with `tmp/are-worker-slots-reachable.mts`). - **Fixed: a building's crew stands in a row in front of it instead of clumping into one spot — the workplace stand point was half a footprint off and spread by a 7-value hash** — owner, looking at a Hunting Spot crew: *"why are they all standing at side of the village?"*, then *"but why all at the same palce not logic"*. []` its other save-boundary readers use) and the row is centred on the footprint, so the geometry is deterministic and cannot collide. **Measured, not asserted** (`tmp/where-do-workers-stand.mts`, an 8-strong crew on a 40×40 spot): before — **7 distinct positions of 8**, a **36 px** band, row centre **20 px right** of the footprint centre, y at `h × 0.92`; after — **8 of 8 distinct**, a **112 px** row (`(crew − 1) × WORKER_STAND_SPACING_PX`), centre **exactly on** the footprint centre (Δ 0), standing `WORKER_STAND_STANDOFF_PX` (10 px) south of the bottom edge, i.e. in front rather than beside. **One pinned test recalibrated, not weakened:** `tests/medium-N4-campAnchor.test.ts` pins the barracks guard's position after a tick, and that guard's post moved (one worker: `x + 15 + offset, y + 27.6` → `x, y + 25`), so its velocity differs in the fifth decimal; the case's *invariant* half — the settler average moves inside the tick and the anchor follows the live average — is untouched, and the new numbers carry a comment saying they move only with the destination owner. **The same class of bug is deliberately left in place elsewhere:** `homeStandPosition`'s porch arc and the leisure/venue stand points (`humanLeisureBehavior`, `humanTick`) still use the corner form, because the night-hiding predicate (`isNearResidence`) shares that point and the handover already records a regression from fixing one side of that pair alone (49.5 % → 39.8 % hidden).

- **Fixed: "Civilization Builder" / "Species role" is gone from the citizen card — the block is deleted, not renamed again** — reported from play twice (*"why is she a civliazation builder then"*, then *"why stil civliazian builder"*). Empty, because `ecology.role` is the **species'** trophic role, a constant (`human: { role: 'Civilization Builder' }`), printed on an **individual settler**, so it read as that person's job while saying nothing about them — every citizen, a newborn included, was a Civilization Builder. The whole block (`Species role` / `Eats` / `Hunted`, and the `foodChainInfo` table that existed only to feed it) is now **removed**. `tsc` clean, oxlint 0 warnings, and a repo-wide search confirms the string survives only in the comment recording its removal.

- **Fixed: the family-tree button existed only for settlers who already had relatives, so for most of a young colony it was not there at all** — reported from play as *"it doesnt work when i cick on famliy three"*. The inspector rendered its `Family tree ↗` door behind `tree.parents.length + tree.siblings.length + tree.children.length > 0 || tree.partner != null` (`SelectedEntityPanel.tsx:569`), so a settler with no recorded parent, sibling, child or partner — which is most of a freshly-founded village — showed **no control**, and the feature read as broken rather than empty. Worse, `FamilyTreeWindow` already had an explicit empty state (*"No living relatives recorded"*) that the condition made **unreachable**. The door is now always rendered and labels the case on the right (`no relatives recorded`), so the window says it instead of the button silently vanishing. **Verified in a real browser** through the canonical bot, which now drives the whole chain: roster → `Locate <name> on the map` → inspector → the tree. Result: `opened: "Family tree of Jere" close="Close Family tree of Jere"`, `closed / gone: {"ok":true} / true`, 0 console errors. Two tooling fixes were needed to get there and are worth recording: every earlier selector attempt *guessed* at the DOM and failed, while the working one was read from the source (`PopulationPanel.tsx:57`); and matching the opener by its visible text hit the *collapsible section header* `🌳Family tree▾` instead of the button, so it now matches the button's `title`.

- **Fixed: the "Family tree" was not a family tree — it was an alphabetical surname list, and it was open by default** — reported from play as *"family three is not a family thee"*, and the screenshot confirms it: the Village tab rendered a `defaultOpen` `CollapsibleSection` titled **Family tree** whose contents were families as flat groups — `Musson` with 2 adults, `Mace` with 2 adults, `Wing`, `Andersen`, `Beeler`… one row per surname. It was both **mislabelled** (a list of surnames is not a tree) and the worst instance of the stacking the owner rejected in the same breath (*"i dont want any thing stacked"*), because being open by default it dominated the tab. It is **deleted**, with the reason recorded where it stood so nobody re-adds a list under that name. The real tree (`FamilyTreeWindow`, opened from a selected settler) now **draws** one: parents across the top, the settler with their `⚭` spouse as the anchor row, siblings hanging off the same parents, children below each on its own stem with a connector — so descent is visible rather than inferred from section headings. That is the difference between the earlier "grouped by generation" pass and this one: grouping is still a list of groups, and a tree needs the line.

- **New: `GameWindow`, the one window shell every subject renders into, and the work & venue hours editors move into their own window** — owner ruling: *"each subject should just have its own window not stacking up"*. `components/GameWindow.tsx` is the primitive — title bar, one close control, one scrollable body — and owns **no** subject logic, so adding a window is a small change rather than a new layout. `FamilyTreeWindow` was refactored onto it (it had hand-rolled its own chrome, so the shell did not exist yet), and the three schedule editors that were buried inside `CitizenOverviewScreen` now live in `components/WorkHoursWindow.tsx`, opened from the Village section by an **Edit hours ↗** button. The editors themselves are unchanged owner-owned panels, so the Apply button behaves exactly as before; routing them into the shell is the whole change. **Deliberately not draggable yet**, and stated in the component: the plan lists draggable/resizable as part of the full shell, there is no drag or resize helper anywhere in this repository, and that is new interaction work rather than a refactor — every window is anchored top-right until it lands.

- **Changed: the work and venue schedules have no length restriction — the owner sets how long the colony works and how long the Tavern and Hotel stay open, and a long window is staffed rather than refused** — reported from play as *"i cannot change work times"* and *"i choose how long the tavern should be open"*. The panel was not broken: a 04:00–23:00 (19 h) Tavern was being **refused** by `MAX_VENUE_SERVICE_HOURS = 18`, and the ordinary work window carried a 2–16 h band. Measured on the owner's own screenshot, the status line read *"Blocked: Venue service cannot exceed 18 hours"* with the Apply button correctly disabled — a bound, not a bug. `MAX_VENUE_SERVICE_HOURS` and `MIN_VENUE_SERVICE_HOURS` are deleted, and so are `MIN_STANDARD_WORK_HOURS` (2) and `MAX_STANDARD_WORK_HOURS` (16); the only width limit left is the long-standing **no-wrapping-past-midnight** rule, so 1 hour is the narrowest window and 23 hours (00:00–23:00) the widest. Verified in a real browser, not by reading: the ordinary work panel accepts **00:00–23:00 (23 h)**, enables Apply, commits it, and the current line reads `00:00–23:00 (23h)`; the Tavern accepts **04:00–23:00** and commits it. **The staffing rule is unchanged and is the point of removing the cap** (owner: *"if its goes over 8 hrrs a extra person should go work at the place"*, clarified *"well 9 hrs also fin"*): `getVenueAutoStaffingTarget` keeps `ceil(hours / 9)` bounded by the building's own crew cap, so 1–9 h is one settler, 10–18 h two and 19 h+ three. A venue open all day is covered by more staff, never refused.

- **Fixed: the normal work day is 9 hours, not 8 — a colony working its own standard day was charged an hour of overtime every single day** — `scheduleFatigue.NEUTRAL_WORK_HOURS` was **8** while `workSchedule.STANDARD_PRODUCTION_WORK_HOURS` was **9**, so the game's own two halves disagreed about what a day is: a settler doing a standard 9-hour shift accrued `FATIGUE_PER_EXCESS_HOUR` (10) of fatigue for it and recovered nothing. `getScheduleTargetHours` caps its target at that constant, which is why the whole system shifted at once when it was corrected. The owner's rule is explicit (*"normal work day is 9 hrs"*), and the correction is self-evidencing: with `NEUTRAL_WORK_HOURS = 9` the existing case *"emits one settlement summary for multiple meaningful work-fatigue increases"* — two settlers on a 12-hour shift — now produces **36** (`30 excess − 4 base`), which is exactly the value the test already asserted and which the 8-hour constant was silently defeating. Tests updated with the arithmetic recorded: a neutral nine-hour day recovers `30 − 4 = 26`, one hour of overtime lands on `0 + 10 − 4 = 6`, and a zero-hour day recovers `80 − (4 + 9×3) = 49`.

- **Fixed: production is paid for by settlers who actually turn up — output scaled with the *configured* work window while a settler asleep in bed counted as working** — reported from play as *"is work hours related to production at this moment and i mean the person shoud eb really at the work palce"*, and the report was right on both halves. **(1)** `dailyBuildingEconomy` and `workshopEconomy` both derived their multiplier from `getWorkScheduleHours(getWorkSchedule(state))` — the *configured window*, not work done — so setting a 23-hour day multiplied every building's output by **2.56×** without anyone being at work. **(2)** The only presence test anywhere in the production path was `staffed = workers > 0`, and `workers` came from `buildingWorkerStats`, keyed on `worker.homeBuildingId` — an **assignment**. Nothing read an entity's position, so a farm produced its full `12 + workers × 5` while its worker slept at home. Attendance is now measured and shared: `resolveDailyScheduleFatigue` snapshots the hours each settler actually worked into `scheduleLastWorkedHours` **before** zeroing the tick accumulator (it runs at `tickLayerDaily:42`, ahead of the economy at `:81`, so without the snapshot the only record of attendance was destroyed before production could read it), and one exported rule — `getWorkplacePresenceShare(workedHours, assigned, scheduleHours, hasAttendance)` — is applied by real production **and** by the workshop estimate, so a preview cannot drift from output. A crew that works its whole window scores **1** and keeps exactly the output the window alone used to give; a crew that never turns up produces **0**; an unmeasured world (a fresh load, before the first day boundary) reports 1 rather than 0, because "not yet measured" must not read as "nobody ever works".

- **Changed: a settler leaves early enough to be AT work when the shift starts, instead of starting to walk then** — owner: *"they should be AT work when working tim start not begining with walking"*. The allowance was a fixed `WORK_COMMUTE_LEAD_HOURS = 1` for everyone, which cannot be met by a long leg, and `COMMUTE_SNAP_DISTANCE` only teleports commutes under 200 units — so anyone further out simply arrived late. `simulation/humanMovement.commuteLeadHoursFor(distance, walkSpeed)` now derives each settler's own lead from the movement owner's numbers (base `speed`, `distRush`, `PATH_STEER_SPEED_RATIO`), so it cannot drift from how the walk behaves, and it is clamped to **1–6 hours**: the floor makes a short commute bit-identical to before, and the ceiling stops a settler being scheduled to leave the night before. **It is an estimate with a floor and a ceiling, not an arrival guarantee** — past `MAX_COMMUTE_LEAD_HOURS` a very long leg cannot be walked in the time available, and the existing shift-start snap is what still puts that settler at their post. Both facts are recorded in the function's doc because the first version of this conflated them.

- **Fixed: people get into and out of prison visibly — an imprisonment is its own event type with its own Chronicle filter** — reported from play as *"i cant seee if people get in prison its not happneing or its not logged and not shown"*. `arrestForScandal` logged `'event'`, so imprisonments landed in the generic Events bucket (1 531 of the owner's 2 000 Chronicle entries) with nothing to group them. There is now a real `'prison'` type on `GameEventLog`, a **Imprisonments (jailed for scandal)** filter, a ⛓️ icon and colour, and — the part that made it invisible even when logged — a `Jails` count in the daily report, which previously showed neither scandals-adjacent number. **The tempting one-line fix (`'event'` → `'scandal'`) was deliberately not taken:** `rumourLedger.SOURCE_KINDS` reads `type === 'scandal'`, so it would have pulled imprisonments into the rumour ledger and inflated the scandal count. The compiler found two more sites by itself — `EventLogPanel`'s `Record<GameEventLog['type'], string>` maps for icons and colours errored immediately, naming the missing member. **A stale test was the reason this class of defect could hide:** `tests/eventLogPanel.filters.test.ts` hand-typed its list of event types and had already fallen behind (`conception` and `divorce` were absent), so the test guarding "every event type has a filter" could not have caught a missing one. It is now keyed as a `Record<GameEventLog['type'], true>`, which turns a new event type into a **compile error** there rather than a silent gap.

- **Fixed: the dev server no longer full-reloads the page from Rust build output, so the game can actually be opened and driven in a browser** — `vite.config.ts` watched everything under the project root, including `src-tauri/target/doc/**`, which `cargo doc` fills with **thousands** of generated HTML files; Vite issued a full-page reload per file, so the page never settled. This is why the first browser run of the session could not reach the game at all. `server.watch.ignored` now excludes `**/src-tauri/target/**`. Nothing under `target/` is ever imported by the app.

- **New: a separate testing bot that raises the whole building catalogue — and it proves a settler is physically at the church, not merely assigned to it** — owner: *"give the bot just options to build all things so cheating but fo rtesting"* and *"keep virtual-player.ts apart thats for in game and use own for testing"*. `scripts/test-bot-build-all.mts` drives the real command boundary and **closes a recorded coverage gap** (`BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-29-automated-runs-never-build-a-church.md`): no automated run in this repository had ever reached `churchStrength > 0`. Result: **21 of 34 buildable types raised, the world still simulates, and the church is live at strength 1.** Two traps were found by running it, both recorded in the script: **(1)** `buildDebug` places a *construction site*, and `getChurchStrength` is gated on `b.completed` — so the first run reported "church built: true, churchStrength: 0"; **(2)** `countWorkersAtBuilding` counts `homeBuildingId`, i.e. an **assignment, not an arrival** (the same assignment-vs-attendance distinction as the production fix above), so the bot now ticks until the keeper is **physically at the church** before it claims success — owner: *"no it need people workin there"*. Two opt-in primitives support it and are unreachable from any shipping path: a `buildDebug` worker command and `startBuilding(..., free)`, which skip **research and cost only** — terrain, overlap and one-per-village placement are still enforced by their owner, because two buildings in one footprint would corrupt the spatial indices rather than merely cheat. `scripts/autoplay-browser-probe.mjs` also gains a reusable `CAPABILITIES` registry, so the UI-driving bot gains named handles onto the game's screens instead of each test re-deriving selectors.

- **Investigated and deliberately reverted: the in-game bot does not build a Church, and the reason is its own pinned ordering** — an attempt to extend the shipping auto-play ladder (owner: *"do extend its building options so let it build a church and other things"*) failed **twice**, at the civic step (**36 of 68** cases in `tests/virtualPlayer.test.ts`) and at the end of the ladder (**15 of 68**), and pre-placing a church in the shared fixture made it *worse* (**18**) because the Church is itself a manual-staff workplace. The cause is not a bug: those 68 cases pin the exact decision sequence and treat `null` ("the colony needs nothing") as a reachable healthy state, and any step that fires in a small, well-stocked fixture contradicts 15 terminal expectations. Extending the in-game repertoire is therefore a redesign of the bot's identity — **does a healthy colony keep building?** — and that is an owner decision, not an edit. The ladder is left exactly as it was and `virtualPlayer.test.ts` is green at 68/68; the church coverage gap is closed by the separate testing bot above instead.

**Verification for this entry:** `npm run test:all` green at **245 files / 1493 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failed**, with **0 lint warnings and 0 errors** over 350 files, and `tsc` clean on both the app and test projects. The two schedule panels and the new window were driven in a real browser (the repo has no DOM test tier): ordinary work accepted and committed `00:00–23:00`, the Tavern accepted `04:00–23:00`, the Hotel `06:00–21:00`, the work-hours window opens and closes from its button, and the console was quiet throughout (`0 console errors`). The canonical browser bot still passes on the live game after the UI change (9 acts, farm before research, status line visible). **Not verified, stated plainly:** the walk-speed and `TICKS_PER_HOUR` rebase discussed in this session was **not** implemented — measured, the map is one settlement site (~102 m across at 11.76 px/m), a crossing currently costs **23 ticks / 7.7 in-game hours**, and the owner's chosen target (one day to cross at `TICKS_PER_HOUR = 5`) requires walk speed ≈ **14 px/tick with `distRush` deleted**, which rebases every duration in the game and is left as its own reviewed change. Footpaths, the night-hiding residual (~38 % still drawn), and the browser pass over the family-tree window and settler map rings also remain open.

- **Fixed: the People screen reports the year's affairs instead of an instant that averages zero — 93 affairs a year were reading as "0"** — reported from play as *"in game there are zero affairs with 400 citizins"*. The engine gate says otherwise: `npm run test:full-year` (seed 12345, 360 days, ~70 settlers) establishes **93** affairs, catches **62** in the act and jails **9** for it, with **12** gossip exposures. What was wrong is the number the player reads. The Life card's `affairs` slot counted settlers holding `affairPartnerId` **at that instant**, and an affair is deliberately short-lived: measured on the same gate, the instantaneous count averages **0.2 per day** and is non-zero on only **35 of 360** days of a year with 93 establishments — so the card read zero on ~90 % of days, including every day the owner happened to look, and the feature looked absent while working. The card now reads `${married} married · ${N} sweethearts · ${N} affairs this year · ${N} jailed`, where the year count comes from the event log the Chronicle already reads, filtered to the current colony year. The establishment phrase is now **one exported constant on its owner** (`AFFAIR_ESTABLISHED_LOG_PHRASE`, `humanRelationships.ts`) used by both the writer and this reader — a second copy of the string would have made the count silently read 0 forever if the two ever drifted. **A correction to my own earlier report, recorded because it reversed the conclusion:** I first measured this with a hand-built 400-citizen probe that pushed settlers straight into `world.entities`, bypassing the registration the immigration path performs. That fixture reported "0 live affairs, 0 exposures, every pair cleared as NOT MUTUAL" and I reported the drama chain as still dead — none of it was the game. The same measurement on a world built only by `prepareColonyWorld` + `gameTick` shows 38 establishments, 72 exposures, 33 catches in a year, and **zero** non-mutual clears. The engine gate is the authority, not an improvised probe. - **Fixed: an affair can be established again — exposure fired at 45 % while establishment needs 100, and a rumour *reset* the progress it interrupted, so in a village with a church no affair ever completed and the entire drama chain downstream of establishment was dead** — reported from play as *"things are not balanced and some are totally missing like affairs"*, and the owner's own save is the proof rather than a model of it: New Frontier (v0.6.5.0, Y0 D198–D279) logged **145 "Whispers spread" and 0 "began a secret affair"**, and with it **0 "was caught with"**, **0 "imprisoned for scandal"** and **0 feuds**. **The cause is an ordering inversion, not a tuning value.** `tryDailyAffairEncounter` establishes a pair only when *both* partners reach `AFFAIR_PROGRESS_MAX` (100) — the bump is 16–27 per tryst at a 0.07/day roll — and `tryDailyAffairGossip` exposed a pair once both passed **45** (church) or **85** (no church). Because `exposeAffair` calls `clearAffairPair`, which sets *both* partners' `affairProgress` back to **0**, a rumour did not merely embarrass a pair mid-climb: it undid the climb. Exposure almost always won, and every gate below it keys off `hasAffairPartner` — which only establishment sets. So `tryExposeCaughtAffairForPair` (both call sites), `arrestForScandal`, the "imprisoned for scandal" line, `tryDivorceOnCaughtCheater(…, caughtInAct = true)` and the **only `startFeud` caller in the game** were all unreachable, and had been since the 2026-09 rebalance that halved the tryst chance (0.14 → 0.07, 0.20 → 0.10). **The fix restores the rule the code already claimed to follow.** `gameConstants.ts` says *"only establishment can produce a scandal"* and `tests/affair.cadence.test.ts` says *"exposure requires an established affair"* — both were false. `tryDailyAffairGossip` now requires `hasAffairPartner` before it rolls at all, and `findAffairLover` resolves only an established mutual lover (its 45-progress scan is deleted with the floors it served). The church branch keeps its own roll and salt (`602`, 0.12/0.22; no-church `601`, 0.06), so seeding a run does not move which days already gossiped; the church still suppresses affairs through `AFFAIR_CHURCH_FLOOR_FACTOR`, which scales the daily tryst chance — what it no longer does is make the drama impossible. **Measured by A/B on the real engine** — the same seeded colony, the same 360 days of shipped `gameTick`, with a completed staffed church added as the only difference between the two columns, and the whole run repeated against the pre-fix source:

| | before, no church | before, **church** | after, no church | after, **church** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| population | 103 | 144 | 130 | 104 |
| `affairsEstablished` | 50 | **17** | 130 | **81** |
