| forced-divorce lines | 71 | 16 | 190 | 82 |
| **rumours per establishment** | 5.82 | **24.35** | 0.08 | **0.26** |

**Why this run is housed and the first one was not.** The first A/B used the shipped `prepareColonyWorld`, which puts 8 houses under 21–70 people, so most settlers were homeless — and a homeless settler passes the encounter's gates *for the wrong reason*, because `isAtMaritalHome` is false when you have no home. **Housing is the variable that decides this bug**, so the numbers above place **60 completed houses** first: the same seed, the same 360 days, measured on `gameTick` with the whole drama chain counted. The church column is the owner's configuration and the difference is 24.35 → **0.26** rumours per establishment, with establishments **17 → 81**, caught **8 → 49**, imprisoned **6 → 35** and feuds **8 → 44**.

**Three measurement errors in my own probes on the way here, all recorded because each produced a false reading:** (1) the first harvested only `type === 'scandal'`, while `"X was imprisoned for scandal"` is logged as `'event'` (`arrestForScandal`); (2) its "staff the church" step took the first adult founder, who is exactly the starter **Prison Guard** `prepareColonyWorld` assigns, so the church arm had no guard and `arrestForScandal` could never fire; (3) it was under-housed, which is the whole regime. And the conclusion the owner's own question forced out: **the church never blocked imprisonment — nothing was ever caught, because nothing ever established.**

**The second half, and the one that actually reaches a housed village — the encounter was evaluated at midnight for the whole settlement at once.** With the gossip gate fixed the owner would still have seen nothing, and asking *"why not just run it more often"* is what surfaced it. `tryDailyAffairEncounter` is the **only** writer of `affairPartnerId` (`tests/affair.cadence.test.ts`: *"the daily owner is the sole establisher"*), and it ran on the global `isNewCalendarDayTick` gate — `getTickOfDay(tick) === 0`, i.e. **00:00 for everyone**. At midnight it cannot pass its own gates: `canPursueSecretAffair` returns false on `isSpouseNearby` (22 px, `humanRelationships.ts`), which is true for a housed couple asleep in the same room, and `isAtMaritalHome` is true besides. So in a well-housed village the one daily sample was taken at the one hour it was guaranteed to fail — which is exactly the owner's objection, *"all affairs at 00:00 is not logical"* / *"people sleep between 23:00 and 06:00"*. **The fix: stagger it.** Each settler's encounter is now evaluated at their own hour across the **waking day** (`affairEncounterHourOfDay(entityId)`, 06:00–19:00 derived from the owner's existing `NIGHT_END`/`NIGHT_START`), once per settler per day. **It costs nothing in probability, which is why staggering is the whole fix:** `personDayRoll` (`humanSchedule.ts`) hashes `(entity, colony day, salt)` and **not** the tick-of-day, so a settler evaluated once a day at their own hour gets *exactly the same single roll at exactly the same chance* — only the hour the conditions are sampled at changes. No latch, no new save field, and `decisionRegistry` already declares this cadence as **"staggered/daily"**: the implementation was the part that was not staggered. (The alternative the owner offered — have cheaters skip sleep and sneak out at night — would work too, but it means changing the sleep/idle schedule, a far larger surface than a call site.)

The church column is the owner's situation, and it is the inversion in one line: before, a housed church village produced **24.35 rumours for every affair that managed to complete**; after, **0.26**. **Verification:** the A/B above is the substantive evidence — it is the shipped `gameTick`, and the "before" arm is produced by restoring `humanRelationships.ts` from HEAD, so the difference is the change and nothing else. `tsc` app+test projects clean; `npm run lint` **0 warnings / 0 errors**; **full standard suite 243 files / 1479 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failed**; `tests/fullYear.integration.test.ts` green (360 days / 25,920 ticks, no invariant violations). **Not verified, and deliberately not faked:** there is no automated regression test for this. Two attempts were made and both were deleted rather than shipped — a `tryDailyAffairGossip` fixture test (it hand-set `affairProgress = 95`, i.e. exactly the manufactured precondition this entry criticises) and a bot-played integration test (it hand-placed the world and took **214 s** for one assertion, far past the standard tier, and it failed because the bot never builds a church). **Also removed, at the owner's instruction:** `tests/affairCaught.reachability.test.ts`, whose header claimed it was *"the reachability regression guard"* for this exact chain while its fixtures **hand-set `affairPartnerId` and `affairProgress = 60`** — it assumed the establishment that was unreachable, so it stayed green for as long as no affair could establish. It was **untracked** (`tests/**` is gitignored), so a verbatim copy is preserved at `tmp/deleted-tests/affairCaught.reachability.test.ts` for recovery. **A Church was briefly added to the virtual player's civic build ladder and reverted:** because step 6 runs before roads, militia, diplomacy and refugee screening, a church the colony can afford (45 wood / 35 stone / 20 gold) shadowed every later decision and broke **36 of the 68** cases in `tests/virtualPlayer.test.ts`. The coverage gap it was meant to close is real and stays recorded instead — see below. **The blindness that let this ship is recorded separately** (`BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-29-automated-runs-never-build-a-church.md`): the only year-long gate, `scripts/run-full-year.mts`, never builds anything — **18 buildings at day 30 and still 18 at day 360**, because `prepareColonyWorld` places a fixed completed skeleton (8 Houses + Tavern + Prison + 8 storage) and the harness never issues a construction command. It therefore runs at `churchStrength` **0** forever, and the in-repo bot never builds one either (`virtualPlayer.ts`: *"(Church, Prison, Barracks, School, Town Hall) — those stay the player's call"*; `CIVIC_BUILD_ORDER` is TownHall + Blacksmith). A church is precisely what selects the branch that killed establishment, so no automated run in this repository could have seen it.

- **Fixed: the Watchtower never draws rotated, and is no longer offered a rotation — a 90° turn laid the tower on its side** — reported from play as *"the watchtower cannot be rotated"*, and the accurate version is that it *was* rotated, wrongly. `BuildingRotation` is `0 | 90` and a 90° turn is applied by **rotating the sprite** (`spriteDrawing.drawSpriteFrame` → `ctx.rotate(Math.PI / 2)`), which is the correct meaning for a flat strip — the run axis, horizontal versus vertical — and the wrong one for a building drawn with height: a Watchtower at 90 was drawn tipped into the ground. One type was doing two unrelated jobs. Three changes, one rule. **(1)** `Watchtower` leaves `ROTATABLE`, so the rotate key does nothing for it. **(2)** A new owner function, `buildingRotation.effectiveBuildingRotation(type, rotation)`, returns the stored rotation only for types the player may rotate and **0 otherwise** — which is why this is not just a menu change: selecting a build type resets `buildRotation` to 0 (`App.tsx`), so the only remaining source of a rotated tower is an **older save**, and that save must not render one into the ground either. **(3)** Both draw sites (the completed pass and the construction pass) now read that one function, so they cannot disagree, and the `flipX` argument that used to mirror the already-sideways tower is **deleted** with the rotation it compensated for. `Bridge` is still in the rotatable set and is not a strip type either, so it has the same defect; left untouched because it was not part of this request. **A pre-existing test asserted the opposite** — `tests/watchtowerDetection.test.ts` had *"Watchtower is rotatable with the R key"* expecting `true` — so that case was **rewritten, not deleted**, to pin the reversed ruling with the reason, rather than leaving two definitions of the same rule. Verification: `tests/watchtowerDetection.test.ts` (4) passes, and the rotation-adjacent files `visualsAudit.renderWiring` / `buildingFootprint.convention` / `footprintBounds.owner` pass (35 across the four). `tsc` app+test projects clean, `npm run lint` 0 warnings / 0 errors, full standard suite green (243 files / 1,486 passed, 2 skipped).

- **Fixed: a wall run is a straight wall again — the diagonal isometric face was being tiled along an axis it cannot fill** — reported from play as *"you dont build walls like this"*, with a wall rendering as a sawtooth of offset slabs. The cause is projection, and it is provable rather than a matter of taste: the world→screen transform is a plain scale+translate with **no isometric skew** (`viewState.worldToScreen`: `screenX = (x − cam.x) × zoom + cw/2`, same for y), so a horizontal run is a horizontal screen line; `wall_isometric.png` is a **45° face**; and `drawProceduralWall` drew that one asset **once per piece** at rotation 0 for every piece in the run (`drawIsometricDefenseAsset`, which also forced `imageSmoothingEnabled = false` and a square `max(w,h) × 1.18` box with no footprint anchor). Every piece therefore drew the same diagonal band at the same rotation, offset by exactly the strip pitch — a periodic sawtooth by construction, not by tuning. Walls now draw through the palisade path that was already written as the asset's fallback, and which was always axis-correct: `beginRotatedStripFrame` rotates the **frame**, not the art, and its width is `max(w,h)` = 60 = the strip pitch, so consecutive pieces abut exactly and the posts rise off the footprint (height preserved — a flat top-down wall would be indistinguishable from `road.png`). **No corner pieces are involved:** a corner is an H run and a V run overlapping, and the wall tool never had a corner type — `drawProceduralWallCorner` / `drawProceduralWallJunction` are exported but have **no caller** (left untouched as pre-existing dead code and reported here). The two isometric PNGs stay as the build-menu icons in `BUILDING_CONFIGS`; only the map drawing changed. Verification: `tsc` app+test projects clean, `npm run lint` 0 warnings / 0 errors, full standard suite green (243 files / 1,485 passed, 2 skipped). **Not verified by me:** the rendered pixels — the test runner has no canvas, so this is a geometry proof plus a green suite, and the visual result needs a human look.

- **New: sweethearts are finally visible — youth love gets a panel line and a People-screen counter, and an affair is no longer invisible while it is still building** — reported from play as *"i played now one year there are zero affairs and zero youth love — is youth love registered?"*. It is registered, and that was the problem: `advanceYouthLove` (`simulation/humanRelationships.ts`, daily from `tickLayerDaily`) writes `youthLovePartnerId` / `youthLoveProgress`, carries them through the save, `simDelta`'s catalog keys and the invariant collector, and logs *"X and Y became sweethearts"* — but **no `.tsx` file in the tree referenced `youthLove` at all**. No panel line, no counter, no map badge: the only in-game trace was one generic 🌝 log entry, while affairs get a notification banner. So the People screen's `0 sweethearts` was not a reading, it was the absence of one. **Now:** `computeCitizenOverview` gains a `youthLove` count (one half only, the `id < partnerId` convention `affairs` and `activeYouthLovePairs` use), the Life card reads `N married · N sweethearts · N affairs · N jailed`, and clicking a youth shows `💗 Sweethearts with <name> (N%)`. **And the same invisibility had a second case, reported as *"what about affair"*:** `affairProgress` accumulates over several trysts (16–27 each) *before* `affairPartnerId` is set at 100, and the panel only read the established id — so a settler at 90 % of an affair displayed nothing. It now reads `💋 Secret affair brewing (N%)` until the pair is established, then the existing named line takes over. **Why the counts read zero is a separate question this does not answer:** the People screen counts only affairs that are established *at this instant*, and exposure is the mechanic's whole point (divorce, arrest, a 21-day scandal cooldown), so `0 affairs` is a normal snapshot on a save where affairs have happened — the permanent record is the Log/Chro…8383 tokens truncated…d rather than left false:** `AGENTS.md` §4 and `OWNERSHIP_OVERVIEW.md` no longer name a `USE_PIXI_GROUND` that does not exist and drop the `Pixi terrain paint` owner row — the same "documented decision with no code behind it" defect the audit raised twice — and the roadmap's Theme 1 rule 3 is closed as enforced by the tree. Historical records were **deliberately not rewritten**: `CHANGELOG.md`'s own older entries, the three `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-17-pixi-*` files and the dated audits under `docs/private/` keep their text, because a release note that gets edited whenever the code moves stops being a record of what shipped. Gates after the removal: `npm run test:all` **240 files / 1475 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failed** (three fewer cases than the 1478 before it, all of them guards on the deleted module), `tsc` clean on both projects, `npm run lint` 0 warnings / 0 errors, `npm run build` exit 0.

- **New: the ground detail is band-limited to the frequencies the eye actually reads roughness in — stone has bedding, snow has drifts, and the sub-pixel grain that carried neither is trimmed** — the material detail in the ground bake was a stack of `valueNoise` octaves chosen by eye, and **every one of stone's and dirt's octaves sat below the band the visual system reads surface roughness in**. Padilla, *Mathematical models for perceived roughness of three-dimensional surface textures* (PhD thesis, Heriot-Watt 2008; *Perceived roughness of 1/f^β noise surfaces*, Vision Research 48), fits a band-pass over the psychophysics of 1/f^β noise surfaces and finds perceived roughness is carried by a **narrow band**: the optimised box filter lands on **1.53–4.58 cycles per degree** (§7.3.1), and detail outside it barely moves perceived roughness. On the display calibrated for those experiments (0.255 mm pixel pitch at 87.7 cm, i.e. 60 px per degree) the band is **≈13–39 px per cycle**. Measured against the old mix, that made a concrete, visible defect: stone's fracture octaves sat at **0.6–4.5 px** periods and dirt's at **0.8–2.9 px**, so a 1:1 crop of a rocky hillside was a **uniform grey speckle with no bedding in it at all** and a snowfield was flat white — the only two materials with anything inside the band were grass (a 21 px clump) and sand (a 33 px dune). The bake now names the band (`ROUGHNESS_BAND_PX`) and the octave each material carries inside it (`ROUGHNESS_OCTAVE_PX`) — stone a bedding ledge plus rubble, dirt a patch, snow a wind-worked drift, grass a tussock, sand a wind ripple — and authors those octaves through `bandDetail(wx, wy, seed, periodPx, pxStep)`, whose period is fixed in **canvas** px so a coarse chunk bake re-samples the same structure instead of pushing it out of the band (a world-fixed octave halves its period when the world step doubles; this one does not). The sub-pixel octaves are **trimmed rather than deleted**, because they still carry material identity at close zoom: dirt's grain 26 → 18 and its 0.8 px term 10 → 5, stone's grain 20 → 8, grass's 10 → 5, sand's ripple 7 → 5, snow's sparkle 16 → 12. **Measured** (1600×1200, seed 12345, spring, whole-map 1:1, luma band-passed to 13–39 px per cycle — the bake's own perceived roughness): **rock 5.45 → 6.01** and hills 4.34 → 4.61 on highland, **rock 4.93 → 5.50** on continental, while grass barely moved (4.06 → 4.09 and 3.25 → 3.29) — the rough materials gained inside the band and the smooth ones did not. Whole-map bake cost **1.28 → 1.44 s** / **1.35 → 1.49 s** for one extra noise octave per land pixel, so a 256 px chunk on the shipping chunked path goes ~27 ms → ~30 ms. One case in `tests/groundLook.bands.test.ts` pins both halves of the rule: every material owns at least one octave inside the band, and `bandDetail` returns the same value when the world step doubles.

- **New: the rain shadow — a mountain range finally has a wet side and a dry side** — moisture was a noise field plus a **symmetric** altitude penalty, so both flanks of a range dried by the same amount: a range had no windward side, no leeward side, and dry ground could only come from the noise itself (which is also why a map's arid ground tended to sit on its coast rather than behind its hills). `terrain/rainShadow.ts` adds the **directional** half of the climate: for every cell it marches **upwind** — a west-south-westerly heading, deliberately not axis-aligned, so a shadow does not read as vertical banding — and records the tallest ground standing between it and the wind, capped so a mountain counts as a wall rather than as an infinite one, and decaying over 48 cells so a leeward plain recovers as it runs on. The result is a 0–1 dryness the moisture pass subtracts before the biome cuts are taken, which is the right order for a reason worth stating: those cuts are **quantiles of the finished field**, so a map keeps its wet/dry *proportions* while their *placement* becomes topographic — the same valley is wooded on the windward side and open on the lee. **Measured** (2560×1920, seed 12345): at matched mid-altitude, shaded ground reads **0.12–0.18** moisture against **0.32–0.36** in the open (arabia 0.320 → 0.122, continental 0.357 → 0.180, black_forest 0.698 → 0.448), with a correlation of **−0.49…−0.65** between the barrier and the moisture on every preset; eight cells downwind of the tallest ridges the ground is drier than the windward cell (coastal 0.303 → 0.224, islands 0.418 → 0.296, scandinavia 0.375 → 0.315). The field is pure — elevation in, dryness out, no RNG and no dependence on iteration order — and each preset's climate stays itself (arabia 68 % dry / 17 % wet, oasis 84 / 2, black_forest 1 / 61, meadows 12 / 63). Two cases in `tests/terragen.latitude.test.ts` guard it: the rule on a synthetic wall (lee shaded, windward clear, decay with distance, level ground unshaded, byte-identical on re-run) and the effect through the generator's own moisture field at matched elevation, where the gap a rain shadow exists to make is **0.213** against a bar of 0.08.

- **New: biome placement follows latitude — a map's north and south edges are cold and its middle row is the equator, from the parameterization in Choroś & Topolski's extended-Voronoi terrain paper** — the generator chose every biome from a per-map climate constant, so `arabia` was hot *everywhere* and `scandinavia` was cold *everywhere*: the same climate in every direction, which is why a generated valley read as a scatter of biome patches rather than as terrain with a north and a south. Choroś & Topolski (*Parameterized and Dynamic Generation of an Infinite Virtual Terrain with Various Biomes using Extended Voronoi Diagram*, J.UCS 22:6, 2016) parameterize a biome by **position** — a latitude axis — rather than by a constant, and that single idea is what ported. **The map's y axis is now its latitude:** `latitudeCooling` in `terrain/terragen.ts` is a **triangular wave** over the cell row — 0 on the middle row (the equator), maximal at both edges (the poles) — so the cool zones are symmetric about the middle instead of biased to one end. **It is additive, and that is why the presets had to be re-based:** latitude only removes heat, and it is subtracted *before* the long-standing `× 1.1` amplifier, so the equator row reads exactly that preset's authored `temperature` and every other row is colder. That makes `temperature` **the equator's air temperature** — and a preset only has zonation at all if it clears `COLD_TEMPERATURE` (0.3) *after* the elevation penalty (0.7 per unit above sea level), because otherwise `classifyTile` calls the whole map cold. Measured, `scandinavia` at its old 0.18 cleared that on **no row at all**: 0 % grass, 0 % forest, 29–41 % dark forest, and latitude changed nothing. The cool presets are therefore re-based so their equator is genuinely temperate — `scandinavia` **0.18 → 0.56**, `highland` **0.40 → 0.62**, and smaller lifts on the rest (`arabia` 0.75 → 0.82, `black_forest` 0.52 → 0.66, `coastal` 0.62 → 0.70, `islands` 0.78 → 0.80, `meadows` 0.60 → 0.68, `oasis` 0.90 → 0.92, `rivers` 0.55 → 0.64, `continental` 0.65 → 0.70), with the rule recorded beside the table in `PRESET_SETTINGS`. **That re-basing is where the zonation comes from, and it is measured:** `scandinavia` equator band **0.08 → 0.46** against 0.23 top / 0.38 bottom, warm land **0 % → 34 %** with cold land 63 % → **29 %**; `highland` 0.22 → 0.45 and warm land **7 % → 33 %**; `arabia` 0.49 → 0.57 at 54 % warm; `continental` 0.53 → 0.59 at 43 % warm. `scandinavia` stays the coldest preset by a clear margin — it is boreal through its **latitude bands and its moisture**, not by being frozen on every row. **The band edge is drifted by coherent noise** (`hash2`, not a value-noise lattice, so the offset brings no lattice of its own) because a raw triangular wave draws perfectly straight horizontal climate lines — the artefact the paper adds detail to avoid; the noise is seeded, so two seeds do not share one set of climate lines and one seed still reproduces its own exactly. **One source of truth with two readers:** the parameter is applied *inside* the `temperature` field rather than at the classifier, so `terrainGrid.classifyTile` (the tile a settler walks on) and the per-pixel ground bake both get the zonation and cannot disagree about it — `WorldMap.temperature` documents the axis. `LATITUDE_SPAN` is **0.3**, deliberately modest: at 0.55 the polar rows land on the `clamp` floor, where the gradient is lost and a whole edge reads as one flat band (measured, `continental` warm-biome share 6 % at 0.55 against 11 % at 0.3, with 9 % of cells pinned). **No limit is left papered over:** the re-base is what opens the zonation, and it is a deliberate change to what these presets mean — `scandinavia` is now boreal through its latitude bands and its moisture rather than by being frozen on every row, which is the intended read but is a tuning decision the owner should know was taken. Measured across two seeds the re-based presets hold their character: `scandinavia` 34 % warm / 29 % cold land at seed 12345 and 56 % / 20 % at seed 777, `highland` 33 % / 17 % and 67 % / 11 %, against `oasis` and `arabia` staying hot (53–75 % warm, 15–17 % cold). The paper's infinite-terrain machinery — its wrapping `mod(2S)` zone cycle, quad LOD layers and spiral draw order — is deliberately **not** ported: Wilderfolk's map is a finite `width`×`height` rectangle, so there is no "last zone" to wrap from. Four cases in `tests/terragen.latitude.test.ts` pin the gradient, its symmetry, its non-saturation, its seed dependence and determinism. Gates: `npm run test:all` **240 files / 1475 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failed**, `tsc` clean on both projects, `npm run lint` 0 warnings / 0 errors, `npm run build` exit 0.

- **Developer: the browser smoke tier is rewritten from scratch on Playwright, and it gates green again** — the old `scripts/browser-smoke.mjs` was a *partial reconstruction* (its own header recorded 1,562 of 2,696 lines re-mined after the 2026-09-20 tree revert, with `VIEWPORT_MATRIX`, `runViewportMatrix`, `runPanelPass` and `runSettlerWalkThrough` present but **uncalled** and the acceptance checklist unrecoverable), and it had been failing on its own flow rather than on the game: it clicked the intro at ~1.2 s, when the intro is a **timed cinematic whose call-to-action is not yet enabled**, and it treated "found a button" as "clicked a button" — so *HUD never appeared*, *no `#camera-zoom-preset` control* and *the simulation is not ticking* were all cascades of that one step (the identical three failures are in `tmp/shots/report.json` dated 21-9, three days before this session). The rewrite drives the build through the real player path — boot → the intro's first **enabled** CTA → settlement screen → `Settle the valley` (including the armed-confirmation branch a save triggers) → gameplay HUD → tutorial card → live valley — and proves each step from the page: the ground actually painted (distinct colours, mean luma, void ratio sampled from the map canvas), the sim actually ticking (the HUD clock, with a 25 s window because a soft-rasterising browser is slow), the zoom control exercised through `#camera-zoom-preset` with its live readout checked, and the console/page/request gates. Two measurements shaped it. **(1) The pause is not the pace.** The guided first-spring overlay freezes the world on purpose, and the HUD button's `aria-label` flips to "running" while the simulation worker still reports `Paused` — a Space press (the shipped shortcut) is what actually resumes it. **(2) The default viewport is 900×600, not 1600×900.** A GPU-less headless browser soft-rasterises every canvas path, and a CPU profile of one valley frame put **92 % of main-thread self time in native `fill`/`stroke`/`restore`** with individual frames of 5–6 s; at that size the tick counter did not advance inside 21 s even with the world resumed, while at 900×600 the same build ticks normally (tick 24 → 27, clock 08:00 → 09:00 in 21 s). Per-frame performance stays another tier's job. **Verified: three consecutive runs, `exit=0`, `verdict=pass`**, and `tmp/shots/report.json` now records the check table. Dependency: **`playwright-core`** (a devDependency) drives the Chrome already on the machine — no bundled browser download. What the rewrite deliberately drops, because it was uncalled or unrecoverable, is listed in the script header and in `docs/plans/browser-testing-evaluation.md`.

- **New: rivers are generated from hydrology — the SIGGRAPH 2013 model — so a river follows a real drainage network and widens with the land that feeds it** — the generator used to pick river sources by an elevation score and then walk a *gradient* with a meander bend, which meant a "river" was a path and not a network: nothing knew how much land drained through a point, two paths could cross, the walk needed a stagnation bail-out for when the gradient flattened on a depression, the channel's width was a function of how far along the path it had travelled (`step / 200`), and every river cell was clamped to `seaLevel - 0.01` with a `seaLevel + 0.04` ring around it — **a mountain stream was cut down to sea level, a canyon through a hillside, and that plate plus its ring is what made every river edge a terrace on the 16 px lattice**. The port is Génevaux et al., *Terrain Generation Using Procedural Models Based on Hydrology*, and it keeps the paper's own chain: **route the water, then read the river off the area that drains through it.** A new owner module, `terrain/hydrology.ts`, does three passes — **fill sinks** by priority flood (a binary heap, ties broken by cell index, so the result is bit-identical run to run), **D8 steepest descent plus flat resolution**, and **accumulation** in one sorted sweep. Sources are now high ground where *little land drains through* (the paper's springs, not its river mouths), paths follow the network downhill so two rivers meet where their catchments meet instead of crossing, and the channel's width comes from the paper's mean-flow law `φ = 0.42·A^0.69` (§5.1, after Dunne & Leopold) through the standard hydraulic-geometry width law `w ∝ φ^0.5`, so `w ∝ A^0.345` — a headwater is 0.3 of a preset's channel width and the map's trunk river 2.4 of it, with the map's own largest catchment as the normaliser so every preset keeps the width it asks for. The carve itself is the paper's river primitive, `h(p) = u_z(p) + δ(d(p))`: the channel is cut below **its own** surface (depth scales with the channel's width, not with sea level) and the profile meets the banks through the replace operator's compact-support weight instead of stopping at a ring. **Two defects were found by measuring while building it, and both are now pinned by tests.** The first: with the fill alone, **14–27 % of cells had no lower neighbour and the longest path from any cell was ~30 cells** — the network was in fragments and every river drawn on it was a stub, because filling a pit turns it into a *flat* and no flat cell has a strictly-lower neighbour; the fix is the flat-resolution sweep, and the "inverted uphill guard" that came with it closed a cycle that made **every walk traverse the whole grid** (measured: 19 200 steps) until the guard was corrected. The second: using the paper's crest term `q_z = max(a_z, b_z, c_z) + λ·d` as an *upper bound* on ground that already exists cuts a bench as wide as the bank run into any slope a river crosses — it swallowed ridgelines, and it starved the tiles one farm fixture depends on; the banks now give up a **bounded** share of the channel depth and fade out, so the hill's own relief is the valley wall. **Measured after:** interior dead ends **14–27 % → 0**, longest drainage walk **~30 → 132–205 cells**, catchment p50 3 with a trunk of **6 190–12 517 cells** against a mean of ~4 (that tail is what makes width-from-area meaningful), rivers per preset unchanged (1–5, and `oasis` still zero by design), generation **+12–158 ms** for the two passes on a 2560×1920 map, and the same seed still produces the identical world (`workerMainThread.parity` and the determinism case in `terragen.riverGeneration.test.ts` both pass). Six cases now guard it — network connectivity and acyclicity, accumulation downstream, and the width law's monotonicity and clamp — and the ground bake keeps its 3×3-smoothed height field, which was added for the old plates and is still worth having for a steep channel. One threshold moved on evidence: the alpine-lawn seam in `groundLook.bands.test.ts` reads **0.08 % → 0.28 %** on highland, because a carved valley really does step at its walls; the ceiling is 0.5 % and the systematic mean gate is untouched, so the defect that test exists for (~100 % lawn) still cannot pass it.

- **New: Teraforge's weather is integrated — the effect registry, camera-parallax cloud and fog layers, and the daylight tint** — the visuals had been reimplemented Wilderfolk-style in `renderer/weather.ts` (Teraforge's ripples, fog bands and snow cover, inside a hard-coded `if`-chain keyed on `state.weather`), so what shipped was Teraforge's *look* without Teraforge's *architecture*, and three of its effects had no equivalent at all. `renderer/weather.ts` is now the ported system: **`registerWeather({ id, label, icon, create })`** with a `weatherRegistry`, and each effect owning **`tint(frame)` / `update(dt, frame)` / `draw(g, frame)`** against a camera-aware `WeatherFrame`. Three consequences worth naming. **(1) Motion is frame-rate independent** — every effect advanced by fixed per-frame increments (`b.x += b.sp * 0.01`, `r.t += 0.03`) before, so weather ran at double speed on a 120 Hz display; `update(dt, …)` fixes that, with `dt` clamped to 100 ms so a backgrounded tab cannot teleport every particle. **(2) The clouds layer is new, and cloud and fog drift with the camera.** Teraforge's soft `multiply` cloud puffs are ported and *composed* into rain, storm, fog and drought rather than registered as a plugin — Wilderfolk has no `Clouds` weather state, and a plugin nothing can select is dead code. Both layers now take a parallax factor against `camX`/`camY` (`CLOUD_PARALLAX`/`FOG_PARALLAX`), which is why `drawWeather` takes the snapshot instead of just `state.weather`. **(3) `daylightTint` is ported** — Teraforge's hour-based daylight table, of which this module applies only the *temperature cast* (drought → summer heat haze, snow → winter cold) between 08:00 and 16:00; the dusk and night terms stay unwired, because Wilderfolk's day/night lighting overlay is disabled on purpose (`renderer/overlay.ts`) and a weather port is not the place to re-enable it. **Three deliberate deviations, each because Wilderfolk's rules outrank Teraforge's code:** randomness comes from `getPresentationRng('weatherFx')`, never `Math.random` (Teraforge's `rnd()` is seeded here — `src/` may not call `Math.random`, and `tests/simRng.test.ts` enforces it), so weather stays deterministic per seed and out of the simulation's streams; **`WEATHER_CONFIGS` stays the one owner of the numbers** (colour, overlay alpha, particle count per state) instead of Teraforge's hard-coded tints; and **`drought`, which Teraforge does not have, is authored in its idiom** (warm dust haze plus the table's brown veil). Two smaller corrections fall out: **fog's declared veil now actually draws** (the table has always said `overlayAlpha: 0.28`, but the old band path returned before it was laid down), and snow's tint brightens with Teraforge's `screen` blend while the veil states keep `source-over`, which is what Wilderfolk's alphas were tuned for. The simulation is untouched — `updateWeather`, the farm multiplier and the dormant hostile-weather switch all behave exactly as before. Gates: `npm run test:types` exit 0, `npm run lint` 0 warnings / 0 errors, `tests/weatherConsequences.test.ts` 13 passed (3 new: one plugin per weather state, every effect updates and draws a frame against a stub 2D context, and the ported daylight terms), `tests/hostileWeather.disabled.test.ts` 3 passed, `npm run test:standard` 239 files / 1465 passed / 2 skipped.

- **Fixed: the ground no longer freezes the frame — a chunk is baked for what the camera can see, at the resolution the screen can show, and a routine build stops re-baking the world** — the per-pixel Whittaker ground had been baked **whole-map, synchronously, on the main thread**: **3.2–3.4 s** on a Medium map and **7.7–8.0 s** on Large, and because the cache key carried `getTerrainRevision()` — which `buildingPlacementActions` bumps when it clears a footprint — **clearing forest to place a building re-baked the entire world**. It is now **chunked by what the camera can see** (`renderer/terrain.ts`: one canvas per visible viewport chunk, margin one chunk, the two map-wide fields built once per map and shared), and two measurements decided the shape of it. **(1) The chunk size is the size of the hitch you feel.** A chunk is baked synchronously the frame it is first needed, so 1024² chunks cost ~0.43 s each *and* bake many times the visible ground — the grid is chunk-aligned and keeps a chunk of margin, so the first frame on Huge measured **16.8 Mpx (11.0 s) for a 0.7 Mpx view**, sixteen times more ground than the camera could show. At **256²** a chunk is ~27 ms and the same frame is 3.1 Mpx (2.0 s). **(2) The bake resolution has to follow the zoom.** Screen px per world px *is* the camera zoom, so `groundWorldStepForZoom` bakes 1 world px per canvas px from 1× up and 2 from the 0.5× overview — at the overview the old path still baked 1:1 and measured **28.3 Mpx / 17.0 s** for one frame on Huge. First frame after both, same window (1600×900) and camera position: **Medium 3.34 → 1.87 s · Large 8.24 → 1.97 s · Huge 10.97 → 2.01 s**, and at the 0.5× overview **Medium 3.24 → 0.90 s · Large 7.87 → 1.91 s · Huge 17.00 → 2.80 s**. Every size is now sharper *and* faster than the whole-map bake it replaced (`Huge` was 4.1 s at half resolution before any of this). **The stale-chunk key is gone:** the bake reads the generated fields and never the sparse `overrides` layer, so the revision counter is out of the key — that is what made a build freeze the frame — and the world dims went in, because the same seed and preset at a different map size is a different map. A latent mis-draw went with it: chunks were drawn at their **canvas** size, which is only equal to the world rect while the bake is 1:1 — wrong the moment it sub-samples, so the cache entry now carries the world rect it covers. Five cases in `tests/groundLook.bands.test.ts` pin the whole arrangement, including that four 256 px chunks at a world step of 2 reproduce the matching crop of a whole-map bake **pixel for pixel**, and that a cleared footprint changes the tile projection without moving a single ground pixel.

- **Fixed: the runtime import cycle, the dead de-speckle branch and a stray 48 MB render** — three recorded findings closed. `placementUtils → terrainGrid` was a **real runtime cycle of two** (`terrainGrid.isTileBuildable` calls `placementUtils.isUnbuildableTerrainType`, while `placementUtils` needs `terrainGrid.tileAt`), and the comment above it claimed the edge was type-only; the "what a terrain type is" pair (`isUnbuildableTerrainType` / `isWaterTerrainType` and their sets) now lives in the leaf module **`terrain/terrainTraits.ts`** — the pattern `OWNERSHIP_OVERVIEW.md` already endorses for `movementSteering` — and the six importers (terrainGrid, terragen, terrainGen, renderer/grid, renderer/weather, placementUtils) read it from there. `npm run audit:deps` now reports **no runtime import cycles** (was *"cycle of 2"*). In `terragen.ts`, the de-speckle pass carried `if (self === B.river) continue;` although `B.river` is never assigned by that function — rivers are the `riverDist` field, not a biome label — so the guard could not fire and is gone; and `buildWaterField`'s output is named `coverage` rather than `dist`, because a 1-to-0 channel coverage is what the tile projection, the biome pass and the bake actually read and nothing takes a distance of it. The 48 MB `map-green-fields-201.png` (a 6144×4608 generator render) moved out of the repository root into `tmp/`, where scratch belongs and where that same artefact already lives.

- **New: the ground finally paints the landscape the classifier describes — mountains are stone, cold crests are snow, water reads as water, and the 16 px lattice no longer shows through** — an audit of the new terrain stack found the generator healthy (every preset reaches rock/snow/hills, deterministic, 0.1–0.2 s) and the **per-pixel ground bake** the thing that looked wrong. **The defect with teeth: the bake coloured land on *absolute* elevation above sea (`e − seaLevel`) while `classifyTile` classifies on the *normalised* land range (`(e − seaLevel) / (1 − seaLevel)`).** On any preset with `seaLevel ≥ 0.12` that puts the whole alpine half of the ramp out of reach — measured on a 2560×1920 scandinavia map the field tops out at **1.077** while the old ramp only turned white at **1.176**, so **0.0 % of the land could ever paint snow** while 1.0 % of its tiles were `Snow`; continental needed `e ≥ 1.143` against a maximum of 1.159, and its 6.3 % `Rocky` + 2.4 % `Mountains` tiles were painted lawn green. The bands are now the classifier's own, exported from `terrainGrid` (`LAND_BANDS` 0.5 / 0.62 / 0.72 / 0.84, `COLD_TEMPERATURE`, `BEACH_BAND_OF_LAND_RANGE`, `MOISTURE_DRY_BELOW_FOREST`) and read by both — one source of truth for where the mountains start, for the walkable tile and for the eye. **Then the four things that make a landscape read, each measured rather than guessed.** *Light:* the old hillshade only darkened, which reads as grime rather than as light — lit faces now warm and shaded faces cool — and the shading reads a **precomputed cell-resolution slope field** (`slopeX`/`slopeY`, one pass per map) rather than differencing raw cells, because a raw difference is constant across a single cell and draws the ground as a mosaic of 16 px squares (caught on a 1:1 crop of the bake); the cast shadow is softer and cooler and the slope rim weaker. *Water:* the shelf and deep arms **did not meet at the shelf edge** (`[12,45,90]` against `[34,83,134]`), which drew a hard dark ring inside every shelf plus a bright cyan rim; there is now one continuous depth ramp, a pale foam line at the waterline, bathymetric contour bands where un-warped cloud noise used to read as dirt, and a river whose core is deeper than its shoulder. *Edges:* the generator's river carve writes whole-cell plates (channel at `seaLevel − 0.01`, banks at `seaLevel + 0.04`), so the waterline landed hard on the 16 px lattice and every carved pool was a staircase — the ground art now reads a **3×3-smoothed height** (classification keeps the raw field, so walkability does not move) and a pixel-scale jitter displaces the coastline and the river-bank threshold on smooth contours. *Material and season:* ground detail moved off the nearest cell's **biome label** — which put every biome boundary on the lattice, and which the old per-pixel static had been hiding — onto the smooth fields, at 3–25 px octaves instead of the old 1.25 px and 0.4 px shimmer, and the season shift is now weighted by vegetation: stone no longer turns brown in summer or grey in winter, and winter settles snow **with height** instead of washing the map flat. Net effect measured on the bake: mean green-minus-red over alpine tiles **0.58 / 1.03 / 0.79** (scandinavia / highland / continental) where the old ramp made them woodland green by ~45. **Cost:** 2.22–2.34 s for the whole-map 2048×1536 bake against a 2.00–2.10 s baseline (**+7…+12 %**, inside the 15 % budget the plan set) — the extra passes are paid for by replacing four bilinear elevation samples with two samples of the precomputed slope field. **Map sizes, measured** (whole-map bake, seed 12345, continental, the shape the shipping renderer still uses): Medium 2560×1920 **3.2–3.4 s** / 18.8 MB at 1:1 · Large 4096×3072 **7.7–8.0 s** / 48 MB at 1:1 · Huge 6144×4608 **4.1 s** / 27 MB at 1:2. **`FULL_BAKE_PIXEL_BUDGET` was 12 M and Large is 12.58 M, so it missed by 4.6 %** — the *larger* map baked at half resolution and rendered visibly softer than Medium at *half* its bake cost, with its coastlines aliasing back into the 16 px steps the jitter exists to remove (the jitter was ~3 px wavelength, sampled under Nyquist at `PX = 2`). Both are addressed: the budget is now 13 M so Large bakes 1:1, and the coast/bank jitter moved to an **~8 px wavelength**, which reads as a meander at 1:1 instead of a torn edge and survives half-resolution sampling for Huge. Huge still bakes 1:2, because 1:1 there is a 113 MB surface. **Known limit, unchanged and recorded:** the renderer still bakes the whole map in one synchronous call (`buildTerrainCache` never passes the `viewRect` the baker supports), so that 7.7–8.0 s is a **frozen main thread** — and the cache key includes `getTerrainRevision()`, so clearing forest for a building re-bakes the world (3.2 s on Medium, 8 s on Large). That is roadmap Theme 1's open item 1, and it is the fix that makes this budget question disappear: a viewport bake is 1:1 by construction, because `PX` is decided by the area actually baked. Gates: `npm run test:types` exit 0, `npm run lint` 0 warnings / 0 errors, `tests/groundLook.bands.test.ts` 3/3 (alpine is stone, a cold crest is snow, the bake is deterministic **and a viewport bake matches the same crop of the whole map** — the invariant a chunked bake will be built on).

- **Fixed: the Teraforge overhaul actually runs — the four-layer tile model was half-migrated, so `generateWorldMap` threw on every call and the valley never generated at all** — the terrain pass had removed `WorldMap.tiles`, the single 10 px `TerrainTile[][]` the whole game read, and left **25 files and 83 type errors** behind it: `terrainGen.generateWorldMap` called its own camp-clearing helpers with a grid that no longer existed (`forEachTileInRadius` dereferenced `undefined`), so **every** `initGame`, every new settlement and every save reload threw `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined`. `npm run build`, `npm run test:types` and 29 test cases were red. **The representation is now finished rather than reverted**, as the roadmap specified: a new owner, `terrain/terrainGrid.ts`, is the one reader of the four-layer grid — `tileAt`/`tileTypeAt` project the 10 px tile from the L2 continuous fields (memoised per map, `undefined` off-map exactly like the old `tiles[ty]?.[tx]`), `setTileOverride`/`patchTile` write the sparse override layer the camp and forest clearing use, `isTileWalkable`/`isTileBuildable` answer from the L0 path grid and the placement owner's own unbuildable set, `rebakeTerrainGrids` re-derives both grids after an edit, and `terrainReader`/`tileGridSnapshot` serve the bulk and test cases. Every consumer was migrated to it — `pathfinding`, `placementUtils`, `terrainSystems`, `worldGen`, `tickLayerSystems`, `buildingPlacementActions`, `audio.workDetect`, `saveLoad`, `terrainAtlas`, `terrainLayer`, the grid/weather/Pixi overlays, `MiniMap` and `MapSetupScreen` — and the 11 test files that hand-built `{ tiles, width, height, seed, preset }` maps now build real ones through a shared `src/test/worldMapFixtures.ts`. **Three real defects fell out of the migration:** the camp clearing wrote to a grid that no longer existed, so the pioneers' clearing was silently dead (it now writes overrides and rebakes the grids, so the cleared ground is walkable *and* buildable); the L0 grid was sized `floor(height / 10)` against tiles of `ceil(height / 10)`, so a 4096×3072 map reserved decor beyond the end of the grid — the reservation was dropped silently by the typed array and a big rock sat on a cell the pathfinder still called open; and `saveLoad.restoreWorldMapFromSave` tested `parsed.worldMap.tiles`, which a `JSON.parse` payload can never have, so the branch was dead and a full save was always regenerated from seed. Gates: `npm run test:types` exit 0, `npm run lint` 0 warnings / 0 errors, `npm run build` exit 0, `npm run test:standard` **266 files / 1459 passed, 2 skipped, 0 failed**, and the 360-day oracle `npm run test:full-year` healthy — **0 invariant violations, 0 stalled ticks, 0 worker fallbacks, 0 serialization spikes** over 25 920 ticks.

- **New: the Teraforge generator is tuned for the landscape it was supposed to make — real mountain ranges, real wet/dry regions, and biomes that follow the climate chart** — the overhaul's engine ran, but the valley it produced was flat, uniformly green and mostly canopy, which is what the owner saw and reported ("the old gen was ugly", then a screenshot with two regions circled for refinement). **Four measured causes, each fixed at its source.** (1) **The height field used only a third of its own range.** Min/max normalisation stretched two outliers across 0–1 and left the bulk of the land in `h ∈ [0.07, 0.32]`, so the biome bands — `rock` at 75 % of the land range, `snow` at 88 % — were **unreachable**: a generated continental map measured **0.1 % rock and 0 % snow**, with no ridgelines to read at all. `spreadHeights` now maps the `[p0.5, p99.5]` quantiles across 0–1 and derives the new sea level from the quantile the old one sat at, so the relief is opened **without moving the coastline or changing the water fraction by a single cell**. (2) **The moisture field was one climate, not a landscape**, and the preset's own climate bias never reached it. A measured map put **67 % of its land above the grass→forest cut and 0 % below the dry cut**, so an arid preset could not produce desert and every preset looked like the same damp field. The bias is now applied, the fBm's narrow spread is amplified against its *measured* range (0.42 → 0.65 at the quartiles, not 0–1, which is what saturated the wet presets on the first attempt), and altitude dries high ground out. (3) **Grassland was a catch-all instead of a biome.** `classifyTile` treated it as the fallback for every unmatched cell, which is why one threshold flipped a map between 40 % grass / 1 % forest and 1 % grass / 47 % forest. It now follows the biome chart the overhaul is modelled on — grassland is the dry-temperate biome, forest above it, taiga where the air is cold, and elevation as the outer gate so the alpine bands are actually reachable — with the two moisture cuts **derived per map from that map's own precipitation distribution**, which is what lets an arid and a wet preset share a c…13034 tokens truncated…P2)** — the roadmap's "path calls" had no counter behind it: `scripts/perf-seeded-year.mts` published the `spatialQueryMetrics` counts under the name `pathCalls` and carried a `source` string telling a reader not to quote the number as pathfinding. **One counter owner, not two:** `src/game/pathfindingMetrics.ts` records `findPath` invocations, nodes expanded, found/failed/node-cap outcomes, the cheap `lineCrossesBlocked` gate, passability-grid rebuilds and waypoint-cache hit/miss — following the established `spatialQueryMetrics` contract (opt-in by env flag, per-tick and session buckets, **zero overhead when disabled**) — and `gameTick` resets and flushes it beside the spatial bucket. `src/game/pathfinding.ts` is its only writer. The harness now publishes a sibling `pathfinder` block while `pathCalls` keeps its historical name with an honest `source`. **Measured over a full 360-day Huge run (seed 12345, `--scenario=producers`, 25 920 ticks):** **11 656 A\* calls (0.53/tick), 854 247 nodes expanded (73.3/call), found 11 656 · failed 0 · node-cap 0**, 108 546 line checks (4.9/tick), **1 grid rebuild** (one passability grid per map, as designed), **71.2 % waypoint-cache hit rate**; tick p50 3.21 / p95 16.76 / max 285.09 ms, 0 stalled ticks, 0 invariant violations over 360 checks, worker 72/72 with 0 fallbacks. The result is worth more than the counter: A\* runs **0.53 times per tick** against **104.5 spatial queries per tick**, so pathfinding is two orders of magnitude rarer than the spatial scans, and the standing assumption that pathfinding is a hot path is not supported by measurement. `tests/pathfindingMetrics.test.ts` (5) pins the contract, including that a disabled harness counts nothing and that a pre-A\* reject is not laundered into a search failure. **And the browser tier's one exception is gone:** `scripts/browser-smoke.mjs`'s `KNOWN_CONSOLE_ERRORS` excused `Failed to load sprite: /sprites/mountains/…` for a defect `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-10-mountain-sprites-missing.md` had already **resolved on 2026-09-13** — the four paths left `preloadAllSprites` and the ridge-peak stamp resolves through the cache-only `stampPropSprite`, so nothing requests the art and the pattern matched nothing (that record's own verification reported `knownConsoleErrors: 0`). The entry contradicted the block's own rule that every exception must name a live bug record, so it is removed and the console gate fails on any error again. Shipping the four directional PNGs remains an **art task, not a defect** — `mountainSpritesReady()` already gates the decor rebuild. No simulation rule, save field, cadence or protocol version changed.

- **New: the logistics overlay shows blocked paths, and the projection can no longer disturb the simulation's path cache (roadmap F4 residual)** — the overlay's row text promised "supply routes, commute pressure, **blocked paths**, and poorly connected buildings", and blocked paths were the one piece never built. **The rule:** a settler's home↔work leg is now `severity: 'blocked'` when its direct line crosses water or mountains, with precedence `blocked → extreme → over_budget`. It is reported **even when the leg is inside the walk budget**, which is the point — such a leg cannot be walked at all, so the settler must detour (`pathfinding.steerWithPath`), and a short-but-blocked commute was previously invisible here because it never exceeded the budget. `totals.blockedCommuteCount` joins the other counters, the renderer gained `BLOCKED_COMMUTE_STYLE` (violet, deliberately outside the red/amber family) and a legend entry, and the severity→colour mapping now lives in one `commuteStrokeStyle` helper instead of a nested ternary. **The hazard this exposed, fixed rather than dodged:** the obvious implementation calls `pathfinding.getPathGrid`, but that grid is a **single module-level cache shared with the simulation**, and `setCurrentPathMap` treats a change of its identity as a new map and calls `pathCache.clear()`. The overlay is recomputed on **every render snapshot** (`renderSnapshot.ts`) and passes its own `buildings` array, so a projection using it would flip that identity back and forth and clear the simulation's A\* cache from the render path — a simulation side-effect produced by a read-only projection, which the architecture forbids ("the renderer decides nothing"). `pathfinding.ts` therefore now has **one builder and two caches**: `buildPathGrid` is the single blocked-terrain/wall rule, `getPathGrid` owns the simulation's grid, and the new `getReadOnlyPathGrid` owns a second cache that touches neither the sim grid nor the waypoint cache and skips `recordGridRebuild`, so the pathfinder metric keeps counting *simulation* builds only. The classification uses the cheap `lineCrossesBlocked` (4–128 samples, scaling with span), **not** A\*, because it runs per snapshot. `tests/logisticsOverlay.projection.test.ts` is now **16** cases: the new ones are a controlled pair (identical buildings, entity and 40 wu distance — only the river column differs, so only terrain can explain the severity change) and a guard that the projection's grid is a different object **and** that asking for it does not swap the simulation's grid identity. No save or simulation impact; the overlay remains opt-in and off by default.

- **New: the long-run harness now measures the colony it gates, and the browser tier measures the build it ships (roadmap P1, P2, P6)** — three roadmap rows closed by measurement rather than assertion, each with the numbers recorded. **P1 (seeded colony health):** `scripts/run-full-year.mts` gained a named scenario switch and both long runs now share one health projection (`scripts/colonyHealth.ts`), so the historical gate and the new one cannot drift. `--scenario=producers --days=100` closes the coverage gap the audit measured — producers are staffed from day 1 and **nothing is restocked after day 0** — while `--scenario=restocked` (the default, `npm run test:full-year`) keeps the old behaviour, and every run header and checkpoint states which is which. Verified over two consecutive seed-12345 runs: checkpoints at days 30/60/90/100, food carried by production instead of being pinned to the cap (650 → 1796 → 1973 → **1437** → 1490, including a **−42 net day**), housing pressure moving comfortable → tight → **critical**, workforce 13 working / 35 idle, lifecycle reported as **8 conceptions ≠ 2 active pregnancies ≠ 9 births ≠ 0 deaths**, 19 marriages with 25 transitions, and **0 invariant violations**. Stalled ticks, serialization spikes and invariant violations are counted separately. Honest limit: every state-derived field is identical across the two runs, but three `deltas` fields (`jsonHash`, `v8Bytes`, `serializeMs`) are not — the health report reproduces, the serialized delta does not, and the 728-byte size difference is recorded rather than explained away. **P2 (one-year performance):** `scripts/perf-seeded-year.mts` runs the Huge map for 360 days in two phases because the repository can only observe them separately. In-process (25 920 ticks): tick **p50 2.57 / p95 8.75 / p99 26.51 / max 255.6 ms**, 0 stalled ticks; delta size per day (JSON p50 1.35 MB, V8 p50 2.57 MB); serialization p99 35.9 ms with **3 spikes** past 50 ms; memory trend heap 64.4 → 201.2 MB (peak RSS 475.9 MB); **console volume 0 calls**; 0 invariant violations over 360 checks. Worker transport: 72/72 ticks, **0 fallbacks**, 0 errors, pipeline depth 4, result gap p95 28.0 ms. "Path calls" has no per-call pathfinder counter in this repo, so the record publishes the `spatialQueryMetrics` counts (2 709 793 queries, 104.5/tick) labelled with their source rather than inventing one. **P6 (normal-zoom presentation):** the browser tier gained a five-configuration viewport/DPI matrix (`Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride` + `Page.captureScreenshot` per configuration, console-error gate re-applied per configuration) and a **41-item acceptance checklist** across the row's five areas plus the settler walk-through and the UI-audit rows, with human-judgement items marked `needs-human-review` and never auto-passed. Verified here: `npm run test:accept` **exit 0**, `verdict: pass`, `failures: []`, checklist 22 pass / **0 fail** / 12 human / 7 not-observed, void ratios 0.005–0.014 against a 0.25 limit, 0 console errors / 0 page exceptions / 0 4xx, and the four audit rows NOT REPRODUCED. The DPR check now compares the effective scale factor against the DPR with a 0.02 tolerance; the previous `cssSize × dpr` comparison produced a 1-px false failure at 150 % scaling. Residuals recorded rather than hidden: the building/road/wall/watchtower and child-sprite items and the walk-through's commute/work beat need a built-out settlement (the harness follows intro → settle → play and does not build for the player), and `scripts/perf-seeded-year.mts` is invoked by its documented path rather than an npm script. Gate on this tree: `test:all` exit 0, `test:accept` exit 0, lint 0 warnings / 0 errors.

- **Developer: the decision registry can no longer point at a test file that does not exist** — `src/game/simulation/decisionRegistry.ts` is the machine-checkable ownership contract, and its `testFile` column told a reader where each decision's coverage lives. One row pointed at `tests/phase678.regression.test.ts`, **which has never existed in this tree**, and four more still promised a test "to be added in Objective N" long after those objectives shipped (`construction`, `socialFeedback`, `conception`, `commands`); a reader following a 404 learns nothing about coverage, and a stale promise reads as missing work. The five rows now name the files that actually cover them (`humanLifecycle`/`conceptionEvent.labelling`, `demolish.roundtrip`, `humanChat.ambientPairing`, `youthConception.ageFloor`, `workerCommand.roundtrip`), and `tests/simulation.decisionRegistry.test.ts` gained a case that makes the column self-checking: every `tests/*.test.ts` path the registry names must exist on disk, the count must stay above a floor so a broken regex cannot pass it vacuously, and the registry may not carry "to be added" text again. Both assertions were proved non-vacuous by restoring each defect in turn (**`registry points at a missing file: tests/phase678.regression.test.ts: expected false to be true`** and **`a stale "to be added" note is back`**), then restoring the file byte-identically. Docs and test only — no runtime, save or protocol change.

- **Fixed: the four browser-measured UI-audit rows, and a pause that froze the sim without telling the player (roadmap 01 closed; P6 browser pass)** — the P6 browser tier supplied the evidence first, and it changed two of the four decisions. **R13, the one that mattered:** at 900×700 the header's content measured **1009 px in a 900 px bar**, `.game-shell` clips both axes with no scrollbar, and the ☰ Menu control sat at x 942–1009 — its centre (975, 27) outside the viewport with `elementFromPoint` unable to resolve it. The save/settings/new-game control was simply unreachable at a narrow window. The header now wraps its control groups (`flex-wrap` + `gap-y-1`) instead of clipping them; post-fix the bar measures 900/900 with 0 px overflow and the Menu hit-test lands on the control. Same fix closes the second browser finding: the bar also overflowed at 1024×640. **R40:** collapsing the inspector hid the panel body while the column kept its 18.5rem, so the map canvas measured **1304 px expanded and 1304 px collapsed** — the player who collapsed it to see more map gained exactly nothing. The collapsed state is now a compact control strip (expand + clear) in a 48 px column, and the canvas measures **1552 px**, a real **+248 px**. The diagnostics drawer is not rendered while collapsed, because a 3rem column cannot lay it out — expanding brings it straight back from its own remembered state. **R20:** the zoom select showed the snapped preset ("145%") directly above the live zoom ("160%") with no visible label, so after one `+` the player saw two bare numbers; they now read **Set** and **Live**, and the select's title no longer conflates the two. **R38:** the work window — the rule that decides whether production happens at all — was tooltip-only; the clock chip now carries a `🛠 06:00–16:00` badge whenever the window is non-default (measured: absent before, `06:00–16:00` after), hidden on weekends. All four now report **NOT REPRODUCED** in a re-run of the real build. **And a defect the browser pass found that no reading of the source would have:** with the simulation frozen, the pause control still read "Pause simulation" and the "⏸ Paused — Space to resume" banner never appeared. Root cause: `GameLoop.mutateWorld` ended in `notify(true)`, and `notify` **dedupes on the tick** (`tickChanged && tick === lastNotifiedTick → return`) — a pause never advances the tick, so the one notification that could repaint the header was swallowed and React kept rendering the pre-pause world; the world object is mutated in place, so nothing else would ever have repainted it. `mutateWorld` now forces its notification, the same shape the loop already uses for its other out-of-tick state changes, and `tests/gameLoop.speedControl.test.ts` gained the regression — red-before `pausing must notify subscribers: expected 0 to be greater than 0`, source restored byte-identically (sha256 compared) before the green run. Gate: `test:all` exit 0 — 239 files / 1 270 passed / 3 skipped, lint 0 warnings / 0 errors. **One item stays open and is not mine to close:** the browser harness itself still exits 1 on its own 1.5× matrix precondition (`expectedBackingStore 1476×1019` vs observed `1476×1018` — a 1-px DPR rounding mismatch), which is present in the harness's own earlier run too; it is reported to the P6 workstream rather than papered over.

- **Fixed: the C2 duplication register — one name, one idle rule, one reputation band, one headroom function (roadmap 01, audit §C2)** — the UI audit's semantic-duplicate list ("the same rule computed in a view instead of read from its owner") is now closed. **The settler-name fallback had three values on one screen:** `SelectedEntityPanel` read `entity.name || 'Unnamed'` for its title and `… || 'Settler'` twice for the spouse and pet lines while its own family list already used the `citizenId` owner — and the same `'Settler'` copy sat in six places in `SelectedBuildingPanel`, with `'Unnamed'` in the dashboard table and `'Unknown'` in `PopulationPanel`. All eleven sites now call `citizenGivenName`, so a nameless settler reads "A settler" everywhere. **The "many adults are idle" threshold** was typed in the overview screen as well as in `citizenOverview.ts`, so retuning it would have left the Work card green while the header mood said "Under pressure" on the same screen; the owner now exports `hasManyAdultsIdle` and both read it. **The reputation bands** that price visitor trade were hand-written in three places; `VISITOR_TRADE_FRIENDLY_REP` / `VISITOR_TRADE_HARSH_REP` and `getVisitorTradeReputationBand` now own them, `getVisitorTradePriceMult` is derived from the band, and the visitor banner and the Village tooltip name the bands instead of restating the numbers. **Storage headroom** in the visitor panel was a local `max(0, cap - current)` that dropped the owner's non-finite-cap branch; it now calls `getAvailableStorageHeadroom`. Verified already fixed in the same register, with the owner in place: building output hints (the drifting "+8 / max +72", "+15", "+25 % per worker", "+600", "+800" prose is gone, built from `getWallSegmentCap`/`SMITH_BONUS_PER_WORKER`/… at render time), skill→output %, resource fill %, the research gate, the ecosystem-health read, the dynasty rule, the food-low threshold, the refugee/rival/recruit gates, the focusable-element selector and the tab mapping. `tests/uiSingleOwner.test.ts` grew from 23 to 27 cases and its name-fallback guard was strengthened: the old one checked two named expressions, which is precisely how three sites in one file kept drifting — the new one asserts that no view file re-types a nameless-settler fallback at all. Red-before proven against a full mirrored pre-fix `src/` tree (**4 failed, 23 passed** — the four new guards, nothing else), with the mirror deleted afterwards. **One regression I introduced and fixed in the same pass, disclosed:** my first version had the visitor panel call `getVisitorTradePriceMult`, which the F18 guard in `tests/visitorTradePanel.eligibility.test.ts` correctly forbids — the panel must render, not price (`expected … not to match /getVisitorTradePriceMult|getVisitorTradeRewardMult/`). The owner now exposes the *band*, the panel asks for that, and both guard files pass together. This time the gate caught it: `test:all` failed 1/1272 before the correction and is green after.

- **Fixed: four more UI-audit rows — the menu now takes focus, the intro stops eating Tab, the mood stops repeating itself, and a refused load on the new-settlement screen finally says why (roadmap 01, rows R11/R36/R37/R39)** — a second sweep of `docs/private/audits/2026-09-16/UI_REFINEMENT_AND_DUPLICATION_2026-09-17.md` re-verified the rows the campaign had not yet reached, and **four more turned out to be already fixed** (R8, whose challenge-progress default branch now covers the year/building goals; R10, the population chart gained a legend from its own `SERIES` plus an accessible name; R18, the pause chip moves to the bottom when a decision card occupies the top centre; R28, the cinematic title card is a focusable `role="button"` with Enter/Space/Escape) — twenty-seven of the forty rows are now closed by earlier work. The four fixed here: **R11** the game menu declared `role="dialog"` and ran a Tab trap, but nothing ever moved focus into the panel, so both wrap branches were unreachable and Tab walked every control of the game behind the click-catching backdrop (a screen reader heard no dialog); focus now enters on open, in the layout effect that already positioned the panel. **R36** the intro screen treated **any** key but Escape as "continue", so Tab could not reach the 🔊/🔇 control beside the CTA and modifier-only chords advanced the intro; Tab and Ctrl/Cmd/Alt are now ignored. **R37** the Valley overview printed the village mood twice on one screen — as a sentence in the header and as the "Village mood" stat card — competing for the same attention; the card keeps the label and the header keeps the explanation. **R39** is the one with teeth: "Load saved game" lives on the new-settlement screen, and that screen never rendered the save banner, so a refused load set a message that was never drawn and expired before the player reached the game — the F19 fix had made the *reason* exist, but this screen could not show it. The banner is now one shared component rendered by both session states, raised above the setup screen's `fixed inset-0 z-50` overlay. `tests/uiRefinement.residuals.test.ts` grew to 9 cases, all four new guards proven non-vacuous against a mirrored pre-fix tree (**8 failed pre-fix**). The four rows still open (R13 header overflow at narrow widths, R20 the preset-vs-live zoom pair, R38 the tooltip-only work window, R40 the collapsed inspector freeing no width) are all visual or measurement questions and are handed to the browser tier for evidence before any fix — none was changed speculatively.
