- **New: blocked story choices explain themselves, normal play stops narrating, the save rule is stated before it bites, and the first load is 29 kB lighter (roadmap U2, U5, T4, T2 + objective O2)** — four open roadmap rows closed in one pass, each with a red-before proof and no save-format, protocol or cadence change. **U2/O2 (explainable, recoverable blocked actions):** the signature story card rendered every choice as an enabled button and never asked the story owner, so an unaffordable answer (the travelling theatre's hospitality food or venue wood, the deer parliament's preserve wood or treaty food, the wedding's gifts, feast and delegation, the invention fair's funding) looked clickable and — because `respondToStoryEvent` re-queues a refused answer — stayed on screen refusing in silence. The card now asks `getStoryChoiceEligibility` per answer, disables what the owner refuses, and prints the owner's own words inline; the raid and diplomacy cards were already doing this, and the story card was the one that never did. The same pass introduces one typed shape for "can the player do this right now?", `src/game/actionOutcome.ts` (`kind` · `reasonCode` · `explanation`), whose contract is that the explanation is the owner's text **verbatim** and a gate that refuses without a reason gets `null` rather than an invented one; reason codes are derived from identifiers the event already carries (`story.wedding_diplomacy.envoy`) and never from the message, so re-wording a refusal cannot change the code. `tests/actionOutcome.test.ts` (16) — one case per refusing answer (10, each with a live free alternative beside it as the negative control), the adapter contract, and source guards proven against a mirrored pre-fix `App.tsx` (4 failed pre-fix). Honest note: my first draft asserted that the wedding `envoy` answer is refused in a fresh colony; the negative control failed and showed `findWeddingEnvoy` picks any living adult, so it is now its own case with a colony of no living settlers — the assertion was wrong, not the code. **U5 (calm diagnostics):** the relationship-diagnostics snapshot was printed to the console once per in-game day in **every** session, because the owner defaults its console switch on and nothing in the app ever turned it off (the test tier silenced it by hand in two files). `App` now sets it from the existing `?debug=1` flag, so normal play is quiet while collection stays on for the drawer and the cumulative history; the stray `🗺️ [WorldGen Test]` dump that ran on every world generation is deleted. `tests/diagnosticsQuietness.test.ts` (3), the wiring guard red-before proven against a mirrored pre-fix `App.tsx`. **T4 (clear save-version experience):** the About box said "Saves may break between updates", which is neither the rule nor an explanation. The save owner now owns the sentence — `describeSaveCompatibility()` names the running build and says a save from another build is refused with its reason instead of half-loading — and the menu renders it, so the pre-emptive text and the refusal message (`describeSaveReadFailure`) cannot drift apart. `tests/saveVersionExperience.test.ts` (3). **T2 (smaller initial game bundle), measured:** `SelectedBuildingPanel` is the largest UI module (~51 kB of source) and renders only once a building is selected, so it moved behind `React.lazy` + `Suspense`: the entry chunk `index-*.js` went **197.90 → 169.10 kB** (gzip 54.81 → 47.16) and the panel became a **28.44 kB** (gzip 8.32) async chunk. `GameMenu`'s static `RoadmapPanel` import was aligned with `MoreTabPanel`'s pre-existing lazy load and measured **neutral** — that module was already in an async chunk, so it is recorded as a consistency fix, not a win. Current sizes for the record: `game-ui` 693.83 kB / gzip 222.33, `game-render` 260.43, `gameWorker` 575.79 (the roadmap's 2026-09-17 figures are stale in both directions). **Roadmap-01 residuals:** four UI-audit rows re-verified live and closed — R30 the chronicle header printed the raw stored filter id mid-sentence ("Showing 500 of 823 death") instead of the label the dropdown shows, R31 the header resource chip announced a bare number to screen readers with its unit and "full, production is lost" warning locked in a `title`, R32 the progress sub-nav conveyed selection by colour alone, R33 the collapsed build rail's grid toggle did too. `tests/uiRefinement.residuals.test.ts` (5, red-before 4/5). The same sweep re-checked R1–R29 and found twenty-three of them **already fixed** by the 2026-09-17 campaign (their row comments and guards are in the code); twelve remain open and are now enumerated in the roadmap's 01 row. **Also fixed:** the repository's single lint warning — `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` on `useGamePersistence.ts`'s unmount cleanup. The rule's remedy (capture `.current` at effect setup) would have **removed** the R1 data-loss guard, because that effect runs on mount when no loop exists yet, so a captured value would be `null` on every real teardown; the cleanup keeps reading the live ref and carries a scoped suppression with that reason. Gate: `test:all` exit 0 — 239 files / 1 261 passed / 3 skipped, **lint 0 warnings / 0 errors**.

- **Developer: the durable-state contract matrix (roadmap O3) now exists, and a seeded worker-vs-main-thread parity gate backs it** — O3's question is *"does a locally correct owner still hold its state when it crosses the worker boundary, the tick delta, the rollback payload and a save/load round trip?"*, because a field can be written perfectly by its owner and still be lost at any of those four seams. `docs/SAVE_DELTA_CONTRACT_MATRIX.md` answers it for **20 durable field families** — calendar/identity, world aggregates, economy, stories/cards/campaign, elections, animal care and migration, residence, workforce and schedule, relationships/family, pregnancy and Moon Howler, defense, venue schedules, requests/visitors/quests, ecology and valley stage, research, village forge, trade routes and build totals, festivals, world events/weather/challenges, and chronicle/stats/notify markers — each with its mutation owner and cadence, delta extract→apply, prep rollback, save key, load normalization, UI reader, invalid-input behaviour, and the test that pins it; plus the deliberately non-durable state and why (host-authored UI fields, runtime caches, presentation events, the compact map descriptor, the RNG snapshot) and a six-step checklist for adding a durable field. Building it surfaced **six residuals, recorded rather than repaired** because each is owner-decided or outside the slice: `disasters` rides the prep payload and the delta but is absent from the save allow-list (dormant while `HOSTILE_WEATHER_ENABLED = false`, and it must join the allow-list first if weather is ever re-enabled); `villageCanHeat` is likewise delta+prep-only and its readers default a missing value to `true`, so a mid-day load reads as able-to-heat until the next daily tick; `ENTITY_PERSISTED_FIELDS` is a **re-apply** list rather than the persistence boundary (saved entities are restored whole), which was undocumented; terrain is regenerated from a compact descriptor, so footprint clears do not persist (the decided boundary); `decisionRegistry.ts`'s `pregnancyBirth` row names `tests/phase678.regression.test.ts`, which does not exist; and family-reference cleanup is measured in the test tier rather than asserted by `collectSimulationInvariants`. The missing seeded parity check is now `tests/workerMainThread.parity.test.ts`: one 240-tick scenario run twice — on the main thread and through the real `gameWorker` module in a **second module registry** (`vi.resetModules()`), hand-off shaped exactly like `GameWorkerHost` (`world.simRng = snapshotSimRng()`) — asserting that both realms hold identical RNG stream positions **and** produce identical save payloads. Red-before: removing `restoreSimRng(nextWorld.simRng)` from `gameWorker.resetWorkerSession` fails it at the stream-position assertion, the worker's snapshot missing the `worldGen`/`entityFactory`/`nameLoader`/`settlerTraits` streams it rewound past; the source was restored byte-identically (sha256 compared). No behaviour, save-format, protocol or UI change. Gate: `test:all` exit 0 — 235 files / 1 234 passed / 3 skipped, lint 0 errors.

- **Developer: dependency-cruiser is gone — the import-cycle gate is now an in-repo scanner that cannot pass while verifying nothing** — the 2026-09-16 audit found `npm run audit:deps*` reporting `✔ no dependency violations found (0 modules, 0 dependencies cruised)` **and exiting 0**: dependency-cruiser 18 refuses the pinned `typescript@7`, so it never parsed a single `.ts` file while printing a green tick. The replacement is `scripts/check-import-cycles.mjs`, and it is deliberately paranoid about exactly that failure: it **asserts its own coverage** (at least 50 modules and one resolved edge, or it fails), treats **runtime** cycles and imports that reach into `tests/` as fatal, and reports the type-only cycle it finds as a non-fatal warning under `--strict`, because `WorldState` holds class-typed grid fields that take `WorldState` back — so `--strict` means one concrete thing: the runtime graph is acyclic. Every `audit:deps*` script now runs it (`audit:deps:cycles:strict` = `--strict`, `audit:deps:graph` = `--json`), the dead `dependency-cruiser` devDependency and its `.dependency-cruiser.cjs` config are now deleted (lockfile refreshed — zero remaining references), the unused `ts-call-graph` devDependency went with them, and `tw-animate-css` is declared in `knip.ts` `ignoreDependencies` with its reason, so `npm run audit:knip` no longer reports it as unused. Measured on this tree: **335 modules / 1 619 runtime dependencies, 0 runtime cycles**, one type-only cycle reported. The guard is proved non-vacuous by pointing it at an empty tree: it prints `Import graph: 0 modules, 0 runtime dependencies` and fails with `Import-cycle gate FAILED: cruised 0 modules / 0 dependencies (minimum 50 modules and 1 dependency). A gate that verifies nothing must not pass.` (exit 1). Also fixed in the same pass: `knip` reported `tailwindcss` and `tw-animate-css` as unused dependencies — a **false positive** (Tailwind v4 is consumed from `src/index.css` via `@import "tailwindcss"`, which knip's JS/TS follower cannot see; it says so in its own configuration hint) that made `npm run audit` red for a reason nobody should act on, and removing either package breaks the build. They are now declared in `knip.ts` `ignoreDependencies` with the reason, so `npm run audit`'s remaining signal is real: 88 unused exports + 12 unused exported types, which is the tracked duplication row C4/C6, not a false positive. The unused `ts-call-graph` devDependency was removed with them.

- **New: Workforce Policy Presets (F3), an Infrastructure & Logistics overlay (F4), Settlement Legacy Goals (F5), and two inspector explanations (P3, P5)** — the first features built from the roadmap's own feature table, each verified before it was called done. **Workforce Policy Presets (F3):** Survival / Growth / Defense / Comfort now steer which job building receives the next idle worker. One owner, `src/game/workforcePolicy.ts`, holds a single data-driven `PRESET_BUILDING_ORDER` table (no per-preset branches), and `survival` is the historical order verbatim, so `DEFAULT_WORKFORCE_POLICY` changes nothing until the player picks. The new durable field `WorldState.workforcePolicy` carries the full contract — save allow-list, `simDelta` extract/apply, `simPrep` key/extract/apply, and a documented default for pre-F3 saves (no version bump) — and changes travel as a typed `setWorkforcePolicy` worker command. **Manual assignment stays authoritative**: the auto pass only adds idle workers, rebalancing donates only from buildings holding two or more, and a building set to manual staffing is never touched, all pinned by `tests/workforcePolicy.presets.test.ts` (10; red-before 2/10 — `preset "growth" must staff its first-choice workplace: expected 'farm' to be 'lumberMill'`). The panel sits beside the work-schedule panel, because the policy is colony-wide rather than per building. **Logistics overlay (F4):** hotkey **X** (also a map toggle with a legend, a shortcuts-sheet entry and a Guide entry) draws a read-only overlay of road connectivity, buildings with poor or no road access, and commute pressure — computed by the pure projection `src/game/logisticsOverlayData.ts` with named thresholds (`GOOD_ROAD_ACCESS_DISTANCE` 40, `MAX_ROAD_ACCESS_DISTANCE` 120, `ROAD_CONNECT_GAP` 26, and the commute budget reused from `WORK_COMMUTE_LEAD_HOURS`/`COMMUTE_SNAP_DISTANCE` rather than restated), drawn per-frame so it is never baked into the tick-keyed entity layer. Presentation only: no simulation writes, no save impact, and the overlay is asserted to touch no canvas property while off. `tests/logisticsOverlay.projection.test.ts` (13; red-before 6/12). **Legacy goals (F5):** the Valley Chronicle now shows four achievements — ❄️ Winter Survivor, 🗳️ A Promise Kept, ⛪ Curse Breaker, 🍞 Out of the Lean Days — and every one is **derived** from data the simulation already records (a closed calendar year, the promise owner's own verdict line, the cure line the Moon Howler writes, a year that closed on an empty larder with food back above the recovery threshold). Deliberately no new save field: every source is already durable, so the projection round-trips for free, and the decision plus the 2 000-entry event-log bound is documented in `src/game/legacyGoals.ts`. `tests/legacyGoals.test.ts` (12), each goal with an adjacent-but-wrong negative control (mid-winter, a 0-of-2 term, a failed rite, a lean-but-not-empty year). **Inspector explanations (P3, P5):** the building inspector explains its staffing — auto or manual, capacity, current workers, and the owner's verbatim reason when a settler is refused — and the settler inspector traces the current route as named stops (home → workplace → venue → home) with the current leg and an explicit blocked/rerouting state. Both are read-only projections over the existing owners (`getWorkerAssignmentRefusal`, `getRouteObstruction`, `isAtActivityTarget`), so the gates and the route test exist once instead of in the view; no state or schema field was added. `tests/inspectorExplanations.test.ts` (7; red-before 7/7). Gate after all five: `npm run test:all` **exit 0**, `npx tsc` clean on both projects, and the campaign tracker `docs/private/audits/2026-09-16/LIVE-FINDINGS-STATUS.md` carries each feature's red-before proof.

- **Fixed: two UI collisions found by the read-only UI audit (2026-09-17)** — **the logistics toggle painted over the minimap:** both are anchored `bottom-4 left-4` in the same positioned parent, and the logistics panel (added by F4 an hour earlier) carried `z-20` and `pointer-events-auto` while the minimap frame has no z-index and renders first, so the panel covered the map thumbnail — and its "click to go" hit area, because it swallowed the pointer events. It now stacks above the minimap. **The Valley overview never claimed the keyboard:** it traps focus (`useModalFocus`) but never called `claimKeyboard`, which the dashboard and gam…12859 tokens truncated…d save data, and a correct value costs nothing) rather than deleted as the audit's alternative suggested. **The Moon Howler's cure-window sweep re-implemented "inside a completed building" (L16):** a bare axis-aligned bounds test, while the owner pads the footprint by 10 px as every other consumer does, so a howler inside that margin counted as outdoors and was never forced away. The sweep now calls the owner helper. A fourth, the `NOTIFY_COOLDOWN_DAYS` comment (L15), was corrected to describe the rule that actually runs — same-stage re-notification is unreachable and a worsening always announces, so the cooldown only ever silences a recovery step; changing *that* would have broken a **tested** contract: `tests/low-5-combat-ecology.test.ts` already asserts that a recovery notice inside the cooldown is suppressed, in both the notification list and the event log, so the behaviour is unchanged and that test — not the audit's reading — is the authority. The same check confirmed the owner's recollection that this whole ladder is parked (`ValleyEcology.ENABLED = false`, 2026-09-08): although the park already guaranteed no notification could fire, nothing pinned that silence, so `tests/low-5-combat-ecology.test.ts` gained a case that feeds the same stress fixture which *does* notify when the ladder runs and asserts the shipped off-state emits zero notifications, zero big news, zero log lines and stays stable. `tests/copyAndDayIndex.owners.test.ts` (2) and `tests/moonHowler.indoorsPad.test.ts` (1) fail with the old code restored (`+8` back in the copy, the literal back in `rivalEvents.ts`, and `expected true to be false` for the howler left indoors), and all five restored sources are sha256-identical.
- **Fixed: two more audit defects where a writer restated a rule instead of calling its owner (L1, L5)** — the same shape as M1 and M5: the rule already had one definition, and the defect was a caller that re-implemented it. **The Invention Fair's "dismantle" refund (L1):** the recover-timber branch was a raw `state.resources.wood += 4`, the last write of its kind, so at a full store it pushed wood past `storageMax` (cap 1000 → 1004) and made the cap non-authoritative for the defects that assume stock is inside it — the `Math.min` sites behind M2 and `canStoreImports`'s over-cap refusal. It now goes through `addCappedResource` like the sibling gains. **Reputation could pass 100 (L5):** the owner `addReputation` clamps `0..100` as its documented contract, but three callers restated it with only the floor — `storyHelpers.bumpVillageReputation` (13 call sites across `deerParliament`, `inventionFair`, `rumourLedger`, `travelingTheatre`, `weddingDiplomacy`), `storyEvents`' private `bumpRep`, and the election-promise judgement in `tickElectionPromises`. All three now call the owner: the wide helper keeps its public name but delegates, `bumpRep` was deleted with its ten call sites repointed (it had no callers outside its own file), and the judgement writes through the owner. Reputation now stops at 100 whether a story reward or a promise evaluation moves it (`98 + 6` was 104; 99 + 2 was 101).
- **Fixed: the workshop's gold estimate now matches what the workshop actually produces (L6)** — the building panel prints `→ ~N gold / 2 days`, and that number was assembled differently from the output itself. Production computes `(1 + workers×0.5) × totalMult × goldMult × globalEff` (`dailyBuildingEconomy.ts`), where `totalMult` carries level · terrain · adjacency · festival · skill · **schedule fatigue** · **work-window scale** and `globalEff` carries global efficiency · **Town-Hall governance efficiency**. The estimate omitted the last three entirely and counted a workshop's workers by `occupants.length`, where production keys its worker stats on the settler's live `homeBuildingId` — so it could advertise output for a shop the game treats as unstaffed, and it ignored a tired crew and a short work day. It now uses the same owner helpers production uses for all of them. `tests/workshopEconomy.estimate.test.ts` (2) drives a tired crew (fatigue 100 → the owner's 0.65 floor) and a 5-hour work day against the standard 9, then an occupied-but-unassigned shop (production says "Needs worker", so the estimate must price it at 0 while still previewing with `{ previewUnstaffed: true }`); both failed with the changes reverted (`expected 15 to be less than 15`, `expected 15 to be +0`). The audit's preferred shape — one exported multiplier assembly shared by production and the estimate — was not taken, because `workshopEconomy` is imported by the UI that renders the estimate and the production module is 846 lines; the two assemblies are now identical in content and the code names the authority.
- **Fixed: spoilage now explains itself, and a Moon Howler save reproduces its own scatter (L8, L13)** — two live defects from the same audit, landed and tested as one group. **The food ledger could not account for spoilage (L8):** `applyFoodSpoilage` removed up to 2 %/day (×1.3 summer, ×0.6 winter) and floated `-N food spoiled`, but the ledger's `consumed` side only ever heard about `meals` and `medicine` — so the "why is my food low?" panel could never reconcile produced − consumed against the real delta. It now records the exact loss as a `spoilage` row (`ECONOMY_SOURCE_LABELS` gained `spoilage: 'Spoilage'`, without which the panel would print the raw key), on days that actually lost food. **The Moon Howler drew from the wrong stream (L13):** `forceMoonHowlerOutside` scattered a transforming settler with `getSimRng('moonHowler')()` and ignored the RNG the tick had been handed, as did the four flavour-line picks; both now use the injected stream (production passes exactly that stream, so live play is unchanged — the point is that the load path can be seeded). The half that actually bit was ordering: `saveLoad` ran the load-time form sync *before* `adoptSimSeedFromWorld`/`restoreSimRng`, so a save written at a full-moon `NIGHT_START` with a cursed settler scattered off the pre-load stream (seed 1) and could not reproduce the positions its own save implied. The snapshot restore now happens before anything on the load path draws. `tests/foodLedger.spoilage.test.ts` (1) and `tests/moonHowler.rngThreading.test.ts` (3) fail 4/4 with the three source changes reverted — including a real `buildSaveData` → `loadGameFromParsed` round-trip loaded from two different realms, which must agree.
- **Changed: "Auto-assign all workers" moved out of the building panel into the Village tab** — owner preference: *"it better i think to auto-assign at a building for that building and main button somewhere to auto assign all"*. The village-wide action was rendered inside `SelectedBuildingPanel`, so it was only reachable while a building happened to be selected — and it sat next to that building's own staffing controls, which is where the per-building actions belong. It now lives in **Village → Population**, under the working/idle/jailed/children row, with the per-building actions (`Fill workers`/`Fill builders`, and the `Auto-fill`/`Manual` mode toggle) left exactly where they were. The prop is threaded `App.tsx` → `CitizenOverviewScreen` → `VillageTabPanel`, and `SelectedBuildingPanel` no longer takes it at all, so there is one button rather than two that could drift. Its label carries **no count** — the staffing owner decides who is assignable, and a locally recomputed idle total contradicted it once already (F7, `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-17-fill-workers-count-disagrees-with-the-owner.md`).
- **Changed: a pregnant settler can be given work (L4)** — owner ruling, *"pregnancy can work"*. The game previously disagreed with itself about this: the automatic pass assigned a pregnant settler and the manual paths refused her, so the same settler was refused by hand on a building ("Auto-staff" on that building) and assigned by "Auto-staff all" in the same breath. The bar lived in **three** places in `buildingStaffingActions.ts` — the idle-pool predicate the roster and preview use, the construction-builder picker, and the assign-button gate for an unfinished site — while all the automatic filters already allowed pregnancy: there is one automatic owner, `workforce.assignMissingWorkers` (12 trigger sites), applying two filters (completed job buildings, and construction crews via `staffConstructionCrews` → `assignBuilderInPlace`). All three manual sites lost the bar. Note that the crew owner `addToConstructionCrew` never checked pregnancy either, so the picker was the only side saying no — which is why the M3 invariant test in `tests/medium-B1-staffing.test.ts` was **updated rather than removed**: it now asserts the picker accepts a pregnant settler *and* that the owner agrees, and re-pins "nobody is announced as a builder when the owner refuses" on a jailed settler. `tests/virtualPlayer.test.ts`'s regression fixture for a non-empty-but-not-empty-enough world used a pregnant settler as its "idle but unassignable" case and now uses a jailed one, preserving the shape. New coverage: `tests/staffing.pregnancy.test.ts` (4) drives every changed site through a public action and failed 4/4 before the change.
- **Fixed: two election-scheduling defects (L3, L11) — a mid-term handover announced as a term election, and a term election that could stall for two years** — both live in `villageLeadership`'s two ceremony starters. **Wrong label (L11):** `tryStartVacancyElectionCeremony` derived the ceremony's reason from the calendar year (`year % ELECTION_INTERVAL_YEARS === 0 ? 'term' : 'succession'`), although it only ever runs because a death or a deposition armed `pendingElectionYear` — so a handover that resolved in Year 2, 4 or 6 was announced and logged as "elected/re-elected village head", and because the reveal stamps `lastElectionYear` only for `'term'`/`'founding'`, it took that year's term slot as well. It now passes `'succession'`. **The stall (L3):** when a *term* election found no eligible candidate, `startElectionCeremony` logged "postponed … no eligible candidates" and returned `false` with nothing scheduled — and its gate only opens on `dayInYear === 0 && year % ELECTION_INTERVAL_YEARS === 0`, so the colony went headless until the next term year, two years later. It now arms the same `VACANCY_ELECTION_DELAY_YEARS` (0.25) schedule the death path uses, so the retry runs a quarter-year on; that is safe because the function has exactly two returns, so `false` can only mean the zero-candidate case. Reachability, stated plainly: L3 needs a term-year day 0 with no eligible leader at all (a colony of children, or every adult jailed), so it is a corner rather than an everyday path; L11 is the reachable one, and it is text plus a consumed term slot. `tests/villageLeadership.electionGaps.test.ts` (3) fails 2/3 with both fixes reverted (`expected 'term' to be 'succession'`, `expected null to be close to 2.25`) while its third case pins that an ordinary term election still reads `'term'` and arms no retry. Source restored byte-identically.
- **Fixed: a settler who no longer held office came back labelled "leader" (M8)** — a leader cursed into Moon Howler form hunts through the night as a werewolf, and `revertToHumanForm` restores the occupation saved at transformation. The office can meanwhile end — a transformed leader is not an eligible candidate (`isEligibleForLeadership` requires a Human-typed entity) while `isActingVillageHead` accepts a cursed werewolf — and the revert then handed the `village_leader` label back to someone with no office, which nothing repaired (`applyLeaderOccupation` only steps down the *current* `villageLeaderId`, and the invariant check covers only the opposite direction). The guard already handled "a different settler holds it now"; it treated `null` — exactly how the office is spelled when **vacant** (`villageLeaderId` is `number | null`, initialised `null`) — the same as an *omitted* option meaning "the caller cannot say". The two are now distinguished, so an explicitly vacant office returns the settler as a settler while an absent option keeps the previous behaviour. The audit flagged this as an owner call because `tests/moonHowler.staleLeaderOccupation.test.ts` documents the opposite; that test pins three cases and none of them is `null` (its "cannot say" case is `undefined`), so the tested contract is untouched and only its prose comment was corrected. A fourth case covers the vacant office and fails 1/4 with the guard reverted while the other three still pass.
- **Fixed: the council report called prisoners homeless and a 3.3-hour gap "yesterday"** — the last two open owner-ruled UI items. **Prisoners:** imprisonment deliberately clears `homeBuildingId` and `residenceBuildingId`, and `deriveConcerns` read that absence as a player-actionable gap, so the dashboard advised building homes or assigning workers for settlers it had itself jailed — while the same screen's settler table and `uiSimSummary` both reported zero. A jailed settler now counts only as imprisoned and is skipped by the homeless/idle tallies. **"vs yesterday":** the Settlers line compared the last two `populationHistory` samples, taken every 10 ticks (~3.3 in-game hours), so the same +1 could appear and vanish several times a day; "Food stored" and "Wildlife" shared that baseline. `deriveCouncil` now picks the most recent sample from an earlier `day` (falling back to `tick <= cur.tick - TICKS_PER_DAY`), so all three lines compare a real day. Presentation only — no simulation, save or cadence change. A third surface needed the same guard: `explainSettler` told a prisoner's detail panel "Build and finish a House so they have somewhere to sleep", so the residence suggestion now carries the guard its workplace suggestion already had. `tests/dashboardData.prisonAndCouncil.test.ts` (4) pins the ruling on all three surfaces and the day-based baseline; all four guards were reverted together to confirm the tests fail without them, and the source was restored byte-identically afterwards. Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-17-prisoners-count-as-idle-and-homeless.md` and `…-vs-yesterday-is-a-ten-tick-sample.md`; both reports move from open to resolved and the register is back to **nothing open**.
- **Changed: a church no longer speeds up courtship; work-shift courting is far slower; a courtship carries a cooldown** — owner rulings. The per-tick advance was `(4 + churchStrength * 2) × … × PER_TICK_RATE_SCALE`, so a full church raised the base 4 → 6 per in-game hour (96 → 144 per day against the 100 needed to marry): a pious village courted *faster*, the opposite sign to every other church effect in the same subsystem (tryst chance 0.07 with vs 0.1 without, floor factor 0.72, caught chance 0.14 vs 0.08). The base is now the flat `COURTSHIP_BASE_RATE_PER_HOUR = 4` via `courtshipRatePerHour(socialTime)`, which takes no church argument. `COURTSHIP_WORK_RATE_FACTOR = 0.15` cuts it to 0.6/hour on shift, and a working settler never leaves the shift to close the distance. `COURTSHIP_COOLDOWN_DAYS = 1` is applied by the courtship system when a courtship dissolves or a marriage ends, counted down daily; `isEligibleToCourt` gained one gate with no tick parameter and no caller changes (a death's `griefUntilTick` is deliberately not reused — it drives the hospital path). New optional field `courtshipCooldownDays`, carried in `simDelta`. Measured on the 360-day gate, seed 12345 (marriages/divorces/caught scandals): cooldown 0 → 65/50/25, 1 day → 69/58/29, 3 days → 54/39/19 — one seed cannot attribute those (behaviour shifts every later draw; the 1-day row out-churning 0 is that drift), so 1 day was chosen **on structure**: the shortest cooldown that forces a clear day between divorce and remarriage, with the least bite out of the dating pool. `tests/courtship.churchRestraint.test.ts` (4); `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-17-church-accelerates-courtship.md`.
- **Fixed: contextual tutorial tips never appeared — and a load could fire the wrong ones** — reported through the 2026-09-16 UI-logic audit (F1). The hook held the live `WorldState` as "the previous world", but the game loop mutates **one** world in place (`gameTick(this.world, focus)` on the main thread; the worker host hands back its single `worldRef`), which `useVirtualPlayer.ts` already documents as "`world` keeps the same identity across ticks". `detectContextualTutorials(prev, curr)` therefore compared an object with itself and every transition check was false by construction — no "Building finished", "first worker assigned", "visitors arrived", "first winter", "research started/complete", "trade route ready/opened", "Moon Howler curse", "food is low", "ecosystem low", "valley strained" or "first child" tip could ever appear; only the day-one `shelter_night` tip survived, and because a load replaced the world object the inverse also happened (loading a winter save mid-summer produced "first winter"). Detection is now a function of the **current state alone**, reading the same "has this happened" derivation the load path already used (`seedTutorialSeenForExistingState`, which now also covers `valley_strained`) — so one definition serves both live play and save loading, and the ~110 duplicated per-tip transition comparisons are gone. A new world and a new game session seed that derivation too, because a fresh colony already has `mining_1` researched and possibly married founders, so the player is never told about history as if it just happened. `tests/contextualTutorial.detection.test.ts` (3) mutates a world in place — exactly as the loop does — and requires the tip; it also pins that after seeding, live detection returns nothing but the day-one nudge, which fails if a tip is ever added without a matching condition in the shared derivation. Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-17-contextual-tips-never-fire.md`.

- **Fixed: the keyboard ignored open overlays, hijacked typing in the search boxes, and could leave the map drifting** — three related defects from the same audit (F3/F4/F5), all in `useKeyboardControls`. **Overlays did not own the keyboard:** the handler listens on `window` in the **capture** phase, so it ran before `GameMenu`'s document handler and `GameDashboard`'s — one Escape closed the menu *and* fell through to the catch-all that clears the map selection — while `showShortcutsRef` gated only the sidebar-tab block, so zoom, `1`–`9`, `b`, `g`, `r`, `h` and Space still acted on the game behind the shortcuts sheet. Overlays now take a shared claim (`src/game/keyboardOwnership.ts`: `claimKeyboard`/`releaseKeyboard`/`isKeyboardClaimed`), released in effect cleanup, and the game handler returns immediately while any claim is held; Escape and `?` stay above a second gate that stops the gameplay keys while the sheet is open. **Tab hotkeys stole typed characters:** the `v/f/n/p/l/m` block sat *above* the `if (inFormControl) return;` guard and even blurred the field it stole from, so the Guide's documented example `forge` stopped at `f` (Frontier), `village` broke at `v`, `militia` at `m`; the guard now comes first and the unreachable `blur()` is deleted, with Ctrl/Cmd+S left above it so saving works from a field. **A held movement key survived focus loss:** `handleKeyUp` skipped its delete whenever the event target was a text field (`isEditableTarget` falls back to `document.activeElement`), alt-tab delivers no keyup at all, and nothing reset the set — so the momentum loop re-added velocity every frame and the map slid by itself; the same gap left the camera drag refs set after an alt-tab mid-drag, because `GameMapStage` binds its mouse handlers on the canvas only. Keyup now deletes unconditionally (deleting an absent key is free), `window.blur` and `document.visibilitychange` clear the held keys and zero the momentum, and `useCanvasInteractions` runs its own exit reset on blur, `pointercancel` and `visibilitychange`. `tests/keyboardGuards.contract.test.ts` (6) guards the ordering and the claim wiring at the source, because this tier has no DOM to dispatch a key event into — that limitation is stated in the test file. Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-17-keyboard-ignores-open-overlays.md`, `…-sidebar-tab-hotkeys-hijack-typing.md` and `…-held-key-survives-focus-loss.md`.

- **Fixed: five more UI defects the same audit confirmed** — none of them changed the simulation; all five are presentation. **An armed "Confirm demolish" survived a building switch (F8, the only one of the audit's findings that could destroy something):** the panel keeps its arm id in component state and compares it against the *current* building, so the arm was invisible — not cleared — while another building was selected, and `App.tsx` rendered the panel with no `key`, so React reused the instance and returning to the armed building showed the destructive confirm with no intervening click; one click destroyed it. The panel is now keyed per building, which also fixes `CollapsibleSection`'s per-building open state (its `storageKey` is read only at mount, so it had behaved as shared state). **"Fill workers (N)" promised settlers the assignment refuses (F7):** the number came from a local restatement of the eligibility rule, while the assignment uses `buildingStaffingActions.isEligibleIdleWorker` — which also excludes pregnant, imprisoned and construction-crew settlers — and the button renders only in the branch where the owner lists *no* assignable worker, so the count was always a promise the owner contradicted; the count is removed rather than re-derived, taking `idleWorkers`, its `App` memo and an import with it, leaving one definition of who may be assigned. **The minimap's viewport rectangle ignored the canvas (F6):** `world.width / zoom * scaleX * 0.5` collapses to `W / (2 * zoom)`, but the renderer's contract is that the visible span is the canvas divided by the zoom (`worldToScreen` centres on `cw / 2`), so the box was the wrong width and never followed a resize; `MiniMap` now measures the map canvas, which `GameMapStage` already held. **The leadership hint printed a raw fractional year (F11):** it interpolated `getYearsUntilElection`, which is `pendingElectionYear - (year + dayInYear / DAYS_PER_YEAR)` while a vacancy is pending, rendering "merit election in 0.08333333333333333 years"; the owner's `formatElectionDelay` is exported (it already rendered the leadership panel's "3 months") and the hint uses it. **Three strings promised elections every 5 years while the term is 2 (F12):** the Guide entry and the leadership tip, plus a third copy in the in-game roadmap that the audit missed and that contradicted a correct line 74 lines above it in the same panel; all three now interpolate `ELECTION_INTERVAL_YEARS`, so the copy cannot drift again. Two further audit findings were verified but **left open**, because each needs a product ruling rather than a code change: whether a prisoner should still count as homeless in the dashboard council (currently counted as both idle and homeless, since imprisonment clears both residence links) and whether "vs yesterday" should be recomputed to a real day or relabelled (it currently compares `populationHistory` samples 10 ticks — about 3.3 in-game hours — apart). Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-17-armed-demolish-survives-building-switch.md`, `…-fill-workers-count-disagrees-with-the-owner.md`, `…-minimap-viewport-ignores-canvas.md`, `…-election-countdown-prints-a-fraction.md`, `…-election-cadence-copy-says-five-years.md`, `…-prisoners-count-as-idle-and-homeless.md` and `…-vs-yesterday-is-a-ten-tick-sample.md`.

- **Changed: the Progress → Goals panel is scannable, and "Buildings" has one source again** — owner feedback: *"the ui should be easy to read not long lists scrolling"* and *"there can only be one truth one owner"*. **The scroll:** `Progress → Goals` stacked `ValleyChroniclePanel` + `DynastyPanel` + `GoalsPortraitPanel` (which itself held the portrait, "Your path (live)", Challenges and Statistics) into one five-block scroll behind a **non-default** sub-tab — which is why the "👶 Humans Born" tile had never been seen in play. Each block is now a `CollapsibleSection` — the component `VillageTabPanel` (9 sections) and `SelectedBuildingPanel` (4) already use, so this is reuse, not a new pattern. Chronicle, Dynasties, Your path, Challenges and Valley statistics all open **collapsed** with a one-line `subtitle` that stays visible when closed (the component's own design), and "How history sees you" opens expanded; "Your path" carries the strongest trait as a badge/subtitle (`Strongest: Nature`). Verified visually in the browser tier, not just by compile: the panel now renders five one-line rows. **The duplicate truth:** the UI showed **three** different numbers under the label "🏗️ Buildings" — `StatisticsPanel.tsx:24` used the raw `state.buildings.length` (counting rival settlements and half-built sites), `StatisticsPanel.tsx:45` showed `lifetimeStats.totalBuildings` under "Lifetime Records", and `VillageTabPanel.tsx:241` re-applied a hand-written filter. The owner already existed: `WorldState.totalBuildingsCompleted` is incremented on completion (`dailyBuildingEconomy.ts:180`), decremented on demolition (`buildingMaintenanceActions.ts:215`) and recomputed from `(b) => b.completed && b.faction !== 'rival'` on load (`saveLoad.ts:488-490`) — so all three sites now read that one field. The "Lifetime Records" tile is **removed** rather than relabelled: the field decrements on demolition, so it is a current count and was simply in the wrong section (a truthful lifetime "built all time" figure does not exist and would need its own counter). No new helper, no new constant, no save change. Gates: `test:types` 0 · `lint` 0/0 · `build` ok · `npm test` 167 files / 905 tests · `audit:deps:cycles:strict` ok · `test:browser` verdict pass.

- **Fixed: the entire ground layer was invisible — the map rendered as bare void** — reported from play as "rivers are constantly dry" with "on the minimap it looks great", and carried into the 2026-09-16 handover as a missing-atlas-art problem. That diagnosis was **wrong**: `public/sprites/tileset_grass.png` ships (192×336 RGBA, 210 of 252 tile slots non-empty) and all ten water-family tiles `ATLAS_TILES` uses are blue-dominant (all-water id 61 = `rgb(73,85,126)` against grass id 3 = `rgb(127,158,69)`), while the fill sprites Pixi samples are shipped and blue too (`water_deep_fill.png` = `rgb(63,127,166)`, loaded at runtime as 128×128). Instrumenting the three `drawGround` outcomes and reading back tile-centre pixels settled it: the canvas2D boot frame painted correct terrain (grassland `rgb(125,158,79)`, river `rgb(57,120,164)`, beach `rgb(193,191,147)`) while **every Pixi frame painted nothing at all** — grassland, forest, river, shallow water and beach all read exactly `rgb(22,40,61)`, the `VOID_WATER` surround `renderer.ts` lays down before the ground pass. The cause was one missing scene-graph edge: `createPixiState()` built the terrain container and added all six graphics children to it but never called `app.stage.addChild(root)`, and `Application.render()` renders the stage — so Pixi, the **first-choice** ground renderer, composited a fully transparent canvas every frame and the void fill underneath became the only visible ground. Water simply made the fault most obvious; every terrain type was affected, and the minimap stayed correct because it needs no art. The canvas2D fallback, which the 2026-09-16 `USE_PAINTED_ATLAS = false` change had been tuning, only ever ran for the single boot frame while Pixi was the live renderer — which is why that change altered nothing the player could see at the time. (That is no longer true of the tree: the ground now ships through the canvas2D path, so `USE_PAINTED_ATLAS` is load-bearing again — see the renderer entry below.) The browser tier **passed with this fault present** (the frame was neither blank nor dark enough to trip `imageLooksBlank`), so `scripts/browser-smoke.mjs` now measures the share of the void-surround colour family inside the map viewport and fails above 25 % — matched as a family, not by equality, because the closing screen vignette darkens the surround (`rgb(17,37,60)` dominates a real void frame while exact `#16283d` is 0.01 % of it); the first equality-based attempt silently missed the fault. Fail-before evidence: **60.7 % / verdict `fail` / exit 1** with only the stage attachment removed, against **0.96 % / verdict `pass`** with it restored. The same pass latched Pixi's failed-init path: a rejected `app.init()` previously nulled both `terrainState` and `initPromise`, so the not-ready guard never held and each frame built a fresh `Application` and re-attempted WebGL init forever with a parameterless `catch` logging nothing; a `pixiUnavailable` latch now attempts once per session, reports once via `console.warn`, and is cleared only by an explicit `resetPixiTerrain()`. Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-17-pixi-terrain-container-never-attached-to-the-stage.md` and `…-pixi-webgl-init-retried-every-frame.md`; the superseded 2026-09-16 report is marked as such with its two false claims corrected.
- **Changed: the ground paints through the canvas2D layer, not Pixi/WebGL** — owner decision, after the stage fix above finally made the Pixi ground visible for review. It had **never rendered in a shipped build** (its container was never attached to `app.stage`), so its look had never been checked by anyone; once fixed it drew a bright vector river ribbon (`drawRiver(0x174a6d…0x47b7dc…0xb6eff4…)`) over pale, washed-out tile water with a visible square-tile checkerboard — two inconsistent water renderings on the same river, which the owner marked as wrong. `drawGround` now takes the canvas2D path (`buildTerrainCache` + `drawProceduralGround`, which bakes terrain into per-viewport chunks from the fill sprites) behind a documented `USE_PIXI_GROUND = false` constant, mirroring the existing `USE_PAINTED_ATLAS` switch so the decision is one reversible line with its reason attached; `pixiTerrain.ts` stays referenced through `resetPixiTerrain` and can be re-enabled once its water matches the tile water. Three measured effects: water and shoreline are now consistent with the minimap the owner treats as the source of truth; the 50 %-zoom surround changes colour rather than disappearing — measured `rgb(17,37,60)` (void blue) under Pixi against `rgb(7,16,11)` (near-black green) under canvas2D, so the `valley-far-zoom` void reading fell 0.5918 → 0.0034 because that colour left the guard's family test, not because the band went away; and the `game-render` chunk dropped from **633.99 kB to 272.56 kB** (gzip 188.28 → 83.47 kB) because the dead branch constant-folds away — `wilderfolk-terrain` no longer appears in `dist`. The browser tier's "this browser exposes no WebGL at all" precondition is **kept as a gate**: the canvas2D ground does not need WebGL, but `pixiTerrain.ts` remains in the tree behind a one-constant switch, so a WebGL-less browser is still not a representative environment for that tier. (An intermediate version of this change downgraded it to a recorded-but-ungated field; that weakened a check to suit the change and was reverted.) Measured in the built output: the render chunk fell 633.99 → 272.56 kB (gzip 188.28 → 83.47 kB) and **my** Pixi source was tree-shaken — `backgroundAlpha`, `wilderfolk-terrain`, `PixiError`, `biome-bases` and `soft-biome-transitions` each appear 0 times in `dist`, because eliminating `createPixiState` leaves `terrainState` provably null, so `resetPixiTerrain` folds to a no-op. **Correction:** `pixi.js` is *not* fully eliminated — library code still ships unused in `game-render` (`pixi.js/`, `ObservablePoint`, `Application`, `TextureSource` all match). An earlier version of this entry claimed Pixi was "gone from the bundle entirely"; that was wrong, and came from grepping only for my own source strings. Runtime confirms the path never executes: a temporary probe inside `ensurePixiReady` was never entered across a full harness run. What is genuinely dead is the `pixi.js` declaration in `package.json`; removing `pixiTerrain.ts` and its reset call would clear that, but would also remove the option to switch back.
- **Fixed: the camera could not be panned to the map's left and right edges** — reported from play as "if it doesnt fit on screen i just should go to left or right". `clampCameraTarget` derives the allowed camera range from a viewport in pixels and falls back to `window.innerWidth`/`innerHeight`, but the map viewport is the **canvas**, not the window: measured live as **1304×813** inside a **1600×900** window, the difference being the left rail and the right inspector (`w-[18.5rem]` = 296 px, fixed). The keyboard/WASD/arrow and momentum pan loop and the click-to-focus nudge both passed **no** viewport, so on the default Medium map (1200×900 world px) at the default 1.45× zoom the allowed camera x range was `[551.7, 648.3]` — 96.6 world px — instead of `[449.7, 750.3]`, 300.6 px: about a third of the map's width, short by exactly the panel chrome. Drag-to-pan already passed the canvas rect, which is why dragging worked while the keyboard did not. Both paths now pass the canvas rect, and `useKeyboardControls` requires a `canvasRef` in its options interface so the omission cannot recur silently. Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-17-camera-clamp-uses-window-instead-of-canvas.md`.
- **Changed: day/night visual differences are gone** — requested by the owner ("i want day/night visuals gone they are really distracting each 48 second a night is annoying"). The atmosphere overlays were already no-ops (`drawNightAtmosphere`, `drawDayAtmosphere` and `drawNightBuildingGlow`), but two real differences remained and both are removed: the placement-grid major line switched from a dark green to a light slate colour at night (`grid.ts`), and lamps lit up after dark with a warm radial glow and amber head (`decorRender.ts`, reached through `buildings.ts`). No night-driven rendering remains wired — `getNightGlowIntensity` has no callers and `drawScreenVignette` is only ever called with `night = false`. The lamp now draws one constant appearance and its building description no longer promises a night glow. Settlers still go indoors and asleep at night: that is simulation behaviour, not a screen tint. Audio's night mode (`setNightMode`) is deliberately untouched, and is the remaining day/night difference if the owner wants that flattened too.

- **New: families and young settlers can now arrive** — daily immigration used to be adults only (18–55 singles, or a couple). An arriving party is now one of three shapes, rolled in this order: a **lone youth** (10 %, aged 12–17 — works, can be courted, cannot marry), a **married couple** (12 %, with a 40 % chance the wife is already expecting), or a **single adult**; a couple may bring **1–2 children** (60 % chance, each child needing a free slot), who take the family surname, are registered with both parents (`fatherId` / `motherId` / `childrenIds`) and enrol at a staffed school like any native child. A child never arrives alone — the simulation's own orphan path adopts or houses a minor without parents. All tuning lives in one new `Immigration` group in `gameConstants.ts` (adult / youth / child age bands, lone-youth chance, couple chance, pregnancy chance, family-child chance, max children) with the age ladder spelled out beside it, and the arrival notice now reads “A young settler arrived” / “A settler family arrived (N)” instead of always “+N Settlers arrived”. `tests/immigration.composition.test.ts` (7) sweeps 150 seeds per slot budget: all three shapes occur, a child a…6209 tokens truncated…ion.gridSync.test.ts` (2) pin all four; the 360-day run on seed 12345 finishes with totals identical to the pre-fix run, so none of the four is a balance change. Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-16-school-day-credited-from-a-tick-counter.md`, `…-shared-home-friendship-keyed-on-the-workplace.md`, `…-feud-against-a-removed-settler-never-pruned.md` and `…-daily-immigrant-bypasses-the-spatial-grid.md`.

- **Balance — an in-game day is 48 real seconds at 1× again** — `BASE_TICKS_PER_SECOND` had been reverted to `3` by the damaged-revision event, so the shipped "slower baseline pacing" was silently absent: at 1× a day ran in 24 real seconds, and every rationale written against 48 s — the two-year election-term decision, the building-progress ramp, the build-bar comment, `villageLeadership.ts`, and `scripts/probe-day-budget.mts`'s printed real-time table — was off by a factor of two. It is back to `1.5` (0.5× ≈ 96 s, 2× ≈ 24 s, 10× ≈ 4.8 s), the constant is exported, and the missing guard is now `tests/gameLoop.pacingContract.test.ts` (3): a silent revert fails a test instead of passing a fully green gate, which is exactly how this regression survived. Scheduler-only — 72 ticks/day, saved `tick` values, the worker protocol and every simulation cadence are unchanged, and no save migrates. Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-16-baseline-pacing-reverted-to-24s-day.md`.

- **Simulation rolls stopped falling back to raw `Math.random`** — ten default parameters (`rng: () => number = Math.random`) still let a rule run on the host's global RNG: four in `simulation/humanRelationships.ts` (schoolyard gossip, schoolyard bonds, youth love, and the **amicable divorce**), four in `moonHowler.ts` (rite outcome, new-moon curse, church cures, the cycle), `rivalProfiles.selectRivalDailyAction`, and `frontierCombat.rollRivalOutgoingRaidResponse`. Every production caller omitted the argument, so a rival's daily decision, a rival's answer to a raid, and a settler's no-scandal divorce drew from the **global** `Math.random` instead of an owner stream — `worldGen` does install a seeded `__global__` stream for a new world, but that override deliberately backs off when something already replaced `Math.random` (a test spy, a host patch), and either way the draws stayed outside the per-domain streams: not owner-scoped, not restored per domain, and native and irreproducible whenever the override was missing. Each default is now its owner stream (`getSimRng('humanRelationships' | 'moonHowler' | 'rivalProfiles' | 'frontierCombat')`, the pattern the Moon Howler production path and `nameLoader` already used), and the injectable parameter is unchanged, so tests still force an outcome by passing `() => 0`. `tests/simRng.seededDefaults.test.ts` (5) pins two of the defaults to their owner stream, shows a replaced global `Math.random` cannot steer them, and keeps `Math.random(` out of every file under `src/` except `simRng.ts`, which owns the seeded global override — `worldGen`'s `nativeRandom()` map-seed draw stays deliberately native. Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-16-simulation-rolls-fall-back-to-raw-math-random.md`; roadmap **T3** no longer has a raw fallback to migrate.

- **Fixed: the caught-in-the-act affair path was unreachable, so no scandal could become a feud** — the only certain exposure route (a spouse walking in on the pair) can only happen at the cheater's own home, and both `isValidAffairTrystSite` and the two `humanTick` encounter guards treated that home as off limits, so the tryst site and the walk-in site were the same place and each rule excluded it. The same predicate counted a spouse who merely *should* be home as a walk-in, while the exposure roll sets `chance = 1` for that flag — so had the site ever been selectable, every home tryst would have been an automatic divorce rather than a witnessed catch. An **empty** marital home is now a valid tryst site (refused only when the spouse is within the same 22 px `AFFAIR_SPOUSE_BLOCK_RADIUS` the other affair gates use), the walk-in requires the spouse to be physically there inside the 55 px arrival window, `isMaritalHomeOccupiedBySpouse` replaces `isAtMaritalHome` in both guards, and `wouldWalkInOnMaritalAffair` drops the "due home" test along with the `hourOfDay` argument of `tryExposeCaughtAffair` / `tryExposeCaughtAffairForPair`. A catch is a catch again: scandal, divorce, and — through `startFeud`, whose only production caller is the caught-cheater divorce — a feud on both sides. `tests/affairCaught.reachability.test.ts` (8) drives the real rule and the real tick, including the control that nothing is caught when the spouse is nowhere near. Same-seed A/B (seed 12345, fix off → on): caught scandals 21 → 21, divorces 38 → 39, affairs 54 → 39, exposures 89 → 91, settlers 73 → 74 — the rule is coherent but the drama is unchanged, because the rendezvous still sends a pair to the **paramour's** residence and so rarely visits the cheater's home; that gap is recorded as an open roadmap item. Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-16-caught-in-the-act-affair-route-unreachable.md`.

- **Fixed: loading a save rebuilt the valley at 1/100 scale, so the colony looked lost** — reported from play as “the savegame failed loading”, with the save attached. The world data was intact (`_version 0.6.4`, 1 202 entities, 290 humans, 102 buildings, tick 13 542); the bug was the map. A save stores the valley compactly and regenerates the terrain on load, and `WorldMap.width/height` are the **tile** grid while `generateWorldMap(width, height, …)` takes **pixels** — the tile counts were passed straight through, so a 1600×1200 px colony came back as a 160×120 px strip (16×12 tiles, `tileSize` reading 100 px instead of 10) with **1197 of its 1202 entities outside the map**. Nothing threw, which is why it presented as a failed load. `restoreWorldMapFromSave` now converts tiles → pixels (`× TERRAIN_TILE_SIZE`), and the attached Large-map save restores at full size with 0 entities off-map and the terrain identical tile for tile. Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-16-compact-save-restores-valley-at-wrong-scale.md`; `tests/saveWorldMap.compact.test.ts` (3) pins preset, default and custom-sized maps, and `tests/workerBoundary.closure.test.ts` now asserts `worldMap` explicitly — the key loop could not see it, because a compact map is not one of `WORLD_STATE_SAVE_KEYS`.
- **Fixed: a refused save now says why** — every load failure used to collapse into a guessed message (“file may be corrupted” on one path, “Save from a different build” on the other) with the real cause only in the console, which is exactly why the report above had to be diagnosed outside the browser. `parseSaveJson` now returns a `reason` (`empty` / `unreadable` / `malformed` / `version-mismatch`) with a `detail` carrying both versions, `describeSaveReadFailure` turns it into one precise sentence, and both Load paths (browser slot and file) show that instead of a guess. `tests/saveRead.failureReason.test.ts` (4) covers the four causes. `scripts/inspect-save.mts` was added alongside it to run a sent-in save through the real load path and report why it fails.

- **New: a school shows which children are in it** — the player could staff a School (capacity 10) but never see who was enrolled, because attendance is deliberately not stored: every tick each player child walks to the nearest **staffed** school with a free seat and seats are handed out in world order through a per-tick reservation map (`humanTick`’s `schoolReserved`). The building inspector now shows **Pupils n/10** with each child’s name, whether they are **in class** right now (physically inside during class hours) or merely enrolled, their education tier, a ⚠️ when the classroom is full, and a specific empty hint that distinguishes “no teacher assigned” from “no pupils yet”. The list comes from `buildSchoolRosters` / `getSchoolRoster` in `education.ts`, which replays the tick’s own rule through the same `findNearestStaffedSchool` + `SCHOOL_MAX_CHILDREN` accounting — so the panel cannot disagree with where the children actually walk — and `describeSchoolRoster` owns the player-facing strings, following the `describeHotelStatus` / `describeTownHallPerks` convention. `tests/school.roster.test.ts` (6) pins capacity and overflow to a second school, the unstaffed case, that only living player children enrol, the “in class” window, and the exact inspector text. No save field, schema or simulation change: attendance stays derived.

- **Settlers become markers when you zoom out — the village-wide view stops being a crowd** — above `HUMAN_MIN_SCREEN_PX` (30 px) the sprite path stopped shrinking, so at village zoom every settler claimed 30 px of screen plus a contact shadow, a walk frame, a status badge, a speech bubble and often a name plate; at 200+ citizens that is the “crazy mess”. Below the new `HUMAN_SPRITE_MIN_ZOOM` (1.15) each settler is now one small dot drawn in their own class colour — visitors cyan, rivals orange, the leader gold, larger and crowned — and the dot path skips the sprite lookup, walk frame, shadow, bob, badge, bubble and label, so the frame cost falls with the noise. The threshold sits between the zoom presets on purpose: 0.5 / 0.75 / 1.0 show dots, 1.25× and closer show people. A **selected** settler always keeps the full sprite (and their ring), so the inspector keeps pointing at somebody, and the entity-layer cache already invalidates on any zoom change, so crossing the threshold rebuilds the layer. Awake night-goers are unaffected, and this composes with the sleep rule above: after dark the map holds only the people who are actually out. `tests/humanSprites.zoomMarker.test.ts` (4) pins the switch at every preset, the selection exemption, the marker size bounds and the per-class colours, and the browser acceptance harness gained a **far-zoom pass** (`scripts/browser-smoke.mjs` sets the 50% preset, captures `valley-far-zoom.png`, then restores `CAMERA_ZOOM_DEFAULT`) so the marker path is exercised in the real bundle — it reported the frame painted (3 960 distinct colours, not blank) with 0 console errors. Frame-rate gain is structural (fewer canvas operations per citizen), not yet measured on a 200-citizen colony.

- **Fixed: settlers talked — and floated a speech bubble — while asleep at home** — there is no `sleeping` flag in the simulation; “asleep” is just the night routine leaving a settler near their house, and the ambient-chatter roll in `humanTick` never checked it while the dialogue picker actively pushed `'home'`/`'sleep'` lines at night (`humanChat.ts`), so a sleeper kept a bubble for the whole 2.5 h+ line. One new predicate in the schedule owner — `isAsleepAtHome(human, buildings, hour)` = the night window (`isNightHour`, 20:00–06:00) plus the residency owner’s 55 px proximity rule (`isNearResidence`) — now gates the ambient roll and ends any running ambient chat (`endAmbientHumanChat`; a scripted dialogue session is exempt, so a story beat is never cut mid-line). Because a sleeper is indoors, the renderer also stops drawing them — body, shadow and bubble — for the night, unless the player has them selected: at 200+ citizens the nightly crowd leaves the map instead of milling about, which is the first slice of the large-village readability work. Awake night-goers (tavern, night shift, visitors, anyone still walking home) are untouched. `tests/humanChat.sleepAtHome.test.ts` (6) pins it, including the control that the same settler does speak away from home on the same tick. Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-16-settlers-talk-and-bubble-while-asleep-at-home.md`.

- **Male settler class ladder — ten characters from the delivered V2 spritesheet** — `public/sprites/new_male_set/V2/Spritesheet_V2_10_males.png` (1536×1024, two rows of five, poorest to richest) is cut into ten tightly cropped RGBA sprites in `public/sprites/new_male_set/`, named for the ladder: **Poor Labourer · Farmhand · Craftsman · Pioneer · Shopkeeper · Clerk · Merchant · Prosperous Farmer · Wealthy Gentry · Aristocrat**. Transparency was verified rather than assumed: the sheet is 56.1% fully transparent with **zero** fully-opaque pixels and transparent borders, and the ten cuts measure exactly like the shipped female art (max alpha 254, mean body alpha ≈246, all four corners alpha 0), so no background removal was needed — `scripts/cut-sprite-sheet.mjs` reproduces the cut, reports per-sprite alpha, and applies the same 6 px padding the female cuts use. The wiring now mirrors the female ladder one-for-one: `PREVIEW_WALK_SHEET_PATHS.male`, `HUMAN_VARIANT_LABELS.male`, the new `HUMAN_MALE_CLASS_COUNT = 10`, and the loader's human-sprite set; male variants are a **weighted social draw** like the female ones, over the single shared `Social.CLASS_LADDER_WEIGHTS` (renamed from `FEMALE_CLASS_WEIGHTS` — one definition serves both ladders), so ~47% of villagers are the bottom two classes and ~1.5% the top two and a gentleman's coat reads as an event the way a grand dress does. The five superseded male files are kept under `new_male_set/legacy/` for rollback (no asset deleted) and the sheet stays as the regeneration source. `tests/humanSprites.maleLadder.test.ts` (3) pins ladder length, on-disk asset existence, the weighted shape for both genders, and out-of-range variant wrapping.

- **Verified: the worker boundary is closed, and now pinned** — an end-to-end audit measured both directions of the host ↔ worker transport by reproducing the real path (`GameWorkerHost.queueFullWorldUpload` / `gameWorker.ts` handlers) including the `postMessage` serialization step: a 87-key world census against the save allow-list, the prep snapshot and the tick delta; a mature-world hand-off that confirms the class-typed runtime caches (`mobileGrid`, `grassGrid`, `treeGrid`, `scentGrid`, `humanSocialGrid`, `adjacency`) are stripped and `entityById` is rebuilt; simRng positions resuming in place from a wire snapshot; 2 880 ticks and three commands pushed through deltas and diffed field by field; a failed-tick rollback compared against the exact pre-tick world; and a worker-authored colony saved, reloaded and compared key by key. **No information loss was found** — the 13 measured differences are all inert: `undefined`-valued key presence at three materialization points, the display world's extra `simRng` snapshot (strictly richer), and four documented load-time normalizations (`paused: true` on load, `ensureFullTradeRoutes`, `seedTutorialSeenForExistingState`, `migrateHumanAges` birth-date backfill, with `age` and every `ENTITY_PERSISTED_FIELDS` value intact). `tests/workerBoundary.closure.test.ts` (5) now pins the closure — delta completeness over 240 ticks, mature-world hand-off, command-delta completeness, exact prep rollback, and save/load field survival with the documented normalizations asserted rather than skipped — and `scripts/worker-boundary-audit.mts` reproduces the measurement and fails only on an unclassified difference. Test- and tooling-only: no `src` file changed.

- **Fixed: the working tree’s gates were red — `npm run build` did not compile** — five errors blocked the production build: `frontierCombat.ts` had lost the closing brace of `launchRaidOnRival` (TS1005), `gameLoop.ts` imported `./simWorker/gameWorkerHost` while the file on disk is `GameWorkerHost.ts` (TS1261), `simRng.ts` carried an unused `WorldState` type import (TS6196), and `moonHowler.ts` plus `worldGen.ts` referenced a `pregnancyDueProgress` member that neither `Entity['moonHowlerSaved']` (`gameTypes.ts`) nor `CreateEntityOptions` (`entityFactory.ts`) declares (TS2561/TS2551). The declaring files carry the older revision, so the repair is on the referencing side: the Moon Howler snapshot keeps the omission the simulation audit already records as an unresolved design question (a cursed pregnant settler returns with the `humanLifecycle` fallback due date), and `worldGen.createImmigrantSettler` keeps relying on `entityFactory`’s constructor guarantee instead of passing a value no option declares. Two `as any` casts on `generateWorldMap` in `saveLoad.ts` were removed as well — both calls match the function’s declared overloads — so Oxlint is back to **0 warnings / 0 errors**. `npm run build`, `npm run test:types`, `npm run lint` (321 files) and `npm test` (143 files / 796 tests) all pass, and the 360-day headless gate (`npm run test:full-year`, seed 12345) completes with its invariants satisfied. Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-16-production-build-red-five-compile-errors.md`.
- **Fixed: the diplomacy militia answer named the wrong requirement** — the `border_dispute` → `militia` gate in `getDiplomacyChoiceEligibility` refused with “Need weapons”, while the L33 contract (and the float `respondToDiplomacyEvent` shows the player) pins “Need spears”; `tests/low-2-events-defense.test.ts` was the only failing case. The sibling “show strength” rule keeps its own wording — it is a different answer and no test or documented contract pins it. Recorded in `BUG_REPORTS/2026-09-16-diplomacy-militia-answer-reports-wrong-block-reason.md`.

- **Fixed: boot preloaded a transition overlay the build does not ship** — `preloadAllSprites()` requested `/sprites/terrain/sand_water_overlay.png`, so every boot logged `Asset preload failed — continuing with fallbacks … Failed to load sprite` and `npm run test:browser` failed on that new console error. The sheet is optional by design: `terrainAtlas` gates the bake on `getSprite(SAND_WATER_OVERLAY_PATH) != null`, and `terrainLayerNeedsRebuild` requests exactly one replacement bake when it becomes ready (pinned by `tests/terrainLayer.overlayCache.test.ts`). The art is not in `public/sprites/terrain/` — `ASSET_REGISTER.md` lists only the water fills and `docs/private/terrain-transition-overlay-and-large-cache-2026-08-21.md` authorizes no asset work — so the path is omitted from the preload list with the reason recorded beside it, exactly as the mountain paths already are; restoring it is a one-line change when the sheet ships.
